📚 本期文献进展 / Literature Brief

  • 本期SARS-CoV-2研究集中呈现以下进展。
  • 第 8 天,所有研究组中的 CD4+ T 细胞(Th1 谱)均有所增加,而除 CV0701 最低剂量组外,所有组中的 CD8+ T 细胞均有所增加。没有报告与 疫苗 相关的严重不良事件或心肌炎/心包炎病例。
  • 校正前有五个变量达到名义显着性(两个 CD8 + T 细胞亚群以及 IL-1β、IL-10 和 MIP-1α),但校正后没有一个变量保持显着性。在先天免疫群体中没有观察到任何变化,也没有检测到残留的证据。
  • 该研究在健康样本对比中,识别出65个在严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2两种变异株中显著差异表达的miRNA,并观察到特定miRNA表达与患者症状严重程度之间存在关联。 [10.1007/s11033-026-12632-x]。
  • 相当比例的 TCR 针对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 基因组 的表位,包括非结构蛋白;分析提示炎症小体活性下降和单核细胞分化受损,表明可能存在免疫耗竭或未成熟。 [10.3389/fimmu.2026.1937175]。
  • This reporting window highlights the following research developments.
  • At Day 8, CD4+ T cells (Th1 profile) increased in all study groups, while CD8+ T cells increased in all groups except the lowest CV0701 dose group.
  • COVID-19 thrombi showed a trend toward increased macrophage abundance and diffuse CD68+ staining, reduced platelet content, and a disorganized fibrillar network. [10.1007/s00401-026-03074-7].
  • Five variables reached nominal significance before correction (two CD8 + T‐cell subsets and IL‐1β, IL‐10, and MIP‐1α), but none remained significant after adjustment.
  • The study identified 65 miRNAs that were significantly differentially expressed in both SARS-CoV-2 variants compared to healthy samples, and observed an association between specific miRNA expression and symptom severity in patients.

📘 主报告：研究论文 / Primary Research

Journal: Human Vaccines & Immunotherapeutics  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-08-26  |  DOI: 10.1080/21645515.2026.2715900  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 22, 1, 2715900

Safety and immunogenicity of an mRNA COVID-19 vaccine administered to adults: A phase 2, randomized, active-controlled trial.

Safety and immunogenicity of an mRNA COVID-19 vaccine administered to adults: A phase 2, randomized, active-controlled trial.
作者： Robynne Wong, Anastasia Kuznetsova, Emna Baba, Tapiwa Ganyani, Stefano Berrè, Philippe Boutet, Roshan Ramanathan, Dominic Douglas, Philipp Mann, Martin Robert Gaudinski
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
This study aimed to evaluate the safety and immunogenicity of an mRNA COVID-19 vaccine in adults who had previously completed a primary vaccination series with or without a booster, addressing the need for improved vaccine strategies against emerging variants.
设计与对象
A phase 2, randomized, active-controlled trial was conducted among healthy adults aged ≥18 years who had completed a primary COVID-19 mRNA vaccination series, with or without a booster, at least 3 months prior.
核心方法
参与者以 1:1:1:1:1 的比例随机接受编码祖先 D614G 和 Omicron BA.4-5 刺突蛋白 的研究性二价 mRNA 疫苗、编码 Omicron BA.4-5 刺突蛋白 的研究性单价 mRNA 疫苗 或获得许可的原始武汉/Omicron BA.4-5 二价 mRNA 疫苗。
主要结果
第 8 天,所有研究组中的 CD4+ T 细胞(Th1 谱)均有所增加,而除 CV0701 最低剂量组外,所有组中的 CD8+ T 细胞均有所增加。没有报告与 疫苗 相关的严重不良事件或心肌炎/心包炎病例。
解释与创新
该研究表明,研究中的 疫苗 能够引发强大的体液和细胞免疫,并且具有可接受的安全性,与许可的二价 mRNA 疫苗 相当。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现为使用这些研究性 疫苗 来增强对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体的免疫力提供了谨慎的支持,特别是对于有 疫苗接种 既往病史的成年人。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于健康成年人,不包括长期随访,但由于随机、对照设计和包含多种 疫苗 类型,证据有力。

Safety and immunogenicity of an mRNA COVID-19 vaccine administered to adults: A phase 2, randomized, active-controlled trial.

Authors: Robynne Wong, Anastasia Kuznetsova, Emna Baba, Tapiwa Ganyani, Stefano Berrè, Philippe Boutet, Roshan Ramanathan, Dominic Douglas, Philipp Mann, Martin Robert Gaudinski
Original abstract (English)
We conducted a phase 2, randomized, active-controlled, observer-blind study (NCT05960097) among healthy adults ≥18 y of age who completed a primary COVID-19 mRNA vaccination series, with or without a booster, ≥3 months earlier. Participants were randomized (1:1:1:1:1) to either receive an investigational bivalent mRNA COVID-19 vaccine encoding ancestral D614G and Omicron BA.4-5 spike proteins (CV0701 mRNA vaccine) at one of three dose levels, an investigational monovalent mRNA COVID-19 vaccine encoding the Omicron BA.4-5 spike protein (CV0601 mRNA vaccine), or a licensed Original Wuhan/Omicron BA.4-5 bivalent mRNA COVID-19 vaccine. The primary objectives were to evaluate reactogenicity, safety and immunogenicity post-vaccination. Secondary and tertiary objectives were to further evaluate humoral and cell-mediated immunity post-vaccination. In total, 425 participants were vaccinated and 381 were included in the Day 29 per-protocol immunogenicity analysis. Most solicited events were mild to moderate. No vaccine-related serious adverse events or myocarditis/pericarditis cases were reported. For the CV0701 mRNA vaccine, a dose-dependent increase in Day 29 neutralizing titers against ancestral D614G and Omicron BA.4-5 was observed. Neutralizing titers against ancestral D614G and Omicron BA.4-5 declined by Days 91 and 181, but remained above baseline. Similar immune responses were observed for the CV0601 mRNA vaccine. At Day 8, CD4+ T cells (Th1 profile) increased in all study groups and CD8+ T cells increased in all study groups, except the lowest CV0701 dose group. The CV0701 and CV0601 mRNA vaccines elicited robust humoral and cellular immunity with an acceptable safety profile, comparable to a licensed, bivalent mRNA vaccine. Clinical Trial Registration EU CT number: 2023-504596-25-00 ClinicalTrials.gov: NCT05960097.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to evaluate the safety and immunogenicity of an mRNA COVID-19 vaccine in adults who had previously completed a primary vaccination series with or without a booster, addressing the need for improved vaccine strategies against emerging variants.
Design and population
A phase 2, randomized, active-controlled trial was conducted among healthy adults aged ≥18 years who had completed a primary COVID-19 mRNA vaccination series, with or without a booster, at least 3 months prior.
Core methods
Participants were randomized in a 1:1:1:1:1 ratio to receive either an investigational bivalent mRNA vaccine encoding ancestral D614G and Omicron BA.4-5 spike proteins, an investigational monovalent mRNA vaccine encoding Omicron BA.4-5 spike protein, or a licensed Original Wuhan/Omicron BA.4-5 bivalent mRNA vaccine.
Main results
At Day 8, CD4+ T cells (Th1 profile) increased in all study groups, while CD8+ T cells increased in all groups except the lowest CV0701 dose group. No vaccine-related serious adverse events or myocarditis/pericarditis cases were reported.
Interpretation and novelty
The study demonstrated that the investigational vaccines elicited robust humoral and cellular immunity with an acceptable safety profile, comparable to a licensed bivalent mRNA vaccine.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings provide cautious support for the use of these investigational vaccines in boosting immunity against SARS-CoV-2 variants, particularly in adults with prior vaccination histories.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study was limited to healthy adults and did not include long-term follow-up, but the evidence is strong due to the randomized, controlled design and the inclusion of multiple vaccine types.

Journal: Physiological Reports  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-08-28  |  DOI: 10.14814/phy2.71031  |  Date basis: online_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 14, 17, e71031

成人长新冠(新型冠状病毒感染后期综合征)中有监督运动对免疫学效应的影响:一项随机交叉试验的二次分析

Immunological effects of supervised exercise in long COVID in adults: A secondary analysis of a randomized crossover trial
作者： Nora García-Alonso, Míkel Izquierdo, Hugo Arasanz, Robinson Ramírez‐Vélez, Robinson Ramírez-Vélez
摘要中文翻译摘要 长新冠(新型冠状病毒感染后期综合征)影响约5%–30%的感染幸存者,其特征为持续性疲劳、呼吸困难、运动不耐受及认知障碍。我们评估了成人长新冠患者在有监督运动下的免疫学效应。在EXER-COVID随机2×2交叉试验的预先指定探索性亚研究中,参与者在3–5天清洗期后,先完成为期6周的有监督运动项目(每周两次)或常规护理,随后交叉。通过多重免疫分析法测定血浆细胞因子(IL-1β、IL-6、IL-10、TNF-α、MCP-1/CCL2、MIP-1α/CCL3、MIP-1β/CCL4及IP-10/CXCL10)。免疫表型分析包括20种CD4+和CD8+ T细胞亚群及5种先天免疫细胞群体。治疗效应通过期内变化得分估计,并通过Benjamini–Hochberg程序(假阳性率<5%)进行多重检验校正。校正前,5个变量达到名义显著性(2个CD8+ T细胞亚群及IL-1β、IL-10和MIP-1α),但校正后无一保持显著。未观察到先天免疫细胞群体的变化,亦未检测到携带效应。有监督运动在校正多重比较后与显著免疫学变化无关,支持中等强度运动在PESE阴性成人长新冠患者中的短期免疫学安全性(ClinicalTrials.gov:NCT04797871)。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
长新冠(严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的急性后遗症)影响大约 5%–30% 的幸存者,其特点是持续疲劳、呼吸困难、运动不耐受和认知障碍。我们评估了 长新冠 成人监督运动的免疫学效果。
设计与对象
在 EXER-COVID 随机 2 × 2 交叉试验的这项预先指定的探索性子研究中,参与者在 3-5 天的冲洗后完成了为期 6 周的监督锻炼计划(每周两次)或常规护理,然后再进行常规护理。免疫表型分析包括 20 个 CD4 + 和 CD8 + T 细胞亚群以及 5 个先天免疫群体。
核心方法
通过多重免疫分析测定血浆细胞因子(IL-1β、IL-6、IL-10、TNF-α、MCP-1/CCL2、MIP-1α/CCL3、MIP-1β/CCL4 和 IP-10/CXCL10)。治疗 效应使用期间变化评分进行估计,并通过 Benjamini-Hochberg 程序控制多重测试(错误发现率 < 5%)。
主要结果
校正前有五个变量达到名义显着性(两个 CD8 + T 细胞亚群以及 IL-1β、IL-10 和 MIP-1α),但校正后没有一个变量保持显着性。在先天免疫群体中没有观察到任何变化,也没有检测到残留的证据。
解释与创新
经过多重比较校正后,监督运动与显着的免疫学变化无关,支持 PESE 阴性 长新冠 成人中等强度运动的短期免疫安全性。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,监督运动可能是成人 长新冠 患者的一种安全干预措施,可能为未来针对这种疾病的治疗方法的研究提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究是对随机交叉试验的二次分析,并对结果进行了多重比较调整。由于分析的探索性和潜在的混杂因素,证据的强度适中。

Immunological effects of supervised exercise in long COVID in adults: A secondary analysis of a randomized crossover trial

Authors: Nora García-Alonso, Míkel Izquierdo, Hugo Arasanz, Robinson Ramírez‐Vélez, Robinson Ramírez-Vélez
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Long COVID (post‐acute sequelae of SARS‐CoV‐2 infection) affects approximately 5%–30% of survivors and is characterized by persistent fatigue, dyspnea, exercise intolerance, and cognitive impairment. We evaluated the immunological effects of supervised exercise in adults with long COVID. In this pre‐specified exploratory substudy of the EXER‐COVID randomized 2 × 2 crossover trial, participants completed a 6‐week supervised exercise program (twice weekly) or usual care before crossing over after a 3–5 day washout. Plasma cytokines (IL‐1β, IL‐6, IL‐10, TNF‐α, MCP‐1/CCL2, MIP‐1α/CCL3, MIP‐1β/CCL4, and IP‐10/CXCL10) were measured by multiplex immunoassay. Immunophenotyping included 20 CD4 + and CD8 + T‐cell subsets and five innate immune populations. Treatment effects were estimated using within‐period change scores, with multiple testing controlled by the Benjamini–Hochberg procedure (false discovery rate < 5%). Five variables reached nominal significance before correction (two CD8 + T‐cell subsets and IL‐1β, IL‐10, and MIP‐1α), but none remained significant after adjustment. No changes were observed in innate immune populations, and no evidence of carryover was detected. Supervised exercise was not associated with significant immunological changes after correction for multiple comparisons, supporting the short‐term immunological safety of moderate‐intensity exercise in PESE‐negative adults with long COVID ( ClinicalTrials.gov : NCT04797871).
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Long COVID (post‐acute sequelae of SARS‐CoV‐2 infection) affects approximately 5%–30% of survivors and is characterized by persistent fatigue, dyspnea, exercise intolerance, and cognitive impairment. We evaluated the immunological effects of supervised exercise in adults with long COVID.
Design and population
In this pre‐specified exploratory substudy of the EXER‐COVID randomized 2 × 2 crossover trial, participants completed a 6‐week supervised exercise program (twice weekly) or usual care before crossing over after a 3–5 day washout. Immunophenotyping included 20 CD4 + and CD8 + T‐cell subsets and five innate immune populations.
Core methods
Plasma cytokines (IL‐1β, IL‐6, IL‐10, TNF‐α, MCP‐1/CCL2, MIP‐1α/CCL3, MIP‐1β/CCL4, and IP‐10/CXCL10) were measured by multiplex immunoassay. Treatment effects were estimated using within‐period change scores, with multiple testing controlled by the Benjamini–Hochberg procedure (false discovery rate < 5%).
Main results
Five variables reached nominal significance before correction (two CD8 + T‐cell subsets and IL‐1β, IL‐10, and MIP‐1α), but none remained significant after adjustment. No changes were observed in innate immune populations, and no evidence of carryover was detected.
Interpretation and novelty
Supervised exercise was not associated with significant immunological changes after correction for multiple comparisons, supporting the short‐term immunological safety of moderate‐intensity exercise in PESE‐negative adults with long COVID.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that supervised exercise may be a safe intervention for adults with long COVID, potentially informing future studies on therapeutic approaches for this condition.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is a secondary analysis of a randomized crossover trial, and the results were adjusted for multiple comparisons. The evidence is moderate in strength due to the exploratory nature of the analysis and the potential for confounding factors.

High levels of cell-free hemoglobin and iron in cerebral thrombi of pre-Omicron COVID-19 stroke patients: novel drivers of SARS-CoV-2–induced prothrombotic state

High levels of cell-free hemoglobin and iron in cerebral thrombi of pre-Omicron COVID-19 stroke patients: novel drivers of SARS-CoV-2–induced prothrombotic state
作者： María Payá, Cristian Alcahut-Rodríguez, Rosa A. Barbella-Aponte, Francisco Hernández-Fernández, Juan D. Molina-Nuevo, Gemma Barroso-García, Beatriz Castro-Robles, Susana López-López, Lourdes Arias-Salazar, Óscar Ayo-Martín
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study addressed the incomplete understanding of mechanisms driving the hypercoagulable state and thrombotic events in COVID-19, aiming to identify novel prothrombotic alterations associated with SARS-CoV-2 infection.
设计与对象
An exploratory, integrated analysis was conducted on cerebral thrombi retrieved by mechanical thrombectomy from six stroke patients with COVID-19 and six without COVID-19 during the first pandemic wave.
核心方法
Histological and ultrastructural assessments were combined with quantitative proteomics (LC–MS/MS) and elemental profiling (TXRF) to compare cellular organization, protein composition, and metal content between thrombi from COVID-19 and non-COVID-19 patients.
主要结果
COVID-19 thrombi showed a trend toward increased macrophage abundance and diffuse CD68+ staining, reduced platelet content, and a disorganized fibrillar network. Proteomics identified 48 differentially expressed proteins among 720 shared proteins, with upregulation of hemoglobin subunits, haptoglobin, biliverdin reductase, and redox-regulating proteins, and downregulation of platelet-related proteins. TXRF confirmed increased iron levels, while glycophorin A immunostaining and erythrocyte structural proteins indicated cell-free hemoglobin and iron presence.
解释与创新
The authors interpret these findings as supporting a novel mechanism for COVID-19-associated hypercoagulability involving elevated cell-free hemoglobin and iron, distinct from NETs burden or erythrocyte abundance. They highlight the identification of hemoglobin/iron-related processes as a potential molecular target for future therapies.
科研与公卫意义
The findings suggest that hemoglobin and iron dysregulation may contribute to the prothrombotic state in COVID-19, potentially informing diagnostic or therapeutic strategies targeting these pathways in stroke patients with SARS-CoV-2 infection.
局限与证据强度
由于样本量较小(每组n=6)和探索性设计,研究结果的普遍性和因果推论受到限制。该研究依赖于特定疫情波次中获取的血栓,可能无法代表后续变异株或更广泛的人群,因此需要谨慎解读。

High levels of cell-free hemoglobin and iron in cerebral thrombi of pre-Omicron COVID-19 stroke patients: novel drivers of SARS-CoV-2–induced prothrombotic state

Authors: María Payá, Cristian Alcahut-Rodríguez, Rosa A. Barbella-Aponte, Francisco Hernández-Fernández, Juan D. Molina-Nuevo, Gemma Barroso-García, Beatriz Castro-Robles, Susana López-López, Lourdes Arias-Salazar, Óscar Ayo-Martín
Original abstract (English)
The pathophysiological mechanisms underlying the hypercoagulable state and thrombotic events associated with COVID-19 remain incompletely understood. To investigate prothrombotic alterations during SARS-CoV-2 infection, we performed an exploratory and integrated analysis of cerebral thrombi retrieved, during the first wave of the pandemic, by mechanical thrombectomy from stroke patients with (n=6) and without (n=6) COVID-19. We combined histological and ultrastructural assessment with quantitative proteomics and elemental profiling to identify differences in cellular organization and in protein and metal composition. Immunohistochemical quantification revealed a trend toward increased macrophage abundance, a more diffuse CD68+ staining pattern, and reduced platelet content in COVID-19 thrombi. In contrast, neutrophil extracellular traps (NETs) burden, neutrophil number, and erythrocyte content did not differ significantly between groups. Transmission electron microscopy showed a disorganized ultrastructural fibrillar network in thrombi from COVID-19 stroke patients, consistent with the irregular and less densely packed extracellular matrix observed by Masson's trichrome staining. Furthermore, quantitative proteomics by liquid chromatography–tandem mass spectrometry (LC–MS/MS) identified 48 differentially expressed proteins among 720 shared proteins, with marked upregulation of hemoglobin subunits (α, β, γ, and δ), haptoglobin, biliverdin reductase and redox-regulating proteins, alongside downregulation of platelet-related proteins in COVID-19 thrombi. Total reflection X-ray fluorescence (TXRF) confirmed increased iron levels in COVID-19-associated thrombi. Notably, glycophorin A immunostaining did not indicate increased erythrocyte abundance, and erythrocyte structural proteins were not differentially expressed in proteomic analysis, suggesting that hemoglobin and iron were largely present in a cell-free form within the retrieved thrombi of COVID-19 stroke patients. In addition, acute-phase reactants, classical complement components, and immunoglobulins were detected exclusively in COVID-19 samples, consistent with a distinctive immune-inflammatory and oxidative signature. Overall, these findings support a novel pathophysiological mechanism underlying COVID-19-associated hypercoagulability and thrombosis, involving elevated circulating cell-free hemoglobin and increased iron content. Furthermore, this study highlights the potential contribution of hemoglobin/iron-related processes to the COVID-19 prothrombotic state and may provide molecular targets for future therapeutic strategies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addressed the incomplete understanding of mechanisms driving the hypercoagulable state and thrombotic events in COVID-19, aiming to identify novel prothrombotic alterations associated with SARS-CoV-2 infection.
Design and population
An exploratory, integrated analysis was conducted on cerebral thrombi retrieved by mechanical thrombectomy from six stroke patients with COVID-19 and six without COVID-19 during the first pandemic wave.
Core methods

Main results
COVID-19 thrombi showed a trend toward increased macrophage abundance and diffuse CD68+ staining, reduced platelet content, and a disorganized fibrillar network. Proteomics identified 48 differentially expressed proteins among 720 shared proteins, with upregulation of hemoglobin subunits, haptoglobin, biliverdin reductase, and redox-regulating proteins, and downregulation of platelet-related proteins. TXRF confirmed increased iron levels, while glycophorin A immunostaining and erythrocyte structural proteins indicated cell-free hemoglobin and iron presence.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret these findings as supporting a novel mechanism for COVID-19-associated hypercoagulability involving elevated cell-free hemoglobin and iron, distinct from NETs burden or erythrocyte abundance. They highlight the identification of hemoglobin/iron-related processes as a potential molecular target for future therapies.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that hemoglobin and iron dysregulation may contribute to the prothrombotic state in COVID-19, potentially informing diagnostic or therapeutic strategies targeting these pathways in stroke patients with SARS-CoV-2 infection.
Limitations and evidence strength
The small sample size (n=6 per group) and exploratory design limit generalizability and causal inference. The study relies on thrombi retrieved during a specific pandemic wave, which may not represent later variants or broader populations, warranting cautious interpretation.

通过在单一mRNA免疫原中配对发散受体结合域以增强对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的抗原性广度

Engineering antigenic breadth against SARS-CoV-2 by pairing divergent RBDs within a single mRNA immunogen.
作者： Isabelle Montgomerie, Rebecca Elizabeth McKenzie, Olga R Palmer, Ngarangi C Mason, Joanna Kuang, Theresa E. Pankhurst, Sarah L. Draper, Sventja von Daake, David A Eccles, Thomas W. Bird
摘要中文翻译能够诱导广泛中和抗体(bnAbs)的疫苗是疫情防控的重要目标。然而,在疫苗领域,如何有意识地招募识别不同病毒变异株间保守表位的罕见B细胞克隆仍是一大挑战。已知bnAbs通常通过亲和力成熟过程中的大量体细胞超突变产生,而本研究描述了一种bnAb选择的替代机制。我们设计了一种mRNA 疫苗,其中两个抗原性不同的(严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2)严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株(Omicron和Delta;O-Δ)的受体结合域(RBD)在单一多肽链上进行物理融合。该设计预计可增强能够同时结合两个RBD上保守表位的B细胞抗原受体的亲和力。匹配的非发散串联RBD(Delta-Delta;Δ-Δ)作为对照。在小鼠中,免疫接种诱导了强效抗体应答并提高了交叉反应性B细胞的频率,这些B细胞可识别Delta、Omicron及2002年大流行株SARS-CoV的RBD。通过多色RBD四聚体和单细胞B细胞受体测序,我们证明了广度的产生通过两种不同途径实现。发散的疫苗优先富集克隆上不同的交叉反应性B细胞(不存在于非交叉反应性B细胞库中),其体细胞超突变水平较低,与胚系偏向前体的选择一致。相比之下,匹配的对照疫苗主要在现有克隆系内产生交叉反应性(交叉反应性与非交叉反应性细胞间存在克隆重叠),且突变负担更高,与亲和力成熟驱动的广度获取一致。本研究建立了一种简单的模块化抗原设计原理,即在单一支架上并置适当发散的抗原可促进bnAb倾向B细胞的富集。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
能够诱导广泛中和抗体(bnAbs)的疫苗是疫情防控的重要目标,然而在疫苗领域,如何有意识地招募识别不同病毒变异株间保守表位的罕见B细胞克隆仍是一大挑战。
设计与对象
本研究设计了一种mRNA 疫苗,其中两个抗原性不同的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株(Omicron和Delta)的受体结合域在单一多肽链上进行物理融合,并以匹配的非发散串联RBD(Delta-Delta)作为对照。
核心方法
研究者使用多色RBD四聚体和单细胞B细胞受体测序,评估了发散与匹配对照疫苗诱导的B细胞受体选择和交叉反应性途径。
主要结果
在小鼠中,发散的疫苗免疫接种诱导了强效抗体应答,并提高了识别Delta、Omicron及2002年大流行株SARS-CoV RBD的交叉反应性B细胞频率。
解释与创新
发散的疫苗优先富集克隆上不同的交叉反应性B细胞,其体细胞超突变水平较低,与胚系偏向前体的选择一致;而匹配的对照则主要通过现有克隆系内的亲和力成熟产生交叉反应性,且突变负担更高。
科研与公卫意义
本研究建立了一种模块化抗原设计原理,即在单一支架上并置适当发散的抗原可促进bnAb倾向B细胞的富集,为针对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株提供更广泛的疫苗覆盖策略。
局限与证据强度
现有证据仅限于小鼠体内的临床前数据,未报告人类免疫原性、应答持久性或临床有效性,因此对其转化潜力应谨慎解读。

Engineering antigenic breadth against SARS-CoV-2 by pairing divergent RBDs within a single mRNA immunogen.

Authors: Isabelle Montgomerie, Rebecca Elizabeth McKenzie, Olga R Palmer, Ngarangi C Mason, Joanna Kuang, Theresa E. Pankhurst, Sarah L. Draper, Sventja von Daake, David A Eccles, Thomas W. Bird
Original abstract (English)
Vaccines capable of eliciting broadly neutralizing antibodies (bnAbs) are a major goal for pandemic preparedness. A challenge across vaccine fields is how to deliberately recruit the rare B cell clones that recognize conserved epitopes shared across diverse viral variants. BnAbs have been known to frequently emerge through extensive somatic hypermutation during affinity maturation, here we describe an alternative mechanism for bnAb selection. We designed an mRNA vaccine in which two antigenically distinct (severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2) SARS-CoV-2 variant's (Omicron and Delta; O-Δ) receptor binding domains (RBDs) are physically fused on a single polypeptide. This design is predicted to favor B cell antigen receptors capable of engaging conserved epitopes on both RBDs with enhanced avidity. A matched nondivergent tandem RBD (Delta-Delta; Δ-Δ) served as a control. In mice, immunization elicited potent antibody responses and increased the frequency of cross-reactive B cells, recognizing Delta, Omicron, and the 2002 pandemic strain SARS-CoV RBDs. Using multicolor RBD tetramers and single-cell B cell receptor sequencing, we show that breadth arises via two distinct pathways. The divergent vaccine preferentially enriches clonally distinct cross-reactive B cells (not present within non-cross-reactive B cell pools) with low levels of somatic hypermutation, consistent with selection of germline-biased precursors. In contrast, the matched control vaccine yields cross-reactivity primarily within existing clonal lineages (clonal overlap between cross-reactive and non-cross-reactive cells) and at higher mutational burdens, consistent with affinity-maturation-driven acquisition of breadth. This work establishes a simple, modular antigen-design principle in which juxtaposing appropriately divergent antigens on a single scaffold promotes the enrichment of bnAb-prone B cells.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Vaccines capable of eliciting broadly neutralizing antibodies (bnAbs) are a major goal for pandemic preparedness, yet a challenge across vaccine fields is how to deliberately recruit the rare B cell clones that recognize conserved epitopes shared across diverse viral variants.
Design and population
The study designed an mRNA vaccine in which two antigenically distinct SARS-CoV-2 variant receptor binding domains (Omicron and Delta) are physically fused on a single polypeptide, with a matched nondivergent tandem RBD (Delta-Delta) serving as a control.
Core methods
Using multicolor RBD tetramers and single-cell B cell receptor sequencing, the authors assessed B cell receptor selection and cross-reactivity pathways induced by the divergent and matched control vaccines.
Main results
In mice, immunization with the divergent vaccine elicited potent antibody responses and increased the frequency of cross-reactive B cells recognizing Delta, Omicron, and the 2002 pandemic strain SARS-CoV RBDs.
Interpretation and novelty
The divergent vaccine preferentially enriches clonally distinct cross-reactive B cells with low somatic hypermutation, consistent with selection of germline-biased precursors, whereas the matched control yields cross-reactivity primarily through affinity maturation within existing clonal lineages.
Scientific and public-health significance
This work establishes a modular antigen-design principle in which juxtaposing appropriately divergent antigens on a single scaffold promotes the enrichment of bnAb-prone B cells, offering a strategy for broader vaccine coverage against SARS-CoV-2 variants.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence is limited to preclinical mouse data and does not report human immunogenicity, durability of responses, or clinical efficacy, warranting cautious interpretation of translational potential.

喜马偕尔邦(印度)严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2奥密克戎谱系新兴亚分支的分子流行病学:一项横断面研究

Molecular Epidemiology of Emerging Subvariants of the SARS-CoV-2 Omicron Lineage: A Cross-sectional Study from Himachal Pradesh, India
作者： Priyanka Rao, Sunite A Ganju, Lata R. Chandel
摘要中文翻译引言:严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2(严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2)表现出持续的遗传变异,导致全球范围内出现多种病毒谱系。这些变异株可能影响病毒的传播能力、致病潜力和免疫逃逸,从而改变疾病模式。基因组监测(基因组监测)允许系统追踪病毒变异株和流行变种,因此监测对于制定知情的公共卫生计划和临床准备至关重要。目的:检测印度喜马偕尔邦山区奥密克戎谱系变种的出现情况。材料与方法:本横断面研究于2024年1月至6月在印度喜马偕尔邦曼迪的Shri Lal Bahadur Shastri医学院进行。所有来自该州四个病毒研究与诊断实验室(VRDLs)的Ct值≤30的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2阳性样本均被纳入研究。使用Q-Line分子病毒RNA提取试剂盒进行RNA提取,并在牛津纳米孔MinION平台上进行全基因组测序(Whole 基因组 Sequencing,WGS)。使用Medaka和Nanopolish生成共识序列,用于变异株识别和系统发育分析。所有人口统计学和临床数据,包括年龄、性别、临床表现和血清学状态,均使用Microsoft Excel 2021记录并整理。研究聚焦于生成的基因组序列,数据以数字和百分比形式呈现。结果:共收到30例严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2阳性样本。在严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2阳性病例中,成年女性占比最高,为17例(56.7%),其中大多数患者年龄在19-60岁之间,共17例(56.7%),且大多数样本来自Shimla,共11例(36.7%)。研究发现,19例(63.3%)为JN.1亚谱系,是感染的主要原因。大多数病例(20例,66.7%)报告至门诊部门,其中35%(7例)表现出轻度症状,如发热和咳嗽。尽管有23例(76.7%)接种了疫苗,但仍有10例(33.3%)需要住院治疗,且未报告任何关注变异株(关注变异株)。结论:追踪严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株显示,奥密克戎的JN.1亚谱系在本地流行,且临床表现通常较轻。持续的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2遗传变异强调了将基因组监测(基因组监测)与流行病学数据整合的必要性,以实现新兴变异株的早期检测并加强控制策略。研究问题与背景:严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的持续遗传变异推动了新谱系的出现,这些新谱系可能对传播能力、致病性和免疫逃逸产生潜在影响,因此需要监测流行变异株以支持公共卫生规划。本研究旨在检测印度喜马偕尔邦奥密克戎谱系亚变种的出现,填补了该地区基因组监测(基因组监测)方面的空白。研究设计与人群:本横断面研究于2024年1月至6月在印度喜马偕尔邦曼迪的Shri Lal Bahadur Shastri医学院进行。所有来自该州四个病毒研究与诊断实验室(VRDLs)的Ct值≤30的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2阳性样本均被纳入研究。方法:使用Q-Line分子病毒RNA提取试剂盒进行RNA提取,并在牛津纳米孔MinION平台上进行全基因组测序(Whole 基因组 Sequencing,WGS)。使用Medaka和Nanopolish生成共识序列,用于变异株识别和系统发育分析。主要结果:共收到30例严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2阳性样本,其中成年女性占56.7%(17/30),大多数患者年龄在19-60岁之间(56.7%,17/30)。样本主要来自Shimla(36.7%,11/30),其中63.3%(19/30)的病例被鉴定为JN.1亚谱系。大多数病例(66.7%,20/30)报告至门诊部门,其中35%(7/20)表现出轻度症状,如发热和咳嗽。尽管有76.7%(23/30)的病例接种了疫苗,但仍有33.3%(10/30)需要住院治疗,且未报告任何关注变异株(关注变异株)。解释与创新性:在测序病例中,JN.1亚谱系的主导地位表明奥密克戎亚变种在本地持续传播,且临床表现通常较轻。本研究强调了基因组监测(基因组监测)在区域环境下检测新兴变异株的实用性。科学与公共卫生意义:这些发现支持将基因组监测(基因组监测)与流行病学数据整合,以实现新兴严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株的早期检测,并加强山区地区的本地控制策略。JN.1亚谱系的主导地位突显了需要针对性的公共卫生应对措施。局限性与证据强度:由于样本量较小(n=30)和单中心设计,研究结果的推广性受到限制,且依赖Ct值≤30可能引入选择偏倚。由于横断面研究设计和缺乏对混杂因素的控制,证据强度中等。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的持续遗传变异推动了新发系统的出现,这些系统可能影响传播力、致病性和免疫逃逸,从而需要对流行变异株进行监测以支撑公共卫生规划。本研究旨在检测印度喜马偕尔邦出现的奥密克戎变异株亚谱系,以填补当地 基因组监测 的数据缺口。
设计与对象
本横断面研究于2024年1月至2024年6月在印度喜马偕尔邦曼迪市的Shri Lal Bahadur Shastri医学院开展。纳入了来自该邦四家病毒研究与诊断实验室且Ct值≤30的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2阳性样本。
核心方法
使用 Q-Line Molecular 病毒 RNA 提取试剂盒进行 RNA 提取,并在 Oxford Nanopore MinION 平台上完成了 基因组 的全基因组测序。使用 Medaka 和 Nanopolish 生成共识序列,用于变异识别与系统发育分析。
主要结果
共收到30份严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2阳性样本,其中成年女性占56.7%(17/30),大多数患者年龄在19–60岁(56.7%,17/30)。样本主要来自喜马偕尔邦(36.7%,11/30),且63.3%(19/30)的病例中检测出JN.1亚谱系。大多数病例(66.7%,20/30)在门诊就诊,其中35%(7/20)出现轻微症状如发热和咳嗽。尽管76.7%(23/30)已完成疫苗接种,但仍有33.3%(10/30)需要住院,未报告死亡率病例。
解释与创新
在测序病例中,JN.1亚谱系占据主导地位,表明奥密克戎亚变异株在当地持续传播,且临床表现通常较轻。该研究强调了基因组监测在区域环境中检测新兴变异株的实用性。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现支持将 基因组监测 与流行病学数据相结合,以实现新兴 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变异株的早期检测,并加强丘陵地区的本地控制策略。JN.1 的优势地位凸显了针对性公共卫生应对措施的必要性。
局限与证据强度
样本量较小(n=30)且为单中心设计,限制了结果的普遍性;依赖Ct值≤30可能引入选择偏倚。由于横断面设计及未控制混杂因素,证据强度为中等。

Molecular Epidemiology of Emerging Subvariants of the SARS-CoV-2 Omicron Lineage: A Cross-sectional Study from Himachal Pradesh, India

Authors: Priyanka Rao, Sunite A Ganju, Lata R. Chandel
Original abstract (English)
Introduction: Severe Acute Respiratory Syndrome Coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) shows continuous genetic variation, resulting in the emergence of multiple viral lineages worldwide. These mutations can influence viral transmissibility, pathogenic potential, and immune escape, resulting in changed disease pattern. Genomic surveillance allows systematic tracking of viral evolution and circulating variants; thus, monitoring is essential for informed public health planning and clinical preparedness. Aim: To detect the emergence of variants of Omicron lineage in the hilly state of Himachal Pradesh, India. Materials and Methods: This cross-sectional study was conducted at the Shri Lal Bahadur Shastri Medical College, Mandi, Himachal Pradesh, India from January 2024 to June 2024. All the SARS-CoV-2 positive samples with Ct value ≤30 received across the state from the four Virus Research and Diagnostic Laboratories (VRDLs) of the state were included. Ribonucleic acid (RNA) extraction was performed using the Q-Line Molecular Viral RNA Extraction Kit, and Whole Genome Sequencing (WGS) was carried out on the Oxford Nanopore MinION platform. Consensus sequences were generated using Medaka and Nanopolish for variant identification and phylogenetic analysis. All demographic and clinical data, like age, sex, clinical presentation, and vaccination status, were recorded and tabulated in Microsoft Excel 2021. The study focused on genomic sequences generated, and the data were presented as numbers and percentages. Results: A total of 30 SARS-CoV-2 positive samples were received. Among SARS-CoV-2 positive cases, adult females constituted the highest proportion, 17 (56.7%), with most patients aged 19-60 years, 17 (56.7%), and the majority of samples originating from Shimla, 11 (36.7%). The study identified JN.1 sublineages in 19 (63.3%) as the predominant cause of infection. Most cases, 20 (66.7%) reported to the outpatient department. Mild symptoms such as fever and cough were seen in 7 (35%) cases. Even though 23 (76.7%) of cases were vaccinated with the COVID-19 vaccine, 10 (33.3%) required admissions. However, no mortality was reported. Conclusion: Tracking SARS-CoV-2 variants revealed the predominance of the JN.1 sublineage of Omicron with generally mild symptoms. Continuous genetic evolution of SARS CoV-2 highlights the need for robust, ongoing genomic surveillance integrated with epidemiological data to enable early detection of emerging variants and strengthen control strategies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Continuous genetic variation in SARS-CoV-2 drives the emergence of new lineages with potential impacts on transmissibility, pathogenicity, and immune escape, creating a need to monitor circulating variants for public health planning. The study aimed to detect the emergence of Omicron lineage subvariants in Himachal Pradesh, India, addressing gaps in local genomic surveillance.
Design and population
This cross-sectional study was conducted at Shri Lal Bahadur Shastri Medical College, Mandi, Himachal Pradesh, India, from January 2024 to June 2024. All SARS-CoV-2 positive samples with Ct value ≤30 received from the state’s four Virus Research and Diagnostic Laboratories were included.
Core methods
RNA extraction was performed using the Q-Line Molecular Viral RNA Extraction Kit, and whole genome sequencing was carried out on the Oxford Nanopore MinION platform. Consensus sequences were generated using Medaka and Nanopolish for variant identification and phylogenetic analysis.
Main results
A total of 30 SARS-CoV-2 positive samples were received, with adult females comprising 56.7% (17/30) and most patients aged 19–60 years (56.7%, 17/30). Samples were predominantly from Shimla (36.7%, 11/30), and JN.1 sublineages were identified in 63.3% (19/30) of cases. Most cases (66.7%, 20/30) presented to outpatient departments, with mild symptoms such as fever and cough in 35% (7/20). Although 76.7% (23/30) were vaccinated, 33.3% (10/30) required admission, and no mortality was reported.
Interpretation and novelty
The predominance of the JN.1 sublineage among sequenced cases indicates ongoing local circulation of Omicron subvariants with generally mild clinical presentation. The study highlights the utility of genomic surveillance in detecting emerging variants within a regional setting.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings support the integration of genomic surveillance with epidemiological data to enable early detection of emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants and strengthen local control strategies in hilly regions. The predominance of JN.1 underscores the need for targeted public health responses.
Limitations and evidence strength
The small sample size (n=30) and single-centre design limit generalisability, and reliance on Ct ≤30 may introduce selection bias. The evidence is moderate due to the cross-sectional design and lack of controls for confounding factors.

作者更正:严格选择驱动收敛至类似奥密克戎的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2受体结合基序

Author Correction: Stringent selection drives convergence toward omicron-like SARS-CoV-2 receptor-binding motifs
作者： Aviv Shoshany, Ruo-Jin Tian, Miguel Padilla-Blanco, Adam Hruška, Aditi Konar, Katarína L. Baxová, Eyal Zoler, Martin Mokrejš, Gideon Schreiber, Jiří Zahradník
摘要中文翻译在最初发表的本文版本中,图6a及其关联的源数据因归一化处理过程中的错误而存在重复值。现已在本文的HTML和PDF版本中更新了该图及源数据。为便于比较,原图6现作为补充信息随本文提供。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在探讨严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2受体结合基序因严格选择压力而向类似奥密克戎的结构收敛的现象,强调理解病毒变异体进化动态的必要性。
设计与对象
本研究为作者对原始出版物中错误的更正,具体涉及因归一化处理问题导致的图6a及其关联源数据中的重复值。更正适用于严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2奥密克戎变异株及相关病毒数据。
核心方法
更正工作包括在本文的HTML和PDF版本中更新图表及源数据,以解决归一化错误。原图现作为补充信息提供。
主要结果

The influence of hypoxia in predicting COVID-19 mortality in patients with kidney dysfunction on admission in 2 centers in sub-Saharan Africa.

The influence of hypoxia in predicting COVID-19 mortality in patients with kidney dysfunction on admission in 2 centers in sub-Saharan Africa.
作者： Yannick Mayamba Nlandu, Jean-Robert Rissassi Makulo, Yannick Mompango Engole, Ben Izizag Bepouka, Marie-France Ingole Mboliasa, Aliocha Natuhoyila Nkodila, Augustin Luzayadio Longo, François Musungayi Kajingulu, Hippolyte Nani-Tuma Situakibanza, Vieux Momeme Mokoli
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to investigate the influence of hypoxia on predicting COVID-19 mortality in patients with kidney dysfunction upon admission, highlighting the need to understand how these factors interact in disease progression.
设计与对象
A two-center retrospective cohort study was conducted involving 359 COVID-19 patients aged 18 years or older, with a focus on those presenting with kidney dysfunction and hypoxia.
核心方法
本研究采用多变量 Cox 回归模型评估 死亡率、低氧与肾功能障碍的关联,显著性水平设定为 p < 0.05。
主要结果
总体院内 死亡率 为 27.6%,在 eGFR < 60 mL/min/1.73 m2 且合并低氧患者中升高至 59.7%(p < 0.001)。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,肾功能障碍与低氧的组合可显著增加 死亡率 风险,提示可能存在肾-肺交互模式。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果强调在撒哈拉以南非洲地区将肾功能障碍和低氧纳入患者分层策略以改善风险评估和管理的重要性。
局限与证据强度
本研究受限于回顾性设计及潜在混杂因素,但观察到的统计关联明确,证据强度较高。

The influence of hypoxia in predicting COVID-19 mortality in patients with kidney dysfunction on admission in 2 centers in sub-Saharan Africa.

Authors: Yannick Mayamba Nlandu, Jean-Robert Rissassi Makulo, Yannick Mompango Engole, Ben Izizag Bepouka, Marie-France Ingole Mboliasa, Aliocha Natuhoyila Nkodila, Augustin Luzayadio Longo, François Musungayi Kajingulu, Hippolyte Nani-Tuma Situakibanza, Vieux Momeme Mokoli
Original abstract (English)
Kidney dysfunction (KD) and hypoxia on admission are separately associated with COVID-19 mortality. Estimation of GFR using creatinine and peripheral oxygen saturation (SpO2) may be useful for stratification of at-risk populations. We performed a two centers retrospective cohort study of 359 COVID-19 patients aged 18 years or older to explore the impact of admission kidney function and hypoxia in predicting COVID-19 mortality. KD at admission was defined as estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) calculated by CKD EPI < 60 mL /min/1.73 m2 using creatinine and hypoxia as a SpO2 ˂ 90%. Survival (time-to death) curves were built using the Kaplan-Meier methods. Association between mortality, hypoxia and KD was evaluated by multivariate Cox regression models with a significance level of the p-value set at 0.05. There were 152 patients (42.3%) with an eGFR < 60 mL/min/1.73 m2 and 208 patients (74.8%) with hypoxia on admission. Overall in-hospital mortality was 27.6%, rising to 59.7% in patients with eGFR below 60 mL/min and hypoxia (p < 0.001). Survival was lower in the group of patients with a eGFR below 60 mL/min/1.73 m2 and hypoxia (p < 0.001). Predictors of mortality were eGFR ≥ 60 mL/min/1.73 m2 with hypoxia (HR 2.05 [1.01-4.16], p = 0.048), eGFR < 60 mL/min/1.73 m2 without hypoxia (HR 3.20 [1.73-5.94], p < 0.001) and eGFR < 60 mL/min/1.73 m2 with hypoxia (HR 6.55 [3.63-11.84], p < 0.001). KD on admission and hypoxia are common in COVID-19. Its association with in-hospital mortality should encourage its use in the stratification of patients at risk of death. Influence of hypoxia could suggest a pattern of kidney-lung cross talk.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate the influence of hypoxia on predicting COVID-19 mortality in patients with kidney dysfunction upon admission, highlighting the need to understand how these factors interact in disease progression.
Design and population
A two-center retrospective cohort study was conducted involving 359 COVID-19 patients aged 18 years or older, with a focus on those presenting with kidney dysfunction and hypoxia.
Core methods
The study used multivariate Cox regression models to evaluate the association between mortality, hypoxia, and kidney dysfunction, with a significance level set at p < 0.05.
Main results
Overall in-hospital mortality was 27.6%, increasing to 59.7% in patients with both eGFR < 60 mL/min/1.73 m2 and hypoxia (p < 0.001).
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that the combination of kidney dysfunction and hypoxia significantly increases mortality risk, indicating a potential pattern of kidney-lung cross talk.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results emphasize the importance of incorporating KD and hypoxia into patient stratification strategies for improved risk assessment and management in sub-Saharan Africa.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its retrospective design and potential for confounding factors, but the evidence is strong given the clear statistical associations observed.

免疫印迹塑造了对KP.2和LP.8.1 疫苗剂量的交叉反应性抗体应答。

Immunological imprinting shapes the cross-reactive antibody responses to the KP.2 and LP.8.1 vaccine doses.
作者： Sanjeev Kumar, Lilin Lai, Madison L Ellis, Anamika B. Patel, Devyani Joshi, Jacob W. Vander Velden, Jana Ziad Abu Faraj, Sonia T. Wimalasena, Ramitha R. Pallavi, Jad Iriss
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在评估单次KP.2 疫苗剂量后体液免疫应答的强度、广度和持续时间。严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2奥密克戎BA.2.86亚变种的出现促使2024-2025 疫苗更新包含KP.2或相关JN.1系刺突抗原。
设计与对象
在21名个体中进行了一项纵向队列研究研究,以评估在接种KP.2 疫苗剂量后6个月内的抗体应答。研究重点在于评估免疫应答对祖先WA.1毒株和各种奥密克戎亚变种的反应。
核心方法
本研究使用活病毒中和试验来测量中和滴度。幂律模型被用于估计针对WA.1和KP.2特异性IgG反应的半衰期。还进行了血清耗竭实验以确定疫苗诱导的抗体的交叉反应性。
主要结果
KP.2 疫苗接种增强了对祖先WA.1毒株以及BA.5、XBB.1.5和KP.2变种的刺突特异性结合和中和抗体。幂律模型估计了WA.1和KP.2特异性IgG反应的半衰期分别为770天和248天。
解释与创新
研究发现,KP.2 疫苗诱导的抗体对近期流行的奥密克戎亚变种保持广泛的中和活性,但对某些新兴变种的中和活性降低表明持续的抗原变化可能影响疫苗的效果。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现有助于了解当前疫苗随时间的性能,并强调了追踪病毒进化和更新疫苗抗原以维持对严重疾病、住院和死亡的广泛保护的必要性。
局限与证据强度
本研究的局限性在于样本量较小以及仅评估单次疫苗剂量。证据强度中等,因为它基于纵向队列研究研究和血清耗竭实验,但结果是直接观察和报告的。

Immunological imprinting shapes the cross-reactive antibody responses to the KP.2 and LP.8.1 vaccine doses.

Authors: Sanjeev Kumar, Lilin Lai, Madison L Ellis, Anamika B. Patel, Devyani Joshi, Jacob W. Vander Velden, Jana Ziad Abu Faraj, Sonia T. Wimalasena, Ramitha R. Pallavi, Jad Iriss
Original abstract (English)
UNLABELLED: The emergence of the SARS-CoV-2 Omicron BA.2.86 subvariant, a lineage derived from the BA.2 strain, led to the 2024-2025 COVID-19 vaccine update to include KP.2 or related JN.1-lineage spike antigens. We evaluated the magnitude, breadth, and durability of humoral immune responses following a single KP.2 vaccine dose in a longitudinal cohort of 21 individuals up to 6 months. KP.2 vaccination increased spike-specific binding and neutralizing antibodies against the ancestral WA.1 strain, as well as against the BA.5, XBB.1.5, and KP.2 variants. Power-law modeling estimated half-lives for WA.1- and KP.2-specific IgG responses at 770 and 248 days, respectively. Additionally, the KP.2 dose increased IgG1 and IgG4 subclasses more than IgG2 and IgG3 responses to both spike proteins. Serum-depletion experiments using WA.1 or KP.2 proteins demonstrated that most vaccine-elicited antibodies were cross-reactive. Consequently, KP.2 vaccine-induced antibodies retained broad neutralizing activity against recently circulating Omicron subvariants (BA.2.86, KP.3.1.1, XEC, LP.8.1, LF.7, XFG.3.12, PQ.1, BA.3.2.1, and RE.2). Using a live virus neutralization assay, XFG.3.12 showed the greatest reduction in neutralizing titers relative to KP.2 (4.2-fold). In a small subset, an LP.8.1 vaccine dose increased neutralizing activity against the matched variant while maintaining WA.1 and KP.2 cross-reactivity, but only modestly increased antibodies to divergent variants BA.3.2.1 and RE.2. Ultimately, these data indicate the KP.2 mRNA vaccine generates durable, cross-reactive responses against current Omicron subvariants. However, ongoing spike evolution impacts the neutralization of emerging lineages, highlighting the need for continued viral monitoring and timely vaccine updates. IMPORTANCE: SARS-CoV-2 continues to evolve, raising ongoing concerns about how well updated vaccines protect against emerging variants. This study evaluates antibody responses after a KP.2 spike mRNA vaccine dose. It shows that a single dose induces durable and broadly cross-reactive immunity against both earlier strains and recently circulating Omicron subvariants. Despite this breadth, reduced neutralizing activity against certain emerging variants indicates that ongoing antigenic changes can impact vaccine-induced antibody effectiveness. These findings provide insight into how current vaccines perform over time and highlight the need to track viral evolution and update vaccine antigens to maintain broad protection against severe disease, hospitalization, and death.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to evaluate the magnitude, breadth, and durability of humoral immune responses following a single KP.2 vaccine dose. The emergence of the SARS-CoV-2 Omicron BA.2.86 subvariant necessitated the 2024-2025 vaccine update to include KP.2 or related JN.1-lineage spike antigens.
Design and population
A longitudinal cohort study was conducted on 21 individuals to assess antibody responses up to 6 months after receiving a KP.2 vaccine dose. The study focused on evaluating the immune response against the ancestral WA.1 strain and various Omicron subvariants.
Core methods
The study used a live virus neutralization assay to measure neutralizing titers. Power-law modeling was applied to estimate the half-lives of IgG responses specific to WA.1 and KP.2. Serum-depletion experiments were also conducted to determine cross-reactivity of vaccine-elicited antibodies.
Main results
KP.2 vaccination increased spike-specific binding and neutralizing antibodies against the ancestral WA.1 strain, as well as against the BA.5, XBB.1.5, and KP.2 variants. Power-law modeling estimated half-lives for WA.1- and KP.2-specific IgG responses at 770 and 248 days, respectively.
Interpretation and novelty
The study found that KP.2 vaccine-induced antibodies retained broad neutralizing activity against recently circulating Omicron subvariants, but reduced neutralizing activity against certain emerging variants indicates ongoing antigenic changes can impact vaccine effectiveness.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings provide insight into how current vaccines perform over time and highlight the need to track viral evolution and update vaccine antigens to maintain broad protection against severe disease, hospitalization, and death.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its small sample size and the focus on a single vaccine dose. The evidence is moderate as it is based on a longitudinal cohort study and serum-depletion experiments, but the results are directly observed and reported.

社论:严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染和长新冠对病毒共感染发病率的影响

Editorial: The influence of SARS-CoV-2 infection and long-COVID on the incidence of viral coinfection
作者： Roberta Rizzo, Rebecca Jane Cox, Alexis Hipólito García, Juan Bautista De Sanctis
摘要中文翻译Abdullah MY (9) 研究了 新型冠状病毒感染 对 1 型糖尿病患者细胞因子和基因多态性的影响。机器学习分析确定 IL-18、CRP 和 IL-6 为 新型冠状病毒感染 病情的关键生物标志物,其中 IL-18 最为重要。在 长新冠 患者中,Fricke F 等 (10) 发现经典单核细胞中促炎基因下调,包括 IL1β 和 CXCL2。他们的分析提示炎症小体活性下降(通过 SNAI1)以及单核细胞分化受损(通过 ATF2),因此得出循环单核细胞要么未成熟要么已耗竭的结论。分析了住院 Omicron 患者的 T 细胞和 TCR 数据,发现相当比例的 TCR 针对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 基因组 的表位,包括参与病毒复制和免疫逃逸的非结构蛋白。研究结果强调 T 细胞保护性免疫受病毒复制周期和 HLA 类型共同影响,进而影响 TCR 亲和力和克隆型形成。此外,Wen,Y. (12) 等评估了 新型冠状病毒感染 患者 Tregs 的异质性和转录组特征,得出 Tregs 可能通过旁观者效应被激活的结论,依据是 TCR 克隆型特征的分析。该分析提出在 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染患者中可能的 治疗 策略。Luo Q 对老年人 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染 6 个月后的应答进行分析。记忆 B 细胞以及 CD69+、CD38+ T 细胞下降,同时 CD4+/CD8+ T 细胞和双阴性 B 细胞中 PD-1+、CTLA-4+ 上升,提示老年人对 BA.5 感染的 免疫应答 可能更快减弱。(14) 开发了一种可解释的机器学习基于 死亡率 的老年人风险分层模型,定位核心免疫生物标志物并揭示剂量阈值效应。模型识别出 10 项关键特征:嗜碱细胞比例、C‐反应蛋白、降钙素原、D‐二聚体、AST/ALT 比值、心肌肌钙蛋白 I、标准碳酸氢盐、年龄、天冬氨酸转氨酶和血氧饱和度。这些参数被视为计算患者生存率的关键。Chen L 等 (15) 回顾了 新型冠状病毒感染 中的线粒体代谢,强调需要长期患者数据并指出分析局限性。他们建议 CoQ10、NAC 和肌酸等营养素可帮助恢复能量代谢并降低氧化应激。作者强调跨学科合作在多组学研究线粒体‐表观遗传相互作用、靶向抗氧化剂以及优化营养递送系统中的重要性。Marandu 等 (16) 发现 疫苗接种 有助于患者从病毒感染中恢复。CD4 细胞仅在活动感染期间受影响,疫苗接种组的 B 细胞和 NK 细胞出现变化。此外,重症患者出现 T 细胞过度反应。Fialho,M.J. 等 (17) 研究了针对 新型冠状病毒感染 及其变体的中和抗体作用。他们得出 新型冠状病毒感染 疫苗接种 能有效产生保护性免疫,但因 Omicron 亚变种的免疫逃逸和免疫衰减仍面临挑战。这些发现凸显了更新加强针策略和持续公共卫生努力以实现全面 疫苗 覆盖和长期保护的必要性。Yin,Z. 等 (18) 分析了大规模血清流行调查数据,发现 IgG 中位抗体水平在 3 个月后下降。较高水平与既往感染和 疫苗接种 相关,而年龄增大尤其在 ≥60 岁成人中与年衰减相关。针对 XBB 再感染的 IgG 保护阈值为 400 AU/mL,每增加一次 疫苗接种 可将再感染风险降低 13‐15%。Bansai A (19) 强调单一蛋白无法诊断后 COVID 患者;表型关联的多分析物面板最具前景。有效的后 COVID 研究需要标准化病例定义、跨平台验证以及整合凝血检测和细胞外囊泡分析以提升组织信号检测。Cruz‐Altamirano,E 等 (20) 对先前 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染后登革热免疫失调进行试点蛋白质组学分析,得出先前 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 暴露可能通过交叉反应抗体增加登革热严重程度,促使 免疫应答 向 Th2 偏移并增强病毒复制。进一步,Parra‐González M. (21) 及其同事回顾了登革热与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的相互作用及其对临床结局的影响,发现共感染可能加重炎症、增加血管和器官损伤并使 治疗 复杂化。然而,抗体依赖增强 (ADE) 的确凿证据仍不充分,强调需进一步机制研究。Zhang,X. 对住院 HIV 与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 共感染患者的蛋白质组学特征进行研究,结果显示 新型冠状病毒感染 在 HIV 患者中导致免疫调节、血栓形成和病毒进入通路紊乱。但仍需更大规模试验以确定共感染对 免疫应答 的影响。Mallapu G 等分析了 新型冠状病毒感染 幸存者有无败血症的程序性细胞死亡标志物,发现两种与疾病严重程度相关的分子特征。第一种特征表现为分泌型程序性细胞死亡标志物(IL‐1‐β、IFN‐γ、MHC I‐A、MHC II‐DRB1)水平升高,见于 新型冠状病毒感染‐败血症和非败血症 队列研究 患者。第二种特征包含细胞内程序性细胞死亡标志物(caspase‐1、caspase‐3、MLKL、p62/SQSTM1),特异于 新型冠状病毒感染‐败血症 队列研究。这些特征可作为疾病严重程度的生物标志物并指导危重症治疗。Calvo‐Silveira,S 等分析了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染与肺炎链球菌定植及疾病严重程度的关系,发现共感染患者合并症更多、临床表现更重。有趣的是,非药物措施可能扰乱 S. pneumoniae 动态,COVID 疫苗接种 能防止共感染并降低疾病严重性。Currey J 等 (25) 使用 C57BL/6 小鼠模型研究流感与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的后病毒后遗症。他们以亚致死剂量的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 (MA30) 或流感 A (PR8) 感染小鼠,并在感染后第 14、21、28 天对肺和脑组织进行组织学分析和散装 RNA 测序,成功建立了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 与流感感染后长期病理的组织特异性轨迹,为解析后病毒肺脑疾病机制提供了基础。该专题汇集了一系列重要手稿,涵盖 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染、疫苗 评估及共感染的多个关键方面,使读者能够全面了解这一复杂现象。值得注意的是,文中提出了多种关于疾病严重程度风险分析、病毒对 免疫应答 的评估以及 疫苗接种 效应的合理方案。此外,还识别出可能对未来研究有益的治疗靶点,包括与 长新冠 相关的目标。尽管疫情已超过六年,但仍有若干未解问题,尤其是感染后后遗症。因此,进一步研究对于阐明感染与共感染的复杂因素仍势在必行。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在探讨 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染和长新冠如何影响病毒共感染的 发病率,强调需要了解这些情况与其他病原体之间的相互作用。
设计与对象
本研究分析了住院 Omicron 患者的 T 细胞和 TCR 数据,重点关注 免疫应答 对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 及其变体的影响,包括参与病毒复制和免疫逃逸的非结构蛋白。
核心方法
Machine learning analyses identified key biomarkers of COVID-19 severity, such as IL-18, CRP, and IL-6, while also examining TCR clonotype characteristics in long COVID patients to assess monocyte function and differentiation.
主要结果
相当比例的 TCR 针对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 基因组 的表位,包括非结构蛋白;分析提示炎症小体活性下降和单核细胞分化受损,表明可能存在免疫耗竭或未成熟。
解释与创新
研究结果凸显了 T 细胞保护性免疫、病毒复制周期和 HLA 类型之间的复杂关系,这些因素共同影响 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染中的 TCR 亲和力和克隆型形成。
科研与公卫意义
结果表明,理解 T 细胞应答及其受病毒因素和 HLA 类型调控的机制,可为改进 免疫应答 并管理 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的长期影响提供策略指导。
局限与证据强度
本研究局限于住院 Omicron 患者,且缺乏长期随访数据,可能影响结果的普适性。鉴于这些限制,证据强度为中等。

Editorial: The influence of SARS-CoV-2 infection and long-COVID on the incidence of viral coinfection

Authors: Roberta Rizzo, Rebecca Jane Cox, Alexis Hipólito García, Juan Bautista De Sanctis
Original abstract (English)
Abdullah MY (9) studied the impact of COVID-19 on cytokines and gene polymorphisms in patients with type 1 diabetes. Machine learning analyses identified IL-18, CRP, and IL-6 as key biomarkers of COVID-19 severity, with IL-18 being the most important.In long COVID patients, Fricke F et al (10) found downregulation of pro-inflammatory genes in classical monocytes, including IL1β and CXCL2. Their analysis suggested reduced inflammasome activity (via SNAI1) and impaired monocyte differentiation (via ATF2), leading them to conclude that circulating monocytes are either immature or exhausted. analyzed T cell and TCR data from hospitalized Omicron patients and found that a significant proportion of TCRs targeted epitopes across the SARS-CoV-2 genome, including non-structural proteins implicated in viral replication and immune evasion. Their findings highlight how T cell protective immunity is influenced by both the viral replication cycle and HLA type, affecting TCR affinity and clonotype formation. Furthermore, Wen, Y. (12) et al evaluated the heterogeneity and transcriptomic characteristics of Tregs in COVID-19 patients and concluded that Tregs may be activated through the bystander effect, as evidenced by the analysis of TCR clonotype characteristics. The analysis proposed possible analyses of treatment strategies in SARS-CoV-2infected patients.The response against SARS-CoV-2 infection in the elderly population after 6 months was analyzed by Luo Q. The decline in memory B cells and CD69+ and CD38+ T cells, along with the rise in PD-1+ and CTLA-4+ in CD4+/CD8+ T cells and double-negative B cells, suggests that immune responses to BA.5 infection may diminish more quickly in the elderly. (14) developed an explainable machine learning-based mortality risk stratification for older adults with COVID-19, pinpointing core immunological biomarkers and revealing dose-threshold effects. The model identified 10 key features: basophil percentage, C-reactive protein, procalcitonin, D-dimer AST/ALT ratio, cardiac troponin I, standard bicarbonate, age, aspartate transaminase, and oxygen saturation. Those parameters were considered critical for calculating patient survival.Chen L et al. (15) reviewed mitochondrial metabolism in COVID-19, highlighting the need for longterm patient data and addressing analytical limitations. They suggested that nutrients like CoQ10, NAC, and creatine could help restore energy metabolism and reduce oxidative stress. The authors emphasized the importance of interdisciplinary collaboration for multi-omics studies of mitochondrial-epigenetic interactions, targeted antioxidants, and optimized nutrient delivery systems.Marandu et al. (16) found that vaccination benefited patient recovery from viral infections. While CD4 cells were impacted only during active infection, changes in B and NK cells were noted in the vaccinated group. Additionally, T cell hyperresponsiveness was seen in patients with severe disease. Fialho, M.J., and colleagues (17) studied the role of neutralizing antibodies against COVID-19 and its variants. They concluded that while COVID-19 vaccination effectively generates protective immunity, challenges remain due to immune evasion by Omicron subvariants and waning immunity. These findings highlight the need for updated booster strategies and sustained public health efforts for full vaccine coverage and long-term protection.Yin, Z. et al (18) analyzed data from large-scale serosurveys and found that median IgG antibody levels declined after 3 months. Higher levels were linked to prior infection and vaccination, while older age was associated with annual decay, especially in adults ≥60 years. The IgG threshold for protection against XBB reinfection was 400 AU/mL, with each additional vaccination reducing reinfection risk by 13-15%.Bansai A (19) emphasized that no single protein can diagnose post-COVID patients; phenotypelinked multi-analyte panels show the most promise. Effective post-COVID research requires standardized case definitions, cross-platform validation, and the integration of coagulation assays and extracellular vesicle profiling to improve tissue signal detection.Cruz-Altamirano, E et al. (20) conducted a pilot proteomic analysis on immune dysregulation in dengue following prior SARS-CoV-2 infection. They concluded that previous SARS-CoV-2 exposure may increase dengue severity through cross-reactive antibodies, shifting the immune response toward Th2 and enhancing viral replication. Furthermore, Parra-González M. ( 21) and colleagues reviewed the interactions between dengue and SARS-CoV-2 and their impact on clinical outcomes. They found that co-infection may worsen inflammation, increase vascular and organ damage, and complicate treatment. However, definitive evidence for antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE) remains inconclusive, highlighting the need for further mechanistic research.The proteomic profile analysis of hospitalized HIV-and SARS-CoV-2 coinfected patients was studied by Zhang, X. The proteomic profile highlighted disruptions in immunomodulation, thrombosis, and viral entry pathways in people living with HIV caused by COVID-19. However, a larger trial is needed to ascertain the effect of coinfection on immune response.The assessment of Programmed cell death markers in COVID-19 survivors with and without sepsis was analyzed by Mallapu G et al. The analysis revealed two molecular signatures linked to disease severity. The first signature, showing high levels of secreted programmed cell death markers (IL-1-β, IFN-γ, MHC I-A, MHC II-DRB1), was found in both COVID-19-Sepsis and no Sepsis cohorts. The second signature, containing cellular programmed cell death markers (caspase-1, caspase-3, MLKL, p62/SQSTM1), was specific to the COVID-19-Sepsis cohort. These signatures may serve as biomarkers for disease severity and guide therapeutic interventions in critical care.The relationship between SARS-CoV-2 infection and Streptococcus pneumoniae colonization and disease severity was analyzed by Calvo-Silveira, S., et al. Patients with co-infections have higher co-morbidities and severe clinical presentations. Interestingly, non-pharmacological measures may disrupt the S. pneumoniae dynamics, and COVID vaccination can prevent the possibility of coinfection and decrease disease severity.Currey J et al. (25) studied post-viral sequelae using a mouse model of influenza and SARS-CoV-2 in C57BL/6 mice. They infected the mice with sublethal doses of SARS-CoV-2 (MA30) or influenza A (PR8) and analyzed lung and brain tissues at 14-, 21-, and 28-days post-infection using histological analysis and bulk-RNA sequencing. They were able to establish distinct tissue-specific trajectories of long-term pathology following SARS-CoV-2 and influenza infection and provide a foundation for dissecting the mechanisms of post-viral lung and brain disease.The topic presents a compelling collection of manuscripts that cover several critical aspects of SARS-CoV-2 infection, vaccine evaluation, and coinfection, thereby enabling readers to gain a comprehensive understanding of this intricate phenomenon. Notably, there are various proposals for conducting rational risk analyses regarding disease severity, evaluating the immune response to the virus, and assessing the effects of vaccination. Furthermore, suggestions for therapeutic targets that may prove beneficial for future investigations, including those related to long COVID, have been identified. Despite the pandemic originating over six years ago, several unresolved issues persist, particularly regarding post-infection consequences. Consequently, further research is essential to unravel the complex elements associated with infection and coinfection.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to explore how SARS-CoV-2 infection and long-COVID influence the incidence of viral coinfection, highlighting the need to understand the interplay between these conditions and other pathogens.
Design and population
The study analyzed T cell and TCR data from hospitalized Omicron patients, focusing on the immune response to SARS-CoV-2 and its variants, including non-structural proteins involved in viral replication and immune evasion.
Core methods
Machine learning analyses identified key biomarkers of COVID-19 severity, such as IL-18, CRP, and IL-6, while also examining TCR clonotype characteristics in long COVID patients to assess monocyte function and differentiation.
Main results
A significant proportion of TCRs targeted epitopes across the SARS-CoV-2 genome, including non-structural proteins, and the analysis suggested reduced inflammasome activity and impaired monocyte differentiation, indicating potential immune exhaustion or immaturity.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings highlight the complex relationship between T cell protective immunity, the viral replication cycle, and HLA type, which influence TCR affinity and clonotype formation in SARS-CoV-2 infection.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results suggest that understanding T cell responses and their modulation by viral factors and HLA types could inform strategies for improving immune responses and managing long-term effects of SARS-CoV-2 infection.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its focus on hospitalized Omicron patients and the lack of long-term follow-up data, which may affect the generalizability of the findings. The evidence is moderate due to these constraints.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2不同变异株感染患者血浆miRNA初步分析

Preliminary miRNA profiling of plasma from patients infected with distinct SARS-CoV-2 variants
作者： Ivana Baranová, Dana Dvorská, Andrea Kapinová, Dušan Braný, Marek Samec, Eva Baranovicova, Veronika Holubekova, Zuzana Kolková, Mária Škereňová, Dušan Loderer
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在探索微小RNA(miRNA)在宿主宿主对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株的应答中的作用,重点关注不同变异株如何影响分子通路,包括表观遗传机制。
设计与对象
该研究采用高通量微阵列技术,评估了感染不同严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株(包括Delta和Omicron)的患者血浆样本中的miRNA表达,并与健康对照组进行了比较。
核心方法
研究团队采用高通量微阵列技术测量miRNA表达水平,并通过miRNA-mRNA相互作用分析识别潜在靶基因。
主要结果
该研究在健康样本对比中,识别出65个在严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2两种变异株中显著差异表达的miRNA,并观察到特定miRNA表达与患者症状严重程度之间存在关联。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,miRNA谱分析可能为宿主对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染的表观遗传学反应提供新的见解,并突出潜在的疾病进展生物标志物。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果可能为开发针对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染中miRNA通路的诊断工具和治疗策略提供参考,包括使用抗病毒药物,为改善预后和治疗带来潜在可能。
局限与证据强度
本研究仅关注miRNA表达谱,未深入分析合并症或通路间交叉影响,且基于单项研究,证据强度为中等。

Preliminary miRNA profiling of plasma from patients infected with distinct SARS-CoV-2 variants

Authors: Ivana Baranová, Dana Dvorská, Andrea Kapinová, Dušan Braný, Marek Samec, Eva Baranovicova, Veronika Holubekova, Zuzana Kolková, Mária Škereňová, Dušan Loderer
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Epigenetic regulation can play a dual role in viral infection, acting as part of the host defense, while being hijacked to control viral latency, replication, and persistence. During the COVID-19 pandemic, substantial evidence indicated that different SARS-CoV-2 variants may lead to distinct outcomes by affecting different molecular pathways, including epigenetic mechanisms. METHODS AND RESULTS: In this study, we assessed miRNA expression using high-throughput microarray technology to simultaneously measure the expression of thousands of miRNAs. We focused on: comparing Delta and Omicron infections with healthy controls; identifying specific miRNAs linked to symptom severity; analyzing miRNA expression across different stages of Delta variant infection; and predicting miRNA target genes and evaluating miRNA-mRNA interactions. We identified 65 miRNAs that were significantly differentially expressed simultaneously in both SARS-CoV-2 variants compared to healthy samples. These miRNAs were categorized into distinct functional groups based on their roles in viral infection. We observed an association between the expression of specific miRNAs and virus-induced symptom severity and patients' outcomes in both variants. Furthermore, we examined miRNA expression across the various phases of the Delta variant and identified 38 miRNAs that were significantly differentially expressed. Finally, mRNA-miRNA interaction analysis revealed PTEN, IGF1R, MYC, and STAT3 as the most interacting genes. CONCLUSION: Profiling thousands of miRNAs in response to different SARS-CoV-2 variants offers new opportunities for diagnosis, prognosis, and therapy. Although comorbidities and pathway cross-talk may influence the obtained results, our findings provide novel insights into the host response to SARS-CoV-2 infection at the epigenetic level.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to explore the role of miRNA in the host response to SARS-CoV-2 variants, particularly focusing on how different variants may affect molecular pathways, including epigenetic mechanisms.
Design and population
The study used high-throughput microarray technology to assess miRNA expression in plasma samples from patients infected with distinct SARS-CoV-2 variants, including Delta and Omicron, compared to healthy controls.
Core methods
The researchers employed high-throughput microarray technology to measure miRNA expression levels and analyzed miRNA-mRNA interactions to identify potential target genes.
Main results
The study identified 65 miRNAs that were significantly differentially expressed in both SARS-CoV-2 variants compared to healthy samples, and observed an association between specific miRNA expression and symptom severity in patients.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that miRNA profiling could provide novel insights into the host's epigenetic response to SARS-CoV-2 infection, highlighting potential biomarkers for disease progression.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results may inform the development of diagnostic tools and therapeutic strategies targeting miRNA pathways in SARS-CoV-2 infections, offering potential for improved prognosis and treatment.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its focus on miRNA expression without detailed analysis of comorbidities or pathway cross-talk, and the evidence is based on a single study with a moderate confidence assessment.

Journal: British journal of health psychology  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-09-01  |  DOI: 10.1111/bjhp.70108  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Future scheduled publication  |  Citation: 2026, 31, 3, e70108
A 高优先级学术文献 · 研究A high priorityAcademic literature · Research

Changes in patient conceptualization of asthma and asthma self-management at key timepoints across the COVID-19 pandemic: A qualitative study.

Changes in patient conceptualization of asthma and asthma self-management at key timepoints across the COVID-19 pandemic: A qualitative study.
作者： Aphra Kite, Daniela Ghio, Chris Keyworth, Tracy Epton
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to explore how people with asthma conceptualized their condition and self-managed it during key stages of the COVID-19 pandemic, as the specific impacts of the pandemic on asthma patients were less certain.
设计与对象
本研究为回顾性横断面定性研究,纳入20名哮喘患者,探索其在疫情前、首次新型冠状病毒感染疫情/封锁期、限制解除期及对其具有重要意义的关键阶段(如新型冠状病毒感染疫情关键事件)的体验。
核心方法
在单一时间点进行半结构化访谈,并采用主题分析法对参与者在疫情期间对哮喘的认知及自我管理体验进行模式识别。
主要结果
共提炼出三个主题:‘进行自身风险评估’、‘哮喘从背景转为前景’及‘在社会中的位置’。前者体现参与者主动评估风险的过程;第二个主题凸显哮喘作为‘既往存在疾病’的突出感知及随之而来的压力与焦虑;第三个主题描述参与者因安全考量在社会中的接纳或排斥体验。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,疫情显著改变了哮喘的认知与管理方式,参与者基于风险评估动态调整行为,符合跨诊断模型。
科研与公卫意义
本研究发现可为未来公共卫生紧急事件中哮喘患者的公共卫生信息传播与支持体系提供参考,以满足其特殊需求与关切。
局限与证据强度
本研究受限于单一时间点的数据收集及对参与者主观体验的依赖,可能引入偏倚。然而,基于代表性哮喘患者样本的主题分析,证据强度较高。

Changes in patient conceptualization of asthma and asthma self-management at key timepoints across the COVID-19 pandemic: A qualitative study.

Authors: Aphra Kite, Daniela Ghio, Chris Keyworth, Tracy Epton
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background The COVID‐19 public health emergency had significant impacts on people living with long‐term health conditions. The specific impacts of COVID‐19 on people with asthma across key stages of the pandemic are less certain. This study therefore aimed to explore the experiences of people with asthma across the pandemic as risk levels changed in a retrospective cross‐sectional study. Methods Semi‐structured interviews (N = 20), conducted at one timepoint, asked about experiences covering pre‐COVID, initial outbreak/lockdown, restrictions lifting, key‐stages salient to them (e.g., vaccination) and longer‐term. Data were analysed using thematic‐analysis. Findings There were three themes. ‘Doing my own risk assessment’ was an active process using pre‐existing/acquired knowledge to determine risk and subsequent asthma‐management/COVID‐19 behaviours at all stages. ‘Asthma from background to foreground’ highlighted how asthma that was mostly previously unobtrusive was suddenly a ‘pre‐existing condition’ leading to prolonged stress and anxiety. ‘Place in society’ describes how the participants were included/excluded by society due to safety concerns from the initial outbreak onwards, which was influenced by government, friends, family and neighbours, the media, health care systems and even themselves. Discussion Findings highlight how some people with asthma experienced the pandemic as a threat to mortality, with evidence of both poor and good adjustment to their asthma management, as per the Transdiagnostic model. When there are future rapid changes in contexts affecting long term conditions, government agencies should use the present study's findings to (a) guide decision‐making, (b) inform public health messaging aimed at targeting at‐risk populations and (c) provide support for at‐risk populations.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to explore how people with asthma conceptualized their condition and self-managed it during key stages of the COVID-19 pandemic, as the specific impacts of the pandemic on asthma patients were less certain.
Design and population
This study was a retrospective cross-sectional qualitative study involving 20 participants with asthma, examining their experiences across pre-COVID, initial outbreak/lockdown, restrictions lifting, and key stages relevant to them, such as vaccination.
Core methods
Semi-structured interviews were conducted at one timepoint, and data were analysed using thematic analysis to identify patterns in participants' experiences and conceptualizations of asthma and self-management during the pandemic.
Main results
Three themes emerged: 'Doing my own risk assessment,' 'Asthma from background to foreground,' and 'Place in society,' highlighting participants' active risk evaluation, heightened awareness of asthma as a pre-existing condition, and societal inclusion/exclusion based on safety concerns.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that the pandemic significantly altered how asthma was perceived and managed, with participants adjusting their behaviors based on evolving risk assessments, as per the Transdiagnostic model.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings can inform public health messaging and support systems for individuals with asthma during future public health emergencies, helping to address their unique needs and concerns.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its single timepoint data collection and reliance on self-reported experiences, which may introduce bias. However, the evidence is strong given the thematic analysis of a representative sample of asthma patients.

在严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变种中估算儿童聚集区的连续间隔和时间变化的再生数:症状出现日期与报告日期

Estimation of serial intervals and time-varying reproduction numbers across SARS-CoV-2 variants in childcare clusters: symptom onset vs. reporting dates
作者： Hee Kyoung Kim, Jin Lee, Young-Man Kim, Sang-Eun Lee, Byung Chul Chun
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
This study aimed to evaluate differences in serial intervals (SI) and time-varying reproduction numbers (Rt) estimates based on symptom onset versus reporting dates in COVID-19 childcare clusters across SARS-CoV-2 variant periods. Respiratory virus transmission in childcare centers is facilitated by dense contact patterns, making accurate estimation of SI and Rt essential for outbreak management.
设计与对象
分析了韩国疾病预防控制 Agency(2020年1月至2022年2月)的全国数据。纳入了四个符合预定义入选标准的基因型确认的聚集区:一个在Delta变种之前,两个Delta变种,一个Omicron变种,每个聚集区至少包含50例流行病学关联病例。
核心方法
连续间隔使用伽马分布进行拟合。时间变化的Rt值分别基于症状出现日期和报告日期使用EpiEstim包进行估算。
主要结果
基于症状出现日期的平均SI在Delta变种之前为9.85天(95% CI:1.97–23.95),在Omicron变种期间降至5.34天(95% CI:2.00–10.28)。相比之下,基于报告日期的SI估计值通常更长,在Omicron变种期间达到8.87天(95% CI:0.71–27.08)。在所有变种时期,基于报告日期的Rt估计值始终低于基于症状出现日期的估计值。
解释与创新
在儿童聚集区中,时间参考的选择对SI和Rt的估算有显著影响。依赖报告日期可能会低估传播的实时强度并扭曲流行病风险评估。
科研与公卫意义
在监测系统中优先使用症状出现日期可能有助于提高高风险环境中的公共卫生响应的准确性和及时性。
局限与证据强度
提供的证据未报告研究设计或分析方法的具体局限性。

Estimation of serial intervals and time-varying reproduction numbers across SARS-CoV-2 variants in childcare clusters: symptom onset vs. reporting dates

Authors: Hee Kyoung Kim, Jin Lee, Young-Man Kim, Sang-Eun Lee, Byung Chul Chun
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Respiratory virus transmission in childcare centers is facilitated by dense contact patterns, making accurate estimation of serial intervals (SI) and time-varying reproduction numbers (Rt) essential for outbreak management. Although symptom onset dates are epidemiologically preferred, reporting dates are frequently used in routine surveillance due to operational constraints. Reliance on reporting dates may introduce systematic bias in transmission parameter estimates. This study aimed to evaluate differences in SI and Rt estimates based on symptom onset versus reporting dates in COVID-19 childcare clusters across SARS-CoV-2 variant periods. Methods Nationwide data from the Korea Disease Control and Prevention Agency (January 2020–February 2022) were analyzed. Four genotype-confirmed clusters meeting the predefined eligibility criteria were included: one Before Delta, two Delta, and one Omicron cluster, each comprising at least 50 epidemiologically linked cases. Serial intervals were fitted using gamma distributions. Time-varying Rt values were estimated using the EpiEstim package separately based on symptom onset and reporting dates. Results Onset-based mean SI declined from 9.85 days (95% CI: 1.97–23.95) in the Before Delta period to 5.34 days (95% CI: 2.00–10.28) during Omicron. In contrast, reporting-based SI estimates were generally longer, reaching 8.87 days (95% CI: 0.71–27.08) during Omicron. Across all variant periods, reporting-based Rt estimates were consistently lower than onset-based estimates. For example, during the Delta period, mean Rt was 1.90 (95% CI: 0.79–3.76) using onset dates compared with 1.08 (95% CI: 0.41–2.39) using reporting dates. Conclusion The choice of temporal reference substantially affects SI and Rt estimation in childcare clusters. Reliance on reporting dates may underestimate real-time transmission intensity and distort epidemic risk assessment. Prioritizing symptom onset data in surveillance systems may improve the accuracy and timeliness of public health responses in high-risk settings.

Prognostic impact of right ventricular dilatation and echocardiography use criteria in hospitalized COVID-19 patients.

Prognostic impact of right ventricular dilatation and echocardiography use criteria in hospitalized COVID-19 patients.
作者： Arnold Méndez-Toro, Ingrid Tatiana Rojas-Ruiz, Luis Eduardo Silva-DiazGranados, Andrés Ruano-Cadena, Manuel Agustín Paz-Meneses, Ricardo Andrés Novoa-Alvarez
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The surge of severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 [coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19)] infection created unprecedented diagnostic and logistical pressures. Given the established potential for cardiac injury and the critical need to optimize scarce resources, establishing the clinical utility and prognostic value of transthoracic echocardiography (TTE) became imperative.
设计与对象
We performed a retrospective cohort study analyzing records from patients with confirmed COVID-19 who underwent TTE. Total 149 patients were analyzed. Median age was 66 years [interquartile range (IQR): 56-73], median hospital stay was 13 days (IQR: 6-23).
核心方法
收集了社会人口学、生化和超声心动图参数。死亡率是主要结局。我们评估了TTE指征的观察者间一致性(Kappa统计量),依据美国心脏病学会基金会2011年和美国超声心动学会2020年的指南,并评估了临床影响的确定(后续患者管理的改变)。
主要结果
总体和重症监护单元死亡率发生率分别为39.6%和60%。升高的生化标志物(白细胞、中性粒细胞、乳酸脱氢酶和C反应蛋白)与死亡率相关。关键的是,右心室(RV)扩张和/或应变(P = 0.008)被确定为唯一显著预测死亡率的超声心动图发现。对AUC分类的观察者间一致性较高(κ ≥ 0.798)。此外,TTE在79.7%的病例中促使了临床管理的改变。
解释与创新
RV pathology is a potent, quantifiable prognostic indicator. While AUC demonstrated high reliability, the significant and frequent changes in management suggest that the current guidelines may possess inherent limitations when addressing the unique, acute prognostic complexities of severe COVID-19 disease.
科研与公卫意义
The findings cautiously inform the importance of incorporating right ventricular assessment in the echocardiographic evaluation of hospitalized COVID-19 patients for improved risk stratification and clinical decision-making.
局限与证据强度
该研究基于回顾性数据,可能引入偏倚并限制因果关系的确立。由于研究性质为观察性且存在潜在混杂因素,证据强度为中等。

Prognostic impact of right ventricular dilatation and echocardiography use criteria in hospitalized COVID-19 patients.

Authors: Arnold Méndez-Toro, Ingrid Tatiana Rojas-Ruiz, Luis Eduardo Silva-DiazGranados, Andrés Ruano-Cadena, Manuel Agustín Paz-Meneses, Ricardo Andrés Novoa-Alvarez
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: The surge of severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 [coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19)] infection created unprecedented diagnostic and logistical pressures. Given the established potential for cardiac injury and the critical need to optimize scarce resources, establishing the clinical utility and prognostic value of transthoracic echocardiography (TTE) became imperative. AIM: To evaluate the prognostic predictors and the adherence to appropriate use criteria (AUC) for TTE in a highly affected setting. METHODS: We performed a retrospective cohort study analyzing records from patients with confirmed COVID-19 who underwent TTE. Socio-demographic, biochemical, and echocardiographic parameters were collected. Mortality was the primary outcome. We assessed inter-observer agreement (Kappa statistic) for TTE indications (based on American College of Cardiology Foundation 2011 and American Society of Echocardiography 2020 guidelines) and the determination of clinical impact (a subsequent change in patient management). RESULTS: Total 149 patients were analyzed. Median age was 66 years [interquartile range (IQR): 56-73], median hospital stay was 13 days (IQR: 6-23). Overall and intensive care unit mortality rates were 39.6% and 60%, respectively. Elevated biochemical markers (leukocytes, neutrophils, lactate dehydrogenase, and C-reactive protein) were associated with mortality. Crucially, right ventricular (RV) dilatation and/or strain (P = 0.008) was identified as the sole echocardiographic finding significantly predictive of mortality. Inter-observer agreement for classifying AUC was high (κ ≥ 0.798). Furthermore, TTE prompted a change in clinical management in 79.7% of the cases. CONCLUSION: RV pathology is a potent, quantifiable prognostic indicator. While AUC demonstrated high reliability, the significant and frequent changes in management suggest that the current guidelines may possess inherent limitations when addressing the unique, acute prognostic complexities of severe COVID-19 disease.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The surge of severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 [coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19)] infection created unprecedented diagnostic and logistical pressures. Given the established potential for cardiac injury and the critical need to optimize scarce resources, establishing the clinical utility and prognostic value of transthoracic echocardiography (TTE) became imperative.

The findings cautiously inform the importance of incorporating right ventricular assessment in the echocardiographic evaluation of hospitalized COVID-19 patients for improved risk stratification and clinical decision-making.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on retrospective data, which may introduce bias and limit the ability to establish causality. The evidence is moderate due to the observational nature of the study and potential confounding factors.
