英格兰在和后续疫苗接种推广期间,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染后患有严重精神疾病人群的死亡率不平等:一项回顾性全国范围关联队列研究研究

Inequalities in mortality following SARS-CoV-2 infection for people with severe mental illness during and following vaccination roll-out in England: a retrospective, whole-country linked cohort study.
作者： Natasha Chilman, Laia Bécares, Alexandru Dregan, Amy Ronaldson, Jadene Lewis, Mehrdad A Mizani, Jayati Das-Munshi, CVD-COVID-UK Consortium
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
People with severe mental illness (SMI) had a higher risk of death following SARS-CoV-2 infection compared with people without SMI during the COVID-19 pandemic peak. It is unclear whether this risk reduced in later stages after vaccination roll-out.
设计与对象
This retrospective, whole-country cohort study analyzed linked electronic health records for all people in England registered to primary care who were alive in November, 2019. The study population included people who had COVID-19 between Jan 31, 2020, and June 30, 2023, with information on deaths and vaccination.
核心方法
Data were extracted from the NHS England Secure Data Environment provided through the British Heart Foundation Data Science Centre CVD-COVID-UK-COVID-IMPACT Consortium linked datasets, which were curated to form a dataset in July, 2024. Cox regression was used to estimate differences in all-cause and COVID-19 mortality.
主要结果
13 463 945 people with COVID-19 were identified, of whom 160 190 (1·19%) had SMI. People with SMI had higher all-cause mortality than those without (adjusted hazard ratio [aHR] 1·56, 95% CI 1·53-1·59). All-cause mortality risk in people with SMI was elevated in stage 1 (aHR 1·27, 95% CI 1·22-1·32), but larger in the stage 2 (aHR 1·56, 1·51-1·61) and stage 3 (aHR 1·55, 1·50-1·59).
解释与创新
Excess mortality in people with SMI persisted throughout the COVID-19 pandemic. Higher COVID-19 mortality risk in people with SMI persisted but was partially attenuated by vaccination (aHR 1·25, 1·17-1·34).
科研与公卫意义
需要采取预防性公共卫生和临床策略,以应对患有严重精神疾病(SMI)人群中 死亡率 的过度发生并降低 疫苗接种 发生率。研究结果凸显了针对该脆弱人群采取有针对性干预措施以减少 死亡率 风险的必要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于关联性电子健康记录,可能在数据完整性和准确性方面存在局限性。由于回顾性研究的性质及潜在未测量混杂因素,证据强度为中等。

Inequalities in mortality following SARS-CoV-2 infection for people with severe mental illness during and following vaccination roll-out in England: a retrospective, whole-country linked cohort study.

Authors: Natasha Chilman, Laia Bécares, Alexandru Dregan, Amy Ronaldson, Jadene Lewis, Mehrdad A Mizani, Jayati Das-Munshi, CVD-COVID-UK Consortium
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: People with severe mental illness (SMI) had a higher risk of death following SARS-CoV-2 infection compared with people without SMI during the COVID-19 pandemic peak. It is unclear whether this risk reduced in later stages after vaccination roll-out. We aimed to assess all-cause and cause-specific mortality following SARS-CoV-2 infection in people with or without SMI across pandemic stages, using whole-country data, while accounting for sociodemographic and clinical characteristics. METHODS: In this retrospective, whole-country cohort study, we analysed linked electronic health records for all people in England registered to primary care who were alive in November, 2019 (>99% of the population). The study population included people who had COVID-19 between Jan 31, 2020, and June 30, 2023, with information on deaths and vaccination. Data extracted from the NHS England Secure Data Environment provided through the British Heart Foundation Data Science Centre CVD-COVID-UK-COVID-IMPACT Consortium linked datasets were curated to form a dataset in July, 2024. Cox regression estimated differences in all-cause and COVID-19 mortality. Analyses were stratified by pandemic stages, defined by WHO policy and UK Government vaccination roll-out: Jan 31-Sept 30, 2020 (stage 1); Oct 1, 2020-July 18, 2021 (stage 2, vaccination roll-out); and July 19, 2021-June 30, 2023 (stage 3, post-vaccination roll-out). SMI comprised schizophrenia, schizoaffective disorder, bipolar disorder, or other affective and non-affective disorder with psychosis. A patient and public involvement panel was consulted during design phases of the study. FINDINGS: 13 463 945 people with COVID-19 were identified, of whom 160 190 (1·19%) had SMI. People with SMI were older [mean age [SD], SMI 49·7 years [17·9], non-SMI 45·0 years [17·6]), had a similar sex split (females SMI vs non-SMI: 56·3% vs 56·6%); and were mostly White British (77·3% or 76·3%). People with SMI had higher all-cause mortality than those without (adjusted hazard ratio [aHR] 1·56, 95% CI 1·53-1·59). All-cause mortality risk in people with SMI was elevated in stage 1 (aHR 1·27, 95% CI 1·22-1·32), but larger in the stage 2 (aHR 1·56, 1·51-1·61) and stage 3 (aHR 1·55, 1·50-1·59). Vaccination prevalence was initially higher for people with SMI (in stage 2: 112 795 [73·2%, 95% CI 72·9-73·4], compared with people without SMI, 8 852 970 [67·4%, 67·3-67·4]). By study end, fewer people with SMI were fully vaccinated (127 145 [79·4%, 79·2-79·6]), compared with people without SMI (11 605 915 [87·2%, 87·2-87·3]. Higher COVID-19 mortality risk in people with SMI persisted but was partially attenuated by vaccination (aHR 1·25, 1·17-1·34). INTERPRETATION: Excess mortality in people with SMI persisted throughout the COVID-19 pandemic. Preventive public health and clinical strategies are needed to address excess mortality and lower vaccination. FUNDING: Health Foundation-Academy of Medical Sciences.

Question and background
People with severe mental illness (SMI) had a higher risk of death following SARS-CoV-2 infection compared with people without SMI during the COVID-19 pandemic peak. It is unclear whether this risk reduced in later stages after vaccination roll-out.
Design and population
This retrospective, whole-country cohort study analyzed linked electronic health records for all people in England registered to primary care who were alive in November, 2019. The study population included people who had COVID-19 between Jan 31, 2020, and June 30, 2023, with information on deaths and vaccination.
Core methods
Data were extracted from the NHS England Secure Data Environment provided through the British Heart Foundation Data Science Centre CVD-COVID-UK-COVID-IMPACT Consortium linked datasets, which were curated to form a dataset in July, 2024. Cox regression was used to estimate differences in all-cause and COVID-19 mortality.
Main results
13 463 945 people with COVID-19 were identified, of whom 160 190 (1·19%) had SMI. People with SMI had higher all-cause mortality than those without (adjusted hazard ratio [aHR] 1·56, 95% CI 1·53-1·59). All-cause mortality risk in people with SMI was elevated in stage 1 (aHR 1·27, 95% CI 1·22-1·32), but larger in the stage 2 (aHR 1·56, 1·51-1·61) and stage 3 (aHR 1·55, 1·50-1·59).

Scientific and public-health significance
Preventive public health and clinical strategies are needed to address excess mortality and lower vaccination rates among people with SMI. The findings highlight the need for targeted interventions to reduce mortality risk in this vulnerable group.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on linked electronic health records, which may have limitations in data completeness and accuracy. The evidence is moderate in strength due to the retrospective nature and potential for unmeasured confounding.

Sources of Stress in Relation to Sleep Health During the COVID-19 Pandemic: Findings from the National Couples' Health and Time Study.

Sources of Stress in Relation to Sleep Health During the COVID-19 Pandemic: Findings from the National Couples' Health and Time Study.
作者： I. Mendez, Rupsha Singh, Jackson CL, Amanda S Hinerman, Rodriquez EJ, Emma Martinez, Camargo JT, Pérez-Stable EJ
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to assess associations between sources of stress and sleep health among married/cohabitating US adults during the COVID-19 pandemic.
设计与对象
本研究分析了美国夫妇健康与时间研究(一项于2020-2021年完成的在线调查)的数据,参与者共3,642人。
核心方法
压力源被分为五个领域,包括整体健康、工作/收入及公共卫生建议。睡眠质量与睡眠时长被测量,并通过调查加权逻辑回归模型计算调整后的优势比(aPR)及95%置信区间(CI)。
主要结果
参与者中,46.6%年龄为35-50岁,44.1%为大学毕业生。不同种族和族裔群体的压力优势比存在差异:整体健康压力在拉丁裔群体中最高(62.0%),工作/收入压力在非裔/非洲裔群体中最高(64.3%)。
解释与创新
研究作者解读认为,整体健康压力和工作/收入压力与已婚及同居个体的较差睡眠健康相关,强调了这些压力源对睡眠结果的影响。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果可为公共卫生危机期间针对脆弱人群的压力管理与睡眠健康促进提供靶向干预依据。
局限与证据强度
本研究受限于自报数据的依赖及对已婚/同居个体的聚焦,可能影响结果的普遍性。由于上述局限性,证据强度为中等。

Sources of Stress in Relation to Sleep Health During the COVID-19 Pandemic: Findings from the National Couples' Health and Time Study.

Authors: I. Mendez, Rupsha Singh, Jackson CL, Amanda S Hinerman, Rodriquez EJ, Emma Martinez, Camargo JT, Pérez-Stable EJ
Original abstract (English)
OBJECTIVE To assess associations between sources of stress and sleep health among married/cohabitating US adults during the COVID-19 pandemic. METHODS We analyzed the National Couples' Health and Time Study, an online survey completed 2020-2021. Stressors were categorized into five domains--1) COVID-19 infection; 2) resources/insurance; 3) overall health; 4) job/income; and 5) public health recommendations. Sleep quality and duration (<7 and 7-9) were measured. Adjusted prevalence ratios (aPRs) and Accepted manuscript ACCEPTED MANUSCRIPT95% CIs were obtained from survey-weighted logistic regression models by estimating predicted marginal probabilities. RESULTS Among 3,642 individuals, 46.6% were aged 35-50 years and 44.1% were college graduates. Within racial and/or ethnic groups, the weighted prevalence of overall health stress was 62.0% among Latino and 55.8% among White individuals; the weighted prevalence of job/income stress was 64.3% among Black/African American and 57.6% among White individuals; and the weighted prevalence of public health recommendations stress was highest among White individuals (34.1%), compared with Black/African American (28.1%) and Latino (27.8%) individuals. Overall health stress was associated with poor/fair sleep quality (aPR=1.35; 95% CI: 1.14-1.60) and <7 hours sleep (aPR=1.19; 95% CI: 1.03-1.39). Job/income stress was associated with fair/poor sleep quality (aPR=1.46; 95% CI: 1.24-1.72) and <7 hours sleep (aPR=1.31; 95% CI: 1.13-1.52). Race-stratified models showed job/income stress significant among Latino and White individuals for both sleep outcomes, and overall health stress among Latino individuals for duration. CONCLUSION Overall health stress and job/income stress were associated with poorer sleep health among married and cohabiting individuals. Futurework is needed on stress-management and sleep health promotion.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to assess associations between sources of stress and sleep health among married/cohabitating US adults during the COVID-19 pandemic.
Design and population
The study analyzed the National Couples' Health and Time Study, an online survey completed between 2020 and 2021, involving 3,642 individuals.
Core methods
Stressors were categorized into five domains, including overall health, job/income, and public health recommendations. Sleep quality and duration were measured, and adjusted prevalence ratios (aPRs) and 95% confidence intervals (CIs) were calculated using survey-weighted logistic regression models.
Main results
Among the participants, 46.6% were aged 35-50 years, and 44.1% were college graduates. The weighted prevalence of stress varied across racial and ethnic groups, with overall health stress being highest among Latino individuals (62.0%) and job/income stress highest among Black/African American individuals (64.3%).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that overall health stress and job/income stress were associated with poorer sleep health among married and cohabiting individuals, highlighting the impact of these stressors on sleep outcomes.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform targeted interventions for stress management and sleep health promotion in vulnerable populations during public health crises.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on self-reported data and its focus on married/cohabitating individuals, which may affect generalizability. The evidence is moderate in strength due to these limitations.

Data-Driven Change-Point SEIR Modeling of the 2025 COVID-19 Epidemic Wave in China: Model Comparison, Robustness Analysis, and Residual Diagnostics

Data-Driven Change-Point SEIR Modeling of the 2025 COVID-19 Epidemic Wave in China: Model Comparison, Robustness Analysis, and Residual Diagnostics
作者： Lilin Xu
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
This study aimed to determine whether the 2025 epidemic wave of COVID-19 in China could be adequately modeled by a standard SEIR model with a constant transmission rate or if a structural change in transmission intensity was supported by the data.
设计与对象
The study analyzed 153 consecutive days of nationally reported COVID-19 cases from 1 May through 30 September 2025, using daily observations digitized from official China CDC figures and calibrated to monthly totals.
核心方法
在两种规格下拟合标准SEIR模型:一种是恒定β基线模型,另一种是具有变化点的分段β模型,变化点通过候选日期的分析和最小化赤池信息准则(AIC)来选择。模型性能通过R平方、均方根误差(RMSE)、AIC和BIC进行评估,稳健性通过变化点、观察缩放和生物参数敏感性分析进行评估,随后进行残差诊断,以检查星期几结构和时间自相关性。
主要结果
AIC分析选择了2025年5月24日作为最佳变化点,比观察到的病例峰值日期2025年5月26日早两天。分段模型显著优于恒定β模型(R平方0.8690 vs. 0.6689;RMSE 1,861.07 vs. 2,959.03;AIC 2,313.85 vs. 2,451.74;BIC 2,329.00 vs. 2,451.74)。模型推导出的再生产指数从变化点前的1.4006下降到变化点后的0.8725。
解释与创新
作者得出结论,数据驱动的分段传播率比恒定率的SEIR模型更准确地描述了2025年的疫情波,表明传播动力学在5月下旬发生了稳健的结构性转变。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现表明,使用变化点建模传播动力学可以提高疫情波预测的准确性,这对公共卫生监测和应对策略具有重要意义。
局限与证据强度
本研究的局限性在于其依赖于全国报告的数据,且在某些极端生物学假设下拟合度可能下降。由于分析仅基于摘要,证据强度中等。

Data-Driven Change-Point SEIR Modeling of the 2025 COVID-19 Epidemic Wave in China: Model Comparison, Robustness Analysis, and Residual Diagnostics

Authors: Lilin Xu
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background: National COVID-19 surveillance in China showed a pronounced epidemic wave during spring 2025, followed by a sustained decline through the summer. This study examined whether that trajectory could be adequately represented by a standard SEIR model with a constant transmission rate or whether the data supported a structural change in transmission intensity. Methods: We analyzed 153 consecutive days of nationally reported COVID-19 cases from 1 May through 30 September 2025. Daily observations were digitized from official China CDC figures and calibrated to the corresponding monthly totals. A standard susceptible-exposed-infectious-removed (SEIR) model was fitted under two specifications: a constant-beta baseline and a piecewise-beta model with a change point selected by profiling candidate dates and minimizing the Akaike Information Criterion (AIC). The main analysis fixed the latent period at 3 days, the infectious period at 5 days, and used an observation scaling factor q to connect model incidence with reported cases. Model performance was assessed using R-squared, RMSE, AIC, and BIC. Robustness was evaluated through change-point, observation-scale, and biological-parameter sensitivity analyses, followed by residual diagnostics for day-of-week structure and temporal autocorrelation. Results: The AIC profile selected 24 May 2025 as the optimal change point, two days before the observed case peak on 26 May. The piecewise model substantially outperformed the constant-beta model (R-squared 0.8690 vs. 0.6689; RMSE 1,861.07 vs. 2,959.03; AIC 2,313.85 vs. 2,451.74; BIC 2,329.00 vs. The model-implied reproduction index declined from 1.4006 before the change point to 0.8725 afterward. This directional transition from expansion to contraction was preserved across all tested change-point dates, observation-scale values, and nine latent/infectious-period combinations, although goodness of fit deteriorated under some extreme biological assumptions. Residuals retained a strong weekly reporting pattern and positive autocorrelation. Conclusions: A data-driven piecewise transmission rate provided a markedly better description of the 2025 epidemic wave than a constant-rate SEIR model. The strongest inference is a robust structural transition in transmission dynamics in late May rather than a precise estimate of any single biological parameter or a causal attribution to a specific intervention.

Question and background
This study aimed to determine whether the 2025 epidemic wave of COVID-19 in China could be adequately modeled by a standard SEIR model with a constant transmission rate or if a structural change in transmission intensity was supported by the data.
Design and population

Model performance was assessed using R-squared, RMSE, AIC, and BIC, with robustness evaluated through change-point, observation-scale, and biological-parameter sensitivity analyses, followed by residual diagnostics for day-of-week structure and temporal autocorrelation.
Main results
The AIC profile selected 24 May 2025 as the optimal change point, two days before the observed case peak on 26 May. The piecewise model substantially outperformed the constant-beta model (R-squared 0.8690 vs. 0.6689; RMSE 1,861.07 vs. 2,959.03; AIC 2,313.85 vs. 2,451.74; BIC 2,329.00 vs. The model-implied reproduction index declined from 1.4006 before the change point to 0.8725 afterward.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors concluded that a data-driven piecewise transmission rate provided a markedly better description of the 2025 epidemic wave than a constant-rate SEIR model, indicating a robust structural transition in transmission dynamics in late May.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that modeling transmission dynamics with a change point can improve the accuracy of epidemic wave predictions, which has implications for public health surveillance and response strategies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on nationally reported data and the potential for goodness of fit to deteriorate under some extreme biological assumptions. The evidence is moderate in strength due to the abstract-only nature of the analysis.

安大略省加拿大严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2活动后肺栓塞风险增加

Increased risk of pulmonary embolism following SARS-CoV-2 activity in Ontario, Canada
作者： Clara Eunyoung Lee, Natalie J. Wilson, David N. Fisman
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染是已知的促凝血触发因素,但其在人群层面与肺栓塞(PE)的时序关系仍不明确。
设计与对象
我们旨在评估呼吸道病毒活动与PE之间的短期关联,并考虑特定的时间滞后。研究在加拿大安大略省进行,采用基于人群的时间序列分析,分析了2011年至2024年期间的PE住院病例。
核心方法
使用分布滞后非线性模型,我们评估了严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2、流感A/B型和呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)标准化周活动与PE风险在5周滞后期内的关联。通过带有交叉基线项的负二项回归模型估计了病毒活动每增加一个标准差(SD)对应的相对风险(RR),捕捉暴露-反应和滞后-反应的非线性关系,并调整了傅里叶季节项和长期趋势。单病毒模型仅评估严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2,而多病原体模型则进一步调整了流感A和RSV。
主要结果
在70,599例PE病例中,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2活动显示出与PE显著的时序关联。5周累计RR每增加一个标准差上升12%(RR 1.12;95% CI 1.00-1.25)。风险遵循一个独特的延迟轨迹,每周累计RR在第3周达到峰值(RR 1.22;95% CI 1.04-1.43)。
解释与创新
多病原体模型显示累积相对风险(RR)较高,为1.26(95%置信区间1.11-1.44),但在第3周出现较低且不显著的峰值(RR 1.13;95%置信区间0.96-1.32)。这一延迟峰值提示存在延长的血栓-炎症窗口期,可能由持续性内皮损伤所驱动。
科研与公卫意义
These findings highlight the vascular burden of COVID-19 and suggest that infection prevention measures, including vaccination, may provide significant downstream protection against thromboembolic disease.
局限与证据强度
提供的证据未报告研究设计的局限性。

Increased risk of pulmonary embolism following SARS-CoV-2 activity in Ontario, Canada

Authors: Clara Eunyoung Lee, Natalie J. Wilson, David N. Fisman
Original abstract (English)
OBJECTIVES: SARS-CoV-2 infection is an established prothrombotic trigger, yet the population-level temporal relationship between circulating viral activity and pulmonary embolism (PE) remains poorly characterized. We aimed to evaluate the short-term association between respiratory viral activity and PE, accounting for specific temporal lags. STUDY DESIGN: Ecological time-series study. METHODS: We conducted a population-level time-series analysis of incident PE hospitalizations in Ontario, Canada, from 2011 to 2024. Using distributed lag non-linear models, we assessed associations between standardized weekly activity of SARS-CoV-2, influenza A/B, and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) and PE risk over a 5-week lag. RR per SD increase in viral activity were estimated via negative binomial regression with cross-basis terms, capturing exposure-response and lag-response non-linearities, and adjusted for Fourier seasonal terms and secular trends. A single-virus model assessed SARS-CoV-2 alone, while a multi-pathogen model additionally adjusted for influenza A and RSV. RESULTS: Among 70,599 PE cases, SARS-CoV-2 activity demonstrated a significant temporal association with PE. Cumulative RR increased 12% per SD over five weeks (RR 1.12; 95% CI 1.00-1.25). The risk followed a distinct delay trajectory, with weekly cumulative RRs peaking at week 3 (RR 1.22; 95% CI 1.04-1.43). The multi-pathogen model, SARS-CoV-2 showed a higher cumulative RR of 1.26 (95% CI 1.11-1.44), with a lower, non-significant peak at week 3 (RR 1.13; 95% CI 0.96-1.32). CONCLUSIONS: Increased population-level SARS-CoV-2 activity is associated with a heightened risk of PE, peaking at approximately the third week. This delayed peak suggests a protracted thrombo-inflammatory window, likely driven by sustained endothelial injury. These findings highlight the vascular burden of COVID-19 and suggest that infection prevention measures, including vaccination, may provide significant downstream protection against thromboembolic disease.

SARS-CoV-2 infection is an established prothrombotic trigger, yet the population-level temporal relationship between circulating viral activity and pulmonary embolism (PE) remains poorly characterized.

Scientific and public-health significance

The supplied evidence does not report limitations of the study design.

A Three-Domain Framework for Interpreting Publicly Reported Outbreak Surveillance Data: Illustrative Lessons from COVID-19 Outbreaks in China

A Three-Domain Framework for Interpreting Publicly Reported Outbreak Surveillance Data: Illustrative Lessons from COVID-19 Outbreaks in China
作者： DINGNAN CAI, CAI D
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
公开报告的暴发 监测数据存在多种不确定性来源,包括病例确认的差异、检测协议和卫生系统状况的变化。本研究旨在探讨此类数据的分析方法学考虑。
设计与对象
The study used two COVID-19 outbreaks in China—the 2020 Wuhan epidemic and the 2021 Putian Delta-variant outbreak—as illustrative cases. It analyzed publicly available data to assess methodological principles relevant to outbreak surveillance.
核心方法
使用公开数据分析了三个分析领域。首先,比较了武汉和其他地区的报告病例死亡率(CFR)。其次,利用时间合理性框架评估了莆田首发病例的RT-PCR检测序列。第三,使用人时标准化的泊松发病率密度框架,比较了两个学生拭子采集者队列研究的感染结果。
主要结果
武汉的CFR(7.68%)是非武汉CFR(2.21%)的3.47倍,这一差异可能与多种解释相容。莆田首发病例的检测序列更符合检疫后期感染,而非从抵达日期起即存在的感染。人时标准化显著改变了学生队列研究比较的解读。
解释与创新
分析展示了三个与暴发 监测相关的分析原则:区域CFR比率在实质性解读前需调整以考虑病例确认条件;RT-PCR检测序列评估应采用时间变化而非恒定的敏感性假设;对于随访时间不等的队列研究,人时标准化是有效比较的前提。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现为解读公开报告的暴发 监测数据提供了谨慎的指导,特别是在评估不同人群和环境中的病例死亡率和感染动态方面。
局限与证据强度
本研究受限于对公开数据的依赖,这些数据可能允许多种解释。由于分析基于摘要级别的数据和假设,证据强度中等。

A Three-Domain Framework for Interpreting Publicly Reported Outbreak Surveillance Data: Illustrative Lessons from COVID-19 Outbreaks in China

Authors: DINGNAN CAI, CAI D
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Publicly reported outbreak surveillance data are subject to multiple sources of uncertainty, including variation in case ascertainment, testing protocols, and health-system conditions. Standard epidemiological methods can be applied to assess whether reported figures are internally interpretable under reasonable statistical assumptions. This study uses two COVID-19 outbreaks in China—the 2020 Wuhan epidemic and the 2021 Putian Delta-variant outbreak—as illustrative cases to examine methodological considerations in the analysis of publicly reported outbreak data. Methods Three analytical domains were examined using publicly available data. First, reported case fatality rates (CFRs) from Wuhan and the rest of China were compared to illustrate the effect of differential case ascertainment on regional CFR ratios. Second, the RT-PCR testing sequence of the Putian index case was assessed using a temporal plausibility framework informed by published time-varying sensitivity curves, without formal probability estimation. Third, infection outcomes among two student swab-collector cohorts (Putian, n = 320, 27 days; Tianshui, n = 413, approximately 3 days) were compared using a person-time standardized Poisson incidence density framework, with exact Poisson confidence intervals and incidence rate ratio estimation. Results The Wuhan CFR (7.68%) was 3.47 times the non-Wuhan CFR (2.21%), a difference compatible with multiple explanations including differential ascertainment and health-system conditions. The Putian index case testing sequence was more consistent with late-quarantine infection than with infection present from the date of arrival, though the available data permit multiple interpretations. Person-time standardization materially changed the interpretation of the student cohort comparison: the Putian cohort contributed 8,640 person-days with zero infections (incidence density: 0, 95% CI: 0–0.43 per 1,000 person-days), compared with the Tianshui cohort’s 1,239 person-days with 3 infections (2.42, 95% CI: 0.50–7.07 per 1,000 person-days). The incidence rate ratio was not calculable under a standard Poisson model due to the zero-event numerator; a mid-p exact method yielded an upper bound consistent with a lower observed rate in the Putian cohort under the stated assumptions, although risk homogeneity cannot be verified. Conclusions The analyses illustrate three methodological principles relevant to outbreak surveillance: regional CFR ratios require adjustment for ascertainment conditions before substantive interpretation; RT-PCR sequence assessment benefits from time-varying rather than constant sensitivity assumptions; and person-time standardization is a prerequisite for valid comparison of cohorts with unequal follow-up duration. Access to primary records would be necessary for more definitive epidemiological characterization of the events described.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Publicly reported outbreak surveillance data are subject to multiple sources of uncertainty, including variation in case ascertainment, testing protocols, and health-system conditions. This study aimed to examine methodological considerations in the analysis of such data.
Design and population
The study used two COVID-19 outbreaks in China—the 2020 Wuhan epidemic and the 2021 Putian Delta-variant outbreak—as illustrative cases. It analyzed publicly available data to assess methodological principles relevant to outbreak surveillance.
Core methods
Three analytical domains were examined using publicly available data. The first compared reported case fatality rates (CFRs) from Wuhan and the rest of China. The second assessed the RT-PCR testing sequence of the Putian index case using a temporal plausibility framework. The third compared infection outcomes among two student swab-collector cohorts using a person-time standardized Poisson incidence density framework.
Main results
The Wuhan CFR (7.68%) was 3.47 times the non-Wuhan CFR (2.21%), a difference compatible with multiple explanations. The Putian index case testing sequence was more consistent with late-quarantine infection than with infection present from the date of arrival. Person-time standardization materially changed the interpretation of the student cohort comparison.
Interpretation and novelty
The analyses illustrate three methodological principles relevant to outbreak surveillance: regional CFR ratios require adjustment for ascertainment conditions before substantive interpretation; RT-PCR sequence assessment benefits from time-varying rather than constant sensitivity assumptions; and person-time standardization is a prerequisite for valid comparison of cohorts with unequal follow-up duration.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings provide cautious guidance for interpreting publicly reported outbreak surveillance data, particularly in assessing case fatality rates and infection dynamics in different populations and settings.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by the reliance on publicly available data, which may permit multiple interpretations. The evidence strength is moderate, as the analyses are based on abstract-level data and assumptions.

可遗传转基因血吸虫作为活体平台分泌严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2中和抗体

Heritable transgenic schistosomes as a living platform for SARS-CoV-2 neutralizing antibody secretion.
作者： Wannaporn Ittiprasert, Michael J. Smout, Victoria H. Mann, Matthew Moyle, Sean M. Kinahan, Daniel N. Ackerman, Danielle N. Rivera, Joshua L. Santarpia, Eric C. Carnes, Margaret M. Mentink-Kane
摘要中文翻译本研究报道了首个可遗传转基因血吸虫株系的生成与传代,以及体内分泌功能性治疗蛋白的株系。研究通过多重CRISPR/Cas介导的同源定向整合技术,将靶向预测基因组安全港的VHH–IgG1 Fc(命名为C5-Fc)转基因插入曼氏血吸虫卵。单毛蚴感染双脐螺后获得亲代P0株系;通过螺类与小鼠宿主的连续传代,获得F2 队列研究,其中所有寄生虫均携带C5-Fc转基因并将C5-Fc分泌至小鼠静脉循环。分子检测确认染色体插入、生殖系传播及系统性分泌。携带C5-Fc转基因蠕虫的小鼠血清在体外中和严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2,其活性与预期的ACE2结合阻断(由重链单域抗体C5可变区介导)一致。研究结果证明:(1)扁形动物稳定可遗传转基因,(2)活体蠕虫在哺乳动物宿主中递送生物活性抗体片段,(3)转基因血吸虫作为持续单次剂量蛋白递送平台的可行性。该技术与递送系统为血吸虫生物学研究提供新的实验方法,并为活体生物制药探索提供动力。曼氏血吸虫的可遗传CRISPR整合产生了一个分泌功能性抗严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2抗体进入小鼠循环的转基因株系。感染小鼠血清的病毒中和实验证明了基于蠕虫的活体生物工厂,可实现持续体内生物制剂递送。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在开发一种新型递送治疗蛋白(特指严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2中和抗体)的方法,利用转基因血吸虫作为活体平台。
设计与对象
研究通过单毛蚴感染双脐螺并经螺类与小鼠宿主连续传代,生成并传代携带可遗传转基因的血吸虫株系,最终获得F2 队列研究。
核心方法
研究采用多重CRISPR/Cas介导的同源定向整合技术,将VHH–IgG1 Fc转基因插入曼氏血吸虫卵;分子检测确认染色体插入、生殖系传播及系统性分泌。
主要结果
分子检测确认C5-Fc转基因的染色体插入、生殖系传播及系统性分泌;携带C5-Fc转基因蠕虫的小鼠血清在体外以强效活性中和严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2,其活性与预期的ACE2结合阻断一致。
解释与创新
研究结果证明转基因血吸虫可作为持续单次剂量蛋白递送平台用于治疗应用(包括严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2中和抗体),展现了该技术的可行性与创新性。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果提示了一种潜在的新策略,用于持续体内递送生物活性抗体,可能对开发针对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2及相关病毒的新型治疗方法产生影响。
局限与证据强度
研究仅基于摘要证据,结果依赖体外中和实验,可能无法完全反映体内疗效或转基因平台的长期稳定性。

Heritable transgenic schistosomes as a living platform for SARS-CoV-2 neutralizing antibody secretion.

Authors: Wannaporn Ittiprasert, Michael J. Smout, Victoria H. Mann, Matthew Moyle, Sean M. Kinahan, Daniel N. Ackerman, Danielle N. Rivera, Joshua L. Santarpia, Eric C. Carnes, Margaret M. Mentink-Kane
Original abstract (English)
We report the generation and propagation of not only the first heritable transgenic schistosome line but also a line that secretes a functional therapeutic protein in vivo. Using multiplexed CRISPR/Cas-mediated homology-directed knock-in targeted to a predicted genomic safe-harbor, we inserted a VHH–IgG1 Fc (termed C5-Fc) transgene into Schistosoma mansoni eggs. Single-miracidium infections of Biomphalaria glabrata yielded parental P0 lines; serial passage through snail and mouse hosts produced an F2 cohort in which all parasites carried the C5-Fc transgene and secreted C5-Fc into the murine venous circulation. Molecular assays confirmed chromosomal insertion, germline transmission and systemic secretion. Sera from mice harboring C5-Fc transgenic worms neutralized SARS-CoV-2 in vitro with potent activity consistent with the expected ACE2-binding blockade by the C5 variable domain of heavy-chain-only antibody (VHH). These results demonstrate (i) stable, heritable transgenesis of a platyhelminth, (ii) delivery of a biologically active antibody fragment by a live helminth in a mammalian host, and (iii) feasibility of using transgenic schistosomes as sustained, single-dose protein delivery platforms. This technology and delivery system enable new experimental approaches for schistosome biology and motivate exploration of living-foundry therapeutics. A heritable CRISPR knock-in in Schistosoma mansonigenerated a transgenic line that secreted a functional anti–SARS-CoV-2 antibody into mouse circulation. Virus neutralization by infected mouse sera demonstrated a worm-based living biofoundry for sustained, in vivo biologic delivery.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to develop a novel approach for delivering therapeutic proteins, specifically SARS-CoV-2 neutralizing antibodies, using transgenic schistosomes as a living platform.
Design and population
The study involved generating and propagating a heritable transgenic schistosome line that secretes a functional therapeutic protein, using single-miracidium infections of Biomphalaria glabrata and serial passage through snail and mouse hosts to produce an F2 cohort.
Core methods
A multiplexed CRISPR/Cas-mediated homology-directed knock-in was used to insert a VHH–IgG1 Fc transgene into Schistosoma mansoni eggs, and molecular assays confirmed chromosomal insertion, germline transmission and systemic secretion.
Main results
Molecular assays confirmed chromosomal insertion, germline transmission and systemic secretion of the C5-Fc transgene, and sera from mice harboring C5-Fc transgenic worms neutralized SARS-CoV-2 in vitro with potent activity.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings demonstrate the feasibility of using transgenic schistosomes as sustained, single-dose protein delivery platforms for therapeutic applications, including SARS-CoV-2 neutralizing antibodies.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results suggest a potential new strategy for sustained in vivo delivery of biologically active antibodies, which could have implications for the development of novel therapeutic approaches against SARS-CoV-2 and related viruses.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to abstract-only evidence, and the results are based on in vitro neutralization assays, which may not fully reflect in vivo efficacy or long-term stability of the transgenic platform.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2和MERS-CoV在感染小鼠肺组织中的差异脂质利用策略通过比较多组学分析揭示

Divergent lipid utilization strategies of SARS-CoV-2 and MERS-CoV revealed by comparative multi-omics profiling of infected mouse lung tissues
作者： Yeonseo Jang, Hyeran Kim, Yufei Li, Jae-Seung Lee, Han-Woong Lee, Nam-Hyuk Cho, Joo-Youn Cho
摘要中文翻译背景 冠状病毒(CoVs),包括严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2(严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2)和中东呼吸综合征冠状病毒(MERS-CoV),可引发具有不同临床结局和病死率的呼吸道感染。然而,这些差异的分子基础仍不清楚。本研究旨在通过直接比较致死性感染小鼠模型肺组织的多组学谱,定义病毒特异性宿主代谢程序。方法 我们对人源血管紧张素转化酶2(hiACE2)-人源二肽基肽酶4(hDPP4)双敲入(DKI)小鼠感染严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2或MERS-CoV后的肺组织进行了整合多组学分析,包括非靶向代谢组学、转录组学和靶向脂质组学。使用数据整合分析与生物标志物发现潜在组分(DIABLO)方法对三个组学层面的数据进行整合,以识别关键区分性分子模式。此外,在感染的Vero E6细胞中检测脂滴动力学,以验证脂质重塑的时间差异。结果 我们识别出两种不同的脂质利用策略。严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染在感染早期(感染后3天,DPI)显示出能量和氨基酸代谢的强烈激活,而MERS-CoV感染则以脂质和核苷酸代谢的持续改变为特征。对三个组学层面的整合DIABLO分析显示,关键区分特征可聚类为病毒特异性分子特征:严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的三酰甘油-脂滴-干扰素轴和MERS-CoV的磷脂-鞘脂-膜中心。在感染的Vero E6细胞中,脂滴动力学的体外实验证实了这一时间差异,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的峰值出现时间早于MERS-CoV。结论 这些发现表明,β-CoVs通过病毒特异性且时间依赖的重塑程序利用宿主脂质代谢,为理解差异性发病机制并开发宿主导向的抗病毒药物策略提供了框架。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究探讨了为什么严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2和MERS-CoV尽管都是β冠状病毒,却导致不同的临床结果,指出这些差异的分子基础仍不清楚。
设计与对象
本研究使用了具有人类化受体(hiACE2-hDPP4双敲入小鼠)的致命性感染小鼠模型,以比较严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2或MERS-CoV感染肺组织的多组学特征。
核心方法
整合的多组学分析包括对肺组织进行非靶向代谢组学、转录组学和靶向脂质组学,并通过DIABLO在三个组学层面应用,以识别关键的区分性分子模式。为验证脂质重塑的时间差异,还检测了感染Vero E6细胞中的脂滴动力学。
主要结果
严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染在感染后3天表现出强烈的能量和氨基酸代谢激活,而MERS-CoV感染则以脂质和核苷酸代谢的持续改变为特征。整合DIABLO分析揭示了病毒特异性的分子特征:严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2具有三酰甘油-脂质滴-干扰素轴,而MERS-CoV则具有磷脂-鞘脂-膜枢纽。
解释与创新
作者将这些发现解释为β冠状病毒通过病毒特异性和时间依赖性的脂质重塑程序利用宿主脂质代谢,为理解差异性发病机制提供了框架。
科研与公卫意义
本研究表明,针对宿主脂质代谢通路可能有助于开发特定针对β冠状病毒的抗病毒药物策略。
局限与证据强度
证据仅限于小鼠模型和体外细胞系统,可能无法完全再现人类感染动态,且本研究依赖于抽象层面的数据,缺乏详细的定量或机制验证。

Divergent lipid utilization strategies of SARS-CoV-2 and MERS-CoV revealed by comparative multi-omics profiling of infected mouse lung tissues

Authors: Yeonseo Jang, Hyeran Kim, Yufei Li, Jae-Seung Lee, Han-Woong Lee, Nam-Hyuk Cho, Joo-Youn Cho
Original abstract (English)
Background Coronaviruses (CoVs), including severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) and Middle East respiratory syndrome (MERS-CoV), cause respiratory infections with distinct clinical outcomes and case fatality rates. However, the molecular basis of these differences remains unclear. In this study, we sought to define virus-specific host metabolic programs by directly comparing multiomics profiles of the lungs of lethally infected mouse models. Methods We performed integrated multiomics analyses, including untargeted metabolomics, transcriptomics, and targeted lipidomics, of lung tissues from human angiotensin-converting enzyme 2 (hiACE2)-human dipeptidyl peptidase 4 (hDPP4) double-knock-in (DKI) mice infected in SARS-CoV-2 or MERS-CoV. Data Integration Analysis and Biomarker discovery using Latent cOmponents (DIABLO) was applied across all three omics layers to identify key distinguishing molecular patterns. Additionally, in vitro lipid droplet kinetics were examined in infected Vero E6 cells to validate temporal differences in lipid remodeling. Results We identified two distinct strategies for lipid utilization. SARS-CoV-2 infection showed strong activation of energy and amino acid metabolism at an early stage of infection (3 days post infection, DPI), whereas MERS-CoV infection was characterized by sustained alterations in lipid and nucleotide metabolism. Integrative DIABLO analysis of all three omics layers revealed that the key distinguishing features clustered into virus-specific molecular signatures: a triacylglycerol–lipid droplet–interferon axis for SARS-CoV-2 and a phospholipid–sphingolipid–membrane hub for MERS-CoV. In vitro lipid droplet kinetics in infected Vero E6 cells confirmed this temporal difference, with SARS-CoV-2 peaking earlier than MERS-CoV. Conclusion These findings show that β-CoVs exploit host lipid metabolism through virus-specific and time-dependent remodeling programs, providing a framework for understanding differential pathogenesis and developing host-directed antiviral strategies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addressed why SARS-CoV-2 and MERS-CoV cause distinct clinical outcomes despite both being betacoronaviruses, noting that the molecular basis for these differences remains unclear.
Design and population
The research used lethally infected mouse models with humanized receptors (hiACE2-hDPP4 double-knock-in mice) to compare multi-omics profiles of lung tissues infected with SARS-CoV-2 or MERS-CoV.
Core methods
Integrated multi-omics analyses included untargeted metabolomics, transcriptomics, and targeted lipidomics of lung tissues, with DIABLO applied across all three omics layers to identify key distinguishing molecular patterns. In vitro lipid droplet kinetics were examined in infected Vero E6 cells to validate temporal differences in lipid remodeling.
Main results
SARS-CoV-2 infection showed strong activation of energy and amino acid metabolism at 3 days post infection, whereas MERS-CoV infection was characterized by sustained alterations in lipid and nucleotide metabolism. Integrative DIABLO analysis revealed virus-specific molecular signatures: a triacylglycerol–lipid droplet–interferon axis for SARS-CoV-2 and a phospholipid–sphingolipid–membrane hub for MERS-CoV.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted these findings as evidence that β-coronaviruses exploit host lipid metabolism through virus-specific and time-dependent remodeling programs, providing a framework for understanding differential pathogenesis.
Scientific and public-health significance
The study suggests that targeting host lipid metabolism pathways could inform the development of host-directed antiviral strategies tailored to specific betacoronaviruses.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence is limited to mouse models and in vitro cell systems, which may not fully recapitulate human infection dynamics, and the study relies on abstract-level data without detailed quantitative or mechanistic validation.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 在无感染情况下破坏人血脑屏障模型的完整性

SARS-CoV-2 disrupts the integrity of a human blood-brain barrier model in the absence of infection
作者： Habib Jmii, Ruth Haverty, Keith D. Rochfort, Nicola F. Fletcher
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
Neurological symptoms such as anosmia, ageusia, cognitive impairments, encephalitis, and ischemic strokes are recognized in patients with COVID-19, and persistent cognitive impairment, including 'brain fog', is part of the long COVID syndrome.
设计与对象
本研究使用由原代人微血管内皮细胞、星形胶质细胞、周细胞和小胶质细胞组成的体外血脑屏障(BBB)模型,调查了灭活的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 或 刺突蛋白 是否能破坏 BBB 完整性,并在无感染情况下表征神经炎症。
核心方法
研究人员使用体外 BBB 模型,将细胞暴露于 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 或 刺突蛋白,通过趋化因子、炎症细胞因子和黏附因子的转录上调以及炎症细胞因子释放,观察 BBB 完整性受损和免疫激活。
主要结果
无论是腔面(模拟“血侧”)还是反腔面(“脑侧”)暴露于 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 或 刺突蛋白,均可观察到 BBB 完整性受损。此外,还观察到趋化因子、炎症细胞因子和黏附因子的转录上调,以及炎症细胞因子释放。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,在无直接感染的情况下,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 仍可在体外诱导 BBB 完整性受损和免疫激活,强调了 宿主 免疫系统在 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染期间破坏 BBB 中的潜在作用。
科研与公卫意义
These results provide insights into the mechanisms of BBB disruption in the context of SARS-CoV-2 infection and underscore the importance of the host immune response in neurological complications associated with COVID-19.
局限与证据强度
本研究仅限于体外模型,未考虑体内复杂性,但证据较为充分,因为其直接证明了在无感染情况下 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 可诱导 BBB 完整性受损和免疫激活。

SARS-CoV-2 disrupts the integrity of a human blood-brain barrier model in the absence of infection

Authors: Habib Jmii, Ruth Haverty, Keith D. Rochfort, Nicola F. Fletcher
Original abstract (English)
Neurological symptoms are recognized in patients with COVID-19, and include anosmia, ageusia, cognitive impairments as well as more severe complications including encephalitis and ischemic strokes. Furthermore, persistent cognitive impairment including 'brain fog' is recognised as part of the long COVID syndrome. While SARS-CoV-2 has been shown to infect cells of the central nervous system (CNS) in vitro and in vivo, it is unclear whether direct infection of the CNS or indirect mechanisms including activation of the coagulation cascade or immune activation are the key drivers of COVID-19 neuropathology. We investigated whether inactivated SARS-CoV-2, or spike protein, can disrupt the integrity of the blood-brain barrier (BBB) and characterised neuroinflammation in the absence of infection. Using an in vitro model of the BBB composed of primary human microvascular endothelial cells, astrocytes, pericytes and microglia, we observed BBB disruption following exposure of the model to SARS-CoV-2 or spike protein. Importantly, loss of BBB integrity was observed in response to luminal (mimicking 'blood side') or abluminal ('brain side') exposure to inactivated virus or spike protein. Transcriptional upregulation of a range of chemokines, inflammatory cytokines and adhesion factors, and release of inflammatory cytokines from BBB models was also observed in response to luminal or abluminal SARS-CoV-2 or spike protein exposure. These data indicate that BBB disruption and immune activation in vitro occurs in response to SARS-CoV-2 in the absence of infection, highlighting the importance of the host immune system in BBB disruption during SARS-CoV-2 infection.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Neurological symptoms such as anosmia, ageusia, cognitive impairments, encephalitis, and ischemic strokes are recognized in patients with COVID-19, and persistent cognitive impairment, including 'brain fog', is part of the long COVID syndrome.
Design and population
The study investigated whether inactivated SARS-CoV-2 or spike protein can disrupt the integrity of the blood-brain barrier (BBB) and characterised neuroinflammation in the absence of infection using an in vitro model of the BBB composed of primary human microvascular endothelial cells, astrocytes, pericytes, and microglia.
Core methods
Using an in vitro BBB model, the researchers exposed the cells to SARS-CoV-2 or spike protein and observed BBB disruption and immune activation through transcriptional upregulation of chemokines, inflammatory cytokines, and adhesion factors, as well as release of inflammatory cytokines.
Main results
BBB disruption was observed following exposure to SARS-CoV-2 or spike protein, regardless of whether the exposure was luminal (mimicking 'blood side') or abluminal ('brain side'). Transcriptional upregulation of chemokines, inflammatory cytokines, and adhesion factors, along with release of inflammatory cytokines, was also noted.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that BBB disruption and immune activation can occur in vitro in response to SARS-CoV-2 in the absence of direct infection, highlighting the potential role of the host immune system in BBB disruption during SARS-CoV-2 infection.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results provide insights into the mechanisms of BBB disruption in the context of SARS-CoV-2 infection and underscore the importance of the host immune response in neurological complications associated with COVID-19.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to an in vitro model and does not account for in vivo complexities, but the evidence is strong as it directly demonstrates BBB disruption and immune activation in response to SARS-CoV-2 in the absence of infection.

Inferring diagnostic serial intervals to understand COVID-19 transmission dynamics in Hong Kong and Mainland China.

Inferring diagnostic serial intervals to understand COVID-19 transmission dynamics in Hong Kong and Mainland China.
作者： M. P. Hossain, Dongxuan Chen, A. Yeung, Adam DC, Lim WW, Lau YC, Eric H. Y. Lau, Wong JY, F. Ho, Huizhi Gao
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在通过推断严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的代间隔(通常以潜伏期近似替代)来准确估算其瞬时繁殖数(Rt),以理解传播动态,满足流行病学评估需求。
设计与对象
本研究分析了香港三波祖先谱系与中国内地首波确诊严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2病例,以诊断间隔(SId)与传统潜伏期(SI)作为可比指标进行研究。
核心方法
研究采用贝叶斯方法推断有效潜伏期(SI)与诊断间隔(SId)的分布,同时推断发病至报告延迟,并比较由此产生的繁殖数(Rt)估算值。
主要结果
SI与SId的分布在各波次间具有可比性,SId均值更短。感染者的报告延迟长于被感染者,这一因素被确认为影响SI与SId时间变异的关键因素。
解释与创新
研究认为,诊断间隔(SId)作为传统潜伏期(SI)的可比指标,在传播性估算中具有潜力,并可推广应用于其他呼吸道病毒。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果可为监测及严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 传播动态分析提供信息,为公共卫生评估提供更可靠的传播性估算指标。
局限与证据强度
本研究受限于摘要证据的依赖性及临床数据潜在回忆偏倚,但基于SI与SId的时间变异繁殖数估算高度一致。

Inferring diagnostic serial intervals to understand COVID-19 transmission dynamics in Hong Kong and Mainland China.

Authors: M. P. Hossain, Dongxuan Chen, A. Yeung, Adam DC, Lim WW, Lau YC, Eric H. Y. Lau, Wong JY, F. Ho, Huizhi Gao
Original abstract (English)
Background Estimating the instantaneous reproduction numbers (Rt) requires inferring generation time (Rt>, the interval between successive infections in the transmission chain), often approximated by the serial interval (SI, the interval between successive onsets in the transmission chain). The serial interval based on clinical outcomes is subject to recall biases, and such clinical information is not always available. As a comparable metric, we defined the diagnostic serial interval (SId, the time between diagnostic reporting of case pairs in a transmission chain) and compared it with the traditional SI. Methods We analyzed confirmed COVID-19 cases from three ancestral waves in Hong Kong and the first wave in mainland China. Using Bayesian methods, we inferred the distributions of effective SI and SId, along with onset-to-reporting delays, and compared the resulting Rt estimates. Results The distributions of SI and SId were comparable across waves, with shorter means observed in SId. Reporting delays for infectors were longer than those for infectees, which was identified as a key factor influencing the temporal variation in SI and SId. Additionally, factors such as public health and social measures (PHSMs), case profile, and demographics were found to significantly impact the estimates. Time-varying estimates of the reproduction number derived from both SI and SId were highly consistent, with median absolute differences ranging from 0.12 to 0.19. Conclusions The diagnostic serial interval shows potentials as a comparable metric to the traditional serial interval for estimating transmissibility in assessing COVID-19 transmission dynamics, and this approach could be extended for other respiratory viruses.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the need for accurate estimation of the reproduction number (Rt) of SARS-CoV-2 by inferring generation time, often approximated by the serial interval (SI), which is critical for understanding transmission dynamics.
Design and population
The study analyzed confirmed SARS-CoV-2 cases from three ancestral waves in Hong Kong and the first wave in mainland China, focusing on the diagnostic serial interval (SId) and traditional serial interval (SI) as comparable metrics.
Core methods
The researchers used Bayesian methods to infer the distributions of effective serial interval (SI) and diagnostic serial interval (SId), along with onset-to-reporting delays, and compared the resulting reproduction number (Rt) estimates.
Main results
The distributions of SI and SId were comparable across waves, with shorter means observed in SId. Reporting delays for infectors were longer than those for infectees, which was identified as a key factor influencing the temporal variation in SI and SId.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that the diagnostic serial interval (SId) has potential as a comparable metric to the traditional serial interval (SI) for estimating transmissibility and could be extended for other respiratory viruses.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform the surveillance and analysis of SARS-CoV-2 transmission dynamics, offering a more reliable metric for estimating transmissibility in the context of public health assessments.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on abstract-only evidence and the potential for recall biases in clinical data, but the time-varying estimates of the reproduction number derived from both SI and SId were highly consistent.

The study aimed to examine the association between the spread of disinformation ("fake news") and the incidence of new COVID-19 cases in Brazil between March 2020 and December 2022.

Impact of disinformation on the dynamics of the COVID-19 pandemic in Brazil

Authors: Letícia Pereira, Silva Cd, Isaac Schrarstzhaupt, Silva MdFe, Vanessa Sígolo, Soraya [UNIFESP] Smaili, Caroline Da Silva, Mariana de França e Silva
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Aim: To examine the association between the spread of disinformation ("fake news") and the incidence of new COVID-19 cases in Brazil between March 2020 and December 2022. Subject and Methods: This study presents an ecological time-series analysis examining the association between the spread of disinformation (“fake news”) and the incidence of new COVID-19 cases in the country between March 2020 and December 2022. We analyzed 194 items of fake news that had been verified by fact-checking agencies, and examined them using dynamic regression with ARIMA errors (ARIMAX), adjusted for socioeconomic variables, social media use and the residential mobility index. Residential mobility was assessed as a potential mediator of the relationship between disinformation and COVID-19 incidence. Results Vaccines were the main topic of disinformation, followed by chloroquine/hydroxychloroquine, masks, and ivermectin. Peaks in fake news coincided with milestones in the vaccination campaign. The study aimed to examine the association between the spread of disinformation ("fake news") and the incidence of new COVID-19 cases in Brazil between March 2020 and December 2022.

Main results

Interpretation and novelty

Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform targeted public health interventions to combat disinformation, especially regarding vaccines, to mitigate the spread of COVID-19.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its ecological design and reliance on verified fake news items, which may not capture individual-level behaviors, and the evidence is moderate in strength due to these constraints.

Convalescent Plasma Restores Viral Clearance After Anti-Spike Monoclonal Antibody Failure in Immunocompromised COVID-19 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Patient Data.

Convalescent Plasma Restores Viral Clearance After Anti-Spike Monoclonal Antibody Failure in Immunocompromised COVID-19 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Patient Data.
作者： Daniele Focosi, Tommaso Francesco Aiello, Arturo Casadevall, Mario Cruciani, Alessandra D'Abramo, Patrizia De Marco, David Gachoud, Carolina Garcia-Vidal, Claudia Glingani, Thomas Hueso
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review examines the efficacy of high-titre COVID-19 convalescent plasma (CCP) in restoring viral clearance among immunocompromised patients with persistent SARS-CoV-2 viraemia following failure of anti-Spike monoclonal antibody (mAb) therapy, focusing on the central question of whether CCP can overcome resistance to mAbs in this population.
证据基础与方法
进行了系统评价和个体参与者数据荟萃分析,检索了 2020 年 1 月至 2026 年 1 月的电子数据库。该方案在 PROSPERO 注册(CRD420251142891)。纳入了 47 例病例(12 项已发表研究中的 30 例和 17 例未发表病例),资格标准包括免疫功能低下患者在 mAb 治疗后 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 持续呈阳性。
共识与结论
该综述得出的结论是,在 70.2% (33/47) 的 mAb 耐药感染免疫功能低下患者中,CCP 输注与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 清除相关,平均输注 2.7 个 CCP 单位。在逻辑回归分析中,CCP 输注总量与病毒清除率呈正相关。
争议与缺口
该综述没有报告长期结果、病毒清除的持久性或与较新的 抗病毒药物 药物的疗效比较。此外,样本量小(47 个案例)和对未发表案例的依赖限制了结论的普遍性和稳健性。
科研与实践启示
该综述强调需要进行随机对照试验来验证 CCP 在免疫功能低下人群中的疗效并确定最佳剂量策略。建议将 监测 用于对 mAb 和 CCP 均具有抗性的新兴 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体,以指导 治疗 方案。

Convalescent Plasma Restores Viral Clearance After Anti-Spike Monoclonal Antibody Failure in Immunocompromised COVID-19 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Patient Data.

Authors: Daniele Focosi, Tommaso Francesco Aiello, Arturo Casadevall, Mario Cruciani, Alessandra D'Abramo, Patrizia De Marco, David Gachoud, Carolina Garcia-Vidal, Claudia Glingani, Thomas Hueso
Original abstract (English)
Anti-Spike monoclonal antibodies (mAbs) progressively lost efficacy during the COVID-19 pandemic due to the emergence and predominance of resistant SARS-CoV-2 variants. By contrast, high-titre COVID-19 convalescent plasma (CCP) collected from vaccinated donors recently recovered from infection provides a polyclonal source of antibodies that remains effective in clearing SARS-CoV-2 in immunosuppressed patients, unable to mount an adequate immune response against the virus. We conducted a systematic review and individual participant data meta-analysis to examine the effect of CCP in immunocompromised patients persistently positive for SARS-CoV-2 viraemia following mAb therapy, and to evaluate possible biological factors associated with a favourable outcome. Electronic databases were searched for studies published from January 2020 to January 2026. All studies including eligible cases were considered in the systematic review. Unpublished cases were also collected from investigators of the selected studies. The protocol was registered at PROSPERO (CRD420251142891). Forty-seven cases (30 cases from 12 published studies and 17 unpublished cases) were included. In 33 out of 47 patients (70.2%) with a mAb-resistant infection, SARS-CoV-2 clearance occurred following CCP transfusion, with a mean of 2.7 CCP units administered. In logistic regression, total CCP volume transfused was positively associated with SARS-CoV-2 clearance. In conclusion, CCP transfusion was associated with SARS-CoV-2 clearance in persistently positive immunocompromised patients following failure of anti-Spike mAb therapy.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the efficacy of high-titre COVID-19 convalescent plasma (CCP) in restoring viral clearance among immunocompromised patients with persistent SARS-CoV-2 viraemia following failure of anti-Spike monoclonal antibody (mAb) therapy, focusing on the central question of whether CCP can overcome resistance to mAbs in this population.
Evidence base and method
A systematic review and individual participant data meta-analysis was conducted, searching electronic databases from January 2020 to January 2026. The protocol was registered at PROSPERO (CRD420251142891). Forty-seven cases (30 from 12 published studies and 17 unpublished cases) were included, with eligibility criteria encompassing immunocompromised patients persistently positive for SARS-CoV-2 after mAb therapy.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that CCP transfusion was associated with SARS-CoV-2 clearance in 70.2% (33/47) of immunocompromised patients with mAb-resistant infections, with a mean of 2.7 CCP units administered. Total CCP volume transfused was positively associated with viral clearance in logistic regression analysis.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not report long-term outcomes, durability of viral clearance, or comparative efficacy against newer antiviral agents. Additionally, the small sample size (47 cases) and reliance on unpublished cases limit generalizability and robustness of conclusions.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the need for randomized controlled trials to validate CCP efficacy in immunocompromised populations and to determine optimal dosing strategies. Surveillance for emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants resistant to both mAbs and CCP is recommended to guide treatment protocols.

Psychoeducational Challenges of Dyslexic Children During the COVID-19 Pandemic: A Systematic Review.

Psychoeducational Challenges of Dyslexic Children During the COVID-19 Pandemic: A Systematic Review.
作者： Seyed Alireza Ghasemi, Mahdi Salarsadeghi, Minoo Miri, Ahmad Khamesan, Ahmad Khāmesan, Miri Minoo
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review focuses on the psychoeducational challenges faced by dyslexic children during the COVID-19 pandemic, particularly in the context of online learning. It examines psychological and educational difficulties, including anxiety, low self-esteem, reduced reading interest, limited access to educational technologies, insufficient teacher support, and challenges with reading fluency and concentration. The central question is about the impact of the pandemic on dyslexic children's learning and mental health.
证据基础与方法
本系统性综述通过电子检索 PubMed、Wiley、Scopus、Web of Science、Google Scholar 及 ScienceDirect 等数据库识别相关文献,并辅以滚雪球法(即检查检索文献参考文献目录)补充文献。研究采用了全面的检索策略,以收集该主题的所有相关研究。
共识与结论
研究发现,有阅读障碍的儿童在大流行期间经历了重大的心理和教育挑战。这些问题包括焦虑增加、自尊心降低、阅读兴趣降低、获得教育技术的机会有限、教师支持不足以及阅读流畅性和注意力困难。研究结果强调需要采取有针对性的干预措施来支持大流行后时期的这些儿童。
争议与缺口
审查指出,证据范围有限,重点是心理和教育挑战,而不是 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的生物学或病毒学方面。目前缺乏关于大流行影响阅读障碍儿童的具体机制的详细数据,并且该研究没有深入探讨这些挑战的长期影响。
科研与实践启示
研究表明,需要采取有针对性的、基于证据的策略来减轻阅读障碍儿童在大流行期间和之后面临的挑战。这些策略包括教师培训、家校合作、适应性学习技术和心理支持。该评论强调了这些干预措施对于改善阅读障碍儿童的教育成果和心理健康的重要性。

Psychoeducational Challenges of Dyslexic Children During the COVID-19 Pandemic: A Systematic Review.

Authors: Seyed Alireza Ghasemi, Mahdi Salarsadeghi, Minoo Miri, Ahmad Khamesan, Ahmad Khāmesan, Miri Minoo
Original abstract (English)
Dyslexic students faced psychological and educational challenges during the COVID-19 pandemic and online learning, including increased anxiety, low self-esteem, reduced reading interest, limited access to educational technologies, insufficient teacher support, and difficulties with reading fluency and concentration. Examining these challenges is crucial for implementing effective measures and enhancing post-pandemic learning. This study investigates the psycho-educational challenges of dyslexic children during the pandemic, highlighting the need for targeted interventions to support them in current and future contexts. This systematic review identified relevant articles through electronic searches of PubMed, Wiley, Scopus, Web of Science, Google Scholar, and ScienceDirect, supplemented by the snowballing technique, which involved examining the bibliographies of retrieved references. The study identified two main categories of challenges for dyslexic children: psychological (mental health, emotional well-being, behavioural issues, parental stress) and educational (socioeconomic status, technology access, special educational needs, teacher support, remote learning difficulties) affecting children with learning disabilities. The research results show that dyslexic children faced various psychological and educational difficulties identified in this review during the COVID-19 pandemic. Therefore, targeted, evidence-based strategies-such as teacher training, family-school collaboration, adaptive learning technologies, and psychological support-are needed to mitigate these challenges in the post-pandemic period.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the psychoeducational challenges faced by dyslexic children during the COVID-19 pandemic, particularly in the context of online learning. It examines psychological and educational difficulties, including anxiety, low self-esteem, reduced reading interest, limited access to educational technologies, insufficient teacher support, and challenges with reading fluency and concentration. The central question is about the impact of the pandemic on dyslexic children's learning and mental health.
Evidence base and method
The systematic review identified relevant articles through electronic searches of PubMed, Wiley, Scopus, Web of Science, Google Scholar, and ScienceDirect, supplemented by the snowballing technique, which involved examining the bibliographies of retrieved references. The study included a comprehensive search strategy to gather all pertinent research on the topic.
Consensus and conclusions
The study found that dyslexic children experienced significant psychological and educational challenges during the pandemic. These included increased anxiety, low self-esteem, reduced reading interest, limited access to educational technologies, insufficient teacher support, and difficulties with reading fluency and concentration. The findings highlight the need for targeted interventions to support these children in the post-pandemic period.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that the evidence is limited in scope, with a focus on psychological and educational challenges rather than biological or virological aspects of SARS-CoV-2. There is a lack of detailed data on the specific mechanisms by which the pandemic affected dyslexic children, and the study does not address the long-term implications of these challenges in depth.
Research and practice implications
The research suggests that targeted, evidence-based strategies are needed to mitigate the challenges faced by dyslexic children during and after the pandemic. These strategies include teacher training, family-school collaboration, adaptive learning technologies, and psychological support. The review emphasizes the importance of these interventions for improving educational outcomes and mental health for dyslexic children.

Timing, Titre, and Host Determinants of Convalescent Plasma Efficacy in COVID-19: A Systematic Review With Design-Stratified Narrative Synthesis.

Timing, Titre, and Host Determinants of Convalescent Plasma Efficacy in COVID-19: A Systematic Review With Design-Stratified Narrative Synthesis.
作者： Agnieszka Kuś, Sebastian Makuch, Jarosław Dybko, Siddarth Agrawal
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review examines the efficacy of convalescent plasma (CP) for COVID-19 caused by SARS-CoV-2, focusing on three interacting determinants: timing of administration, antibody titre, and host characteristics, to reconcile conflicting trial outcomes.
证据基础与方法
进行了设计分层叙述综合的系统评价(PRISMA 2020;SWiM),检索 PubMed、Web of Science 和 Scopus,直至 2026 年 5 月,将标准化的 91 场提取矩阵应用于 31 项纳入研究(20,793 名患者;8 项随机对照试验,23 项观察性研究)。
共识与结论
CP 仅在特定条件下有效:早期给药、足够的抗体滴度以及缺乏内源性抗体反应的血清阴性或免疫功能低下的 宿主。在随机对照试验中,6 项中的 4 项发现早期给药有益,4 项中的 3 项发现较高滴度的 CP 有益,两项大型随机对照试验 (n=12,522) 显示对血清阴性受者有优先益处。
争议与缺口
CP 产品中的非 IgG 免疫球蛋白 (IgA/IgM) 的表征几乎不存在,尽管有证据表明它们对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 具有有效的中和活性,但 31 项研究中只有 2 项报告了此类数据,这代表了产品评估中的系统性差距。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应优先考虑 CP 产品中 IgA/IgM 滴度的标准化量化,并评估未来大流行中被动免疫治疗的精确部署策略,利用时机和滴度决定因素来优化疗效。

Timing, Titre, and Host Determinants of Convalescent Plasma Efficacy in COVID-19: A Systematic Review With Design-Stratified Narrative Synthesis.

Authors: Agnieszka Kuś, Sebastian Makuch, Jarosław Dybko, Siddarth Agrawal
Original abstract (English)
The efficacy of convalescent plasma (CP) in COVID-19 has been contested, with large randomized trials producing conflicting results that led to recommendations against routine use. We performed a systematic review with design-stratified narrative synthesis (PRISMA 2020; SWiM; OSF: https://osf.io/tjcuq) to test whether these conflicting outcomes can be reconciled through three interacting determinants-timing, antibody titre, and host characteristics-using a standardized 91-field extraction matrix applied to each study. PubMed, Web of Science, and Scopus were searched through May 2026. Thirty-one studies (20,793 patients; 8 RCTs, 23 observational) were included. The synthesis indicates that CP is effective, but only under a specific convergence of conditions: when administered early in the disease course, with sufficient antibody titre, to seronegative or immunocompromised hosts who have not yet mounted an endogenous antibody response. Among RCTs analysing timing, 4 of 6 found benefit with earlier administration; for titre, 3 of 4 RCTs with explicit comparisons found benefit with higher-titre CP; and two large RCTs (combined n = 12,522) demonstrated preferential benefit in seronegative recipients. This interpretation is consistent with modelling estimates that CP, deployed largely to hospitalised patients, nonetheless saved tens of thousands of lives in the United States during the first pandemic year, an effect attributed to the subset treated early enough to modify disease progression. A second, previously unquantified finding concerns antibody class: characterisation of non-IgG immunoglobulins was nearly absent, with only 2 of 31 studies-both from our group-reporting IgA or IgM data, despite evidence that IgA and IgM are among the most potent neutralising classes against SARS-CoV-2. This hypothesis-generating observation identifies a systematic gap in CP product assessment. These findings reframe CP efficacy from a binary question to a conditional one and provide a template for the precision deployment of passive immunotherapy in future pandemics.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the efficacy of convalescent plasma (CP) for COVID-19 caused by SARS-CoV-2, focusing on three interacting determinants: timing of administration, antibody titre, and host characteristics, to reconcile conflicting trial outcomes.
Evidence base and method
A systematic review with design-stratified narrative synthesis (PRISMA 2020; SWiM) was conducted, searching PubMed, Web of Science, and Scopus through May 2026, applying a standardized 91-field extraction matrix to 31 included studies (20,793 patients; 8 RCTs, 23 observational).
Consensus and conclusions
CP is effective only under specific conditions: early administration, sufficient antibody titre, and in seronegative or immunocompromised hosts lacking endogenous antibody responses. Among RCTs, 4 of 6 found benefit with earlier administration, 3 of 4 with higher-titre CP, and two large RCTs (n=12,522) showed preferential benefit in seronegative recipients.
Controversies and gaps
Characterisation of non-IgG immunoglobulins (IgA/IgM) in CP products is nearly absent, with only 2 of 31 studies reporting such data despite evidence of their potent neutralising activity against SARS-CoV-2, representing a systematic gap in product assessment.
Research and practice implications
Future research should prioritise standardised quantification of IgA/IgM titres in CP products and evaluate precision deployment strategies for passive immunotherapy in future pandemics, leveraging timing and titre determinants to optimise efficacy.

The Role of Community Health Workers in Increasing Access to Essential Health and Social Services During the COVID-19 Pandemic.

The Role of Community Health Workers in Increasing Access to Essential Health and Social Services During the COVID-19 Pandemic.
作者： Tabeth T Jiri-Beta, Katie C. Stalker, Mihaly Imre, Ada M. Wilkinson-Lee, Natalie Truesdell, Mary-Ellen Brown, Durrell J Fox, Jennifer Rubio, Flavio F. Marsiglia
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
To describe the health care and social services needs addressed by community health workers (CHWs) through referrals in the Community Health Workers for COVID-19 Response and Resilient Communities (CCR) program.
证据基础与方法
我们对2021年至2024年美国67个CCR受援单位的国家绩效指标数据进行了描述性分析,记录了转诊情况,并对2024年初美国63名项目管理人员和689名社区健康工作者(CHWs)开展了2次网络调查。
共识与结论
社区健康工作者共进行了464 158次社会服务转介和323 769次医疗转介,其中282 596次转介得以完成。社区健康工作者将个体与医疗保健联系起来,并提供随访护理和社会支持。综合协调的医疗和社会服务对优化转介与服务提供至关重要。
争议与缺口
转介成功受益于社区资源、社区健康工作者的知识及网络建设,但受限于成本、不信任、语言障碍、交通及儿童照护挑战。
科研与实践启示
研究结果支持推动社区健康工作者队伍的整合、扩大与可持续发展的政策。

The Role of Community Health Workers in Increasing Access to Essential Health and Social Services During the COVID-19 Pandemic.

Authors: Tabeth T Jiri-Beta, Katie C. Stalker, Mihaly Imre, Ada M. Wilkinson-Lee, Natalie Truesdell, Mary-Ellen Brown, Durrell J Fox, Jennifer Rubio, Flavio F. Marsiglia
Original abstract (English)
To describe the health care and social services needs addressed by community health workers (CHWs) through referrals in the Community Health Workers for COVID-19 Response and Resilient Communities (CCR) program. We performed a descriptive analysis of national performance measure data from 67 CCR recipients from 2021 to 2024 documenting referrals, alongside 2 web-based surveys of 63 program administrators and 689 CHWs in the United States in early 2024. CHWs made 464 158 social and 323 769 health referrals, with 282 596 referrals completed. Referrals included services for food, housing, legal assistance, health insurance, lifestyle interventions, chronic disease management, COVID-19‒related care, and maternal and child health care. Referral success was aided by community resources, CHW knowledge, and networking, and limited by costs, mistrust, language barriers, transportation, and child care challenges. CHWs connect individuals to health care and provide follow-up care and social support. Comprehensive and coordinated health and social services are essential to optimize the referral and service delivery. Public Health Implications. Findings support policies promoting the integration, expansion, and sustainability of the CHW workforce. (Am J Public Health. 2026;116(S4):S331-S340. https://doi.org/10.2105/AJPH.2026.308642).
View review five-element analysis

Evidence base and method

Consensus and conclusions
CHWs made 464 158 social and 323 769 health referrals, with 282 596 referrals completed. CHWs connect individuals to health care and provide follow-up care and social support. Comprehensive and coordinated health and social services are essential to optimize the referral and service delivery.
Controversies and gaps

Research and practice implications

The Impact of Setting Characteristics on Community Health Worker Roles and COVID-19 Activities: An Examination of the Community Health Workers for COVID Response and Resilient Communities Program.

The Impact of Setting Characteristics on Community Health Worker Roles and COVID-19 Activities: An Examination of the Community Health Workers for COVID Response and Resilient Communities Program.
作者： Katie C. Stalker, Mary-Ellen Brown, Noelle Wiggins, Victoria Adewumi, Sahlea Tubbeh, Flavio F. Marsiglia, Floribella Redondo-Martinez, Chidinma Ibe, Ada M. Wilkinson-Lee
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本研究旨在考察参与国家社区健康工作者新型冠状病毒感染应对与韧性社区(CCR)项目的社区健康工作者(CHWs)所执行的角色与活动,并基于设置特征分析差异。
证据基础与方法
We used data from a CHW self-report survey (n = 689) conducted February through March 2024 in the United States. We ran a series of ordered logistic regression models to examine the association between setting characteristics (i.e., type of organizational setting, organizational experience with CHWs, and populations of focus) and CHW core roles and COVID-19‒specific activities.
共识与结论
分析样本中,69.2%的CHWs报告履行了全部10项核心角色。结果表明,CCR项目中的CHWs根据其所服务社区的独特因素调整角色与活动。组织对CHWs的经验及重点关注人群成为CHWs核心角色与活动最显著的预测因子。
争议与缺口
所提供的证据未报告争议或证据缺口。

The Impact of Setting Characteristics on Community Health Worker Roles and COVID-19 Activities: An Examination of the Community Health Workers for COVID Response and Resilient Communities Program.

Authors: Katie C. Stalker, Mary-Ellen Brown, Noelle Wiggins, Victoria Adewumi, Sahlea Tubbeh, Flavio F. Marsiglia, Floribella Redondo-Martinez, Chidinma Ibe, Ada M. Wilkinson-Lee
Original abstract (English)
To examine the roles and activities performed by community health workers (CHWs) involved in the national Community Health Workers for COVID Response and Resilient Communities (CCR) program and examine differences based on setting characteristics. We used data from a CHW self-report survey (n = 689) conducted February through March 2024 in the United States. We ran a series of ordered logistic regression models to examine the association between setting characteristics (i.e., type of organizational setting, organizational experience with CHWs, and populations of focus) and CHW core roles and COVID-19‒specific activities. A total of 69.2% of CHWs in the analytic sample reported performing all 10 core roles. Organizational experience with CHWs and populations of focus emerged as the most salient predictors of CHW core roles and activities. A substantial proportion of CHWs performed the full range of core roles. Results suggest that CHWs in the CCR program tailored their roles and activities according to the unique factors of the communities in which they worked. Public Health Implications. Programs such as CCR may result in CHWs using a wide range of core roles across multiple settings. (Am J Public Health. 2026;116(S4):S295-S304. https://doi.org/10.2105/AJPH.2026.308660).
View review five-element analysis

Evidence base and method
We used data from a CHW self-report survey (n = 689) conducted February through March 2024 in the United States. We ran a series of ordered logistic regression models to examine the association between setting characteristics (i.e., type of organizational setting, organizational experience with CHWs, and populations of focus) and CHW core roles and COVID-19‒specific activities.
Consensus and conclusions
A total of 69.2% of CHWs in the analytic sample reported performing all 10 core roles. Results suggest that CHWs in the CCR program tailored their roles and activities according to the unique factors of the communities in which they worked. Organizational experience with CHWs and populations of focus emerged as the most salient predictors of CHW core roles and activities.
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not report controversies or evidence gaps.
Research and practice implications
Public Health Implications.

Evaluating the Potential Influence of Federal COVID-19 Response Funding on the Building and Sustainability of the CHW Workforce.

Evaluating the Potential Influence of Federal COVID-19 Response Funding on the Building and Sustainability of the CHW Workforce.
作者： Tabeth Tsitsi Jiri-Beta, Terry Greene, Yu Fu Michael Chen, Marianne Mabida, Rachel McCloud, Durrell J Fox, Margaret Frankhouser, Alina Bardwell, Noelle Wiggins
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
To describe the potential influence of Community Health Workers for COVID-19 Response and Resilient Communities (CCR) funding on community health worker (CHW) hiring, retention, and workforce sustainability.
证据基础与方法
本研究通过在美国开展的一项横断面网络调查,对63个CCR受资助机构的主管与协调员进行了描述性分析,调查时间为2024年2月至3月。
共识与结论
Survey questions assessed program funding sources, payment mechanisms for CHW services, and CHW hiring and retention. About 65% of CCR recipients relied solely on CCR funds, whereas others leveraged over $117 million in additional funding, mostly federal, to support CCR program implementation. Of 1359 CHW positions supported by CCR, respondents expected to maintain 749.
争议与缺口
受资助者计划将拨款资金与医疗保险和医疗补助服务中心报销作为留任策略。研究结果强调了通过多元化资金来源进行长期投资以及提升项目能力建设的重要性,以加强并维持CHW队伍。
科研与实践启示
联邦资金可在不同场景下为维持CHW就业做出有意义的贡献,但这一重要公共卫生队伍的可持续性仍未得到保障。公共卫生意义。

Evaluating the Potential Influence of Federal COVID-19 Response Funding on the Building and Sustainability of the CHW Workforce.

Authors: Tabeth Tsitsi Jiri-Beta, Terry Greene, Yu Fu Michael Chen, Marianne Mabida, Rachel McCloud, Durrell J Fox, Margaret Frankhouser, Alina Bardwell, Noelle Wiggins
Original abstract (English)
To describe the potential influence of Community Health Workers for COVID-19 Response and Resilient Communities (CCR) funding on community health worker (CHW) hiring, retention, and workforce sustainability. A descriptive analysis of a cross-sectional, web-based survey of directors and coordinators of 63 CCR recipient organizations administered between February and March 2024 was conducted in the United States. Survey questions assessed program funding sources, payment mechanisms for CHW services, and CHW hiring and retention. About 65% of CCR recipients relied solely on CCR funds, whereas others leveraged over $117 million in additional funding, mostly federal, to support CCR program implementation. Of 1359 CHW positions supported by CCR, respondents expected to maintain 749. Recipients planned to use grant funding and Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services reimbursement as strategies for retention. Federal funding can contribute meaningfully to sustaining CHW employment across different settings, yet sustainability of this vital public health workforce is not assured. Public Health Implications. The findings underscore the importance of long-term investment via diverse funding sources and building programmatic capacity to strengthen and sustain the CHW workforce. (Am J Public Health. 2026;116(S4):S321-S330. https://doi.org/10.2105/AJPH.2026.308646).
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
To describe the potential influence of Community Health Workers for COVID-19 Response and Resilient Communities (CCR) funding on community health worker (CHW) hiring, retention, and workforce sustainability.
Evidence base and method
A descriptive analysis of a cross-sectional, web-based survey of directors and coordinators of 63 CCR recipient organizations administered between February and March 2024 was conducted in the United States.
Consensus and conclusions
Survey questions assessed program funding sources, payment mechanisms for CHW services, and CHW hiring and retention. About 65% of CCR recipients relied solely on CCR funds, whereas others leveraged over $117 million in additional funding, mostly federal, to support CCR program implementation. Of 1359 CHW positions supported by CCR, respondents expected to maintain 749.
Controversies and gaps
Recipients planned to use grant funding and Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services reimbursement as strategies for retention. The findings underscore the importance of long-term investment via diverse funding sources and building programmatic capacity to strengthen and sustain the CHW workforce.
Research and practice implications
Federal funding can contribute meaningfully to sustaining CHW employment across different settings, yet sustainability of this vital public health workforce is not assured. Public Health Implications.
