A Community Health Worker-Led Model of Training and Technical Assistance to Support National Community Health Worker Program Implementation for COVID-19 Response: United States, 2021-2025.

A Community Health Worker-Led Model of Training and Technical Assistance to Support National Community Health Worker Program Implementation for COVID-19 Response: United States, 2021-2025.
作者： Lisa Renee Holderby-Fox, Kira Wortmann, Kelsey N. Witmeier, Theresa Mason, Jennifer Park-Mroch, Catherine G Haywood, Julie Smithwick, Sherri L. Ohly, Erin L Caple, Soledad Bolden
摘要中文翻译为评估美国67个疾病控制与预防中心资助的社区健康工作者(CHW)项目在培训与技术援助(TTA)方面的需求,并评估一个旨在满足所识别需求的全国CHW主导TTA项目的覆盖范围。2021年12月至2022年1月,我们通过在线调查和与项目工作人员的访谈评估TTA需求。我们利用调查结果指导TTA内容与交付方式。2021年至2025年,我们收集参与数据以评估项目覆盖范围。在59名调查受访者及来自10个组织的代表中,资金受助方将招聘、项目实施、资金可持续性及同伴学习列为TTA的优先事项。基于这些发现,我们开发了网络研讨会、实践社区、领导力培训及个性化TTA。该项目覆盖了所有资助受助方,并产生了8824次参与者接触,其中54.7%为CHW。实施CHW项目的卫生部门对CHW主导的TTA表现出了显著需求与参与度。决策者应优先持续投资TTA基础设施,以支持CHW项目的实施与长期可持续性。(Am J Public Health. 2026;116(S4):S314-S320. https://doi.org/10.2105/AJPH.2026.308665)
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review examines the training and technical assistance (TTA) needs of community health worker (CHW) programs in the United States during the COVID-19 response, focusing on the implementation and reach of a national CHW-led TTA program to support these programs from 2021 to 2025.
证据基础与方法
本综述描述了2021年12月至2022年1月期间通过在线调查与访谈评估67个CDC资助的CHW项目TTA需求的方法,并利用调查结果指导TTA内容与交付方式。2021年至2025年期间收集参与数据以评估项目覆盖范围。
共识与结论
本综述得出结论:实施CHW项目的卫生部门对CHW主导的TTA表现出显著需求与参与度,该项目覆盖了所有资助受助方,并产生了8824次参与者接触,其中54.7%为CHW。
争议与缺口
所提供的证据未报告不同地区、人群或项目类型间TTA需求的异质性,亦未涉及与CHW主导TTA有效性相关的冲突发现或未解决机制。
科研与实践启示
本综述暗示决策者应优先持续投资TTA基础设施,以支持CHW项目的实施与长期可持续性,特别是在资金、招聘、项目实施、资金可持续性及同伴学习等方面。

A Community Health Worker-Led Model of Training and Technical Assistance to Support National Community Health Worker Program Implementation for COVID-19 Response: United States, 2021-2025.

Authors: Lisa Renee Holderby-Fox, Kira Wortmann, Kelsey N. Witmeier, Theresa Mason, Jennifer Park-Mroch, Catherine G Haywood, Julie Smithwick, Sherri L. Ohly, Erin L Caple, Soledad Bolden
Original abstract (English)
To assess training and technical assistance (TTA) needs among 67 Centers for Disease Control and Prevention-funded community health worker (CHW) programs in the United States and to assess the reach of a national CHW-led TTA program designed to meet the needs identified. From December 2021 through January 2022, we assessed TTA needs through an online survey and interviews with program staff. We used findings to inform TTA content and delivery. From 2021 through 2025, we collected participation data to assess program reach. Among 59 survey respondents and representatives from 10 organizations, funding recipients identified hiring, program implementation, financial sustainability, and peer learning as priorities for TTA. Based on these findings, we developed webinars, communities of practice, leadership training, and individualized TTA. The program reached all funded recipients and generated 8824 participant encounters, 54.7% of whom were CHWs. Health departments implementing CHW programs demonstrated substantial demand for and engagement in CHW-led TTA. Policymakers should prioritize sustained investment in TTA infrastructure to support implementation and long-term sustainability of CHW programs. (Am J Public Health. 2026;116(S4):S314-S320. https://doi.org/10.2105/AJPH.2026.308665).
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the training and technical assistance (TTA) needs of community health worker (CHW) programs in the United States during the COVID-19 response, focusing on the implementation and reach of a national CHW-led TTA program to support these programs from 2021 to 2025.
Evidence base and method
The review describes an online survey and interviews conducted from December 2021 through January 2022 to assess TTA needs among 67 CDC-funded CHW programs, using findings to inform TTA content and delivery. Participation data were collected from 2021 through 2025 to assess program reach.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that health departments implementing CHW programs demonstrated substantial demand for and engagement in CHW-led TTA, with the program reaching all funded recipients and generating 8824 participant encounters, 54.7% of whom were CHWs.
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not report heterogeneity in TTA needs across regions, populations, or program types, nor does it address conflicting findings or unresolved mechanisms related to CHW-led TTA effectiveness.
Research and practice implications
The review implies that policymakers should prioritize sustained investment in TTA infrastructure to support the implementation and long-term sustainability of CHW programs, particularly in funding, hiring, program implementation, financial sustainability, and peer learning.

细胞因子驱动的高炎症反应在长新冠中的作用:机制、生物标志物、补体失调及新兴免疫治疗——叙述性综述

Cytokine-Driven Hyperinflammation in Long COVID: Mechanisms, Biomarkers, Complement Dysregulation, and Emerging Immunotherapies-A Narrative Review.
作者： Emmanuel Ifeanyi Obeagu
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
长新冠,又称严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染后急性期后遗症(PASC),是一种多系统疾病,其特征是感染急性期后持续存在的症状。
证据基础与方法
本研究通过PubMed、Scopus、Web of Science、Embase及Google Scholar数据库检索2020年至2026年间发表的文献。优先纳入系统性综述、荟萃分析、病例报告、机制研究及临床试验,这些研究关注长新冠中的免疫失调、炎症生物标志物、补体通路及靶向治疗。
共识与结论
持续升高的促炎细胞因子(包括白细胞介素-6、白细胞介素-1β、肿瘤坏死因子-α、干扰素-γ及白细胞介素-17)与替代途径及凝集素途径的补体激活共同导致内皮损伤、微血管血栓形成、神经炎症、纤维化及多系统功能障碍。
争议与缺口
仍需开展大规模前瞻性研究及随机对照临床试验以验证这些策略并优化长期临床结局。
科研与实践启示
新兴生物标志物包括炎症细胞因子、补体蛋白、内皮激活标志物、凝血指标及多组学特征,这些可能有助于疾病分层及治疗监测。整合验证的生物标志物与精准免疫治疗策略可能改善诊断、风险分层及个体化管理。

Cytokine-Driven Hyperinflammation in Long COVID: Mechanisms, Biomarkers, Complement Dysregulation, and Emerging Immunotherapies-A Narrative Review.

Authors: Emmanuel Ifeanyi Obeagu
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Background and Aims Long COVID, also known as post‐acute sequelae of SARS‐CoV‐2 infection (PASC), is a multisystem condition characterized by persistent symptoms that continue beyond the acute phase of infection. Growing evidence indicates that sustained immune dysregulation involving cytokine‐mediated inflammation, complement activation, endothelial dysfunction, thromboinflammation, and viral antigen persistence contributes to its pathogenesis. This narrative review summarizes current evidence on the mechanisms underlying hyperinflammation in Long COVID, highlights emerging biomarkers, and evaluates evolving immunotherapeutic strategies. Methods A narrative literature search was conducted using PubMed, Scopus, Web of Science, Embase, and Google Scholar for studies published between 2020 and 2026. Priority was given to systematic reviews, meta‐analyses, cohort studies, mechanistic investigations, and clinical trials examining immune dysregulation, inflammatory biomarkers, complement pathways, and targeted therapies in long COVID. Results Persistent elevation of pro‐inflammatory cytokines, including interleukin‐6, interleukin‐1β, tumor necrosis factor‐α, interferon‐γ, and interleukin‐17, together with activation of the alternative and lectin complement pathways, contributes to endothelial injury, microvascular thrombosis, neuroinflammation, fibrosis, and multisystem dysfunction. Emerging biomarkers include inflammatory cytokines, complement proteins, endothelial activation markers, coagulation indices, and multi‐omics signatures that may improve disease stratification and therapeutic monitoring. Investigational therapies include cytokine‐targeted biologics, complement inhibitors, Janus kinase inhibitors, mesenchymal stem cell therapy, microbiome‐directed interventions, and precision immunotherapy. Conclusion Long COVID results from complex interactions between persistent inflammation, complement dysregulation, vascular injury, and immune dysfunction. Integrating validated biomarkers with precision immunotherapeutic approaches may improve diagnosis, risk stratification, and individualized management. However, robust prospective studies and randomized clinical trials remain essential to validate these strategies and optimize long‐term clinical outcomes.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Long COVID, also known as post-acute sequelae of SARS-CoV-2 infection (PASC), is a multisystem condition characterized by persistent symptoms that continue beyond the acute phase of infection.
Evidence base and method
A narrative literature search was conducted using PubMed, Scopus, Web of Science, Embase, and Google Scholar for studies published between 2020 and 2026. Priority was given to systematic reviews, meta-analyses, cohort studies, mechanistic investigations, and clinical trials examining immune dysregulation, inflammatory biomarkers, complement pathways, and targeted therapies in long COVID.
Consensus and conclusions
Persistent elevation of pro-inflammatory cytokines, including interleukin-6, interleukin-1β, tumor necrosis factor-α, interferon-γ, and interleukin-17, together with activation of the alternative and lectin complement pathways, contributes to endothelial injury, microvascular thrombosis, neuroinflammation, fibrosis, and multisystem dysfunction.
Controversies and gaps
Robust prospective studies and randomized clinical trials remain essential to validate these strategies and optimize long-term clinical outcomes.
Research and practice implications
Emerging biomarkers include inflammatory cytokines, complement proteins, endothelial activation markers, coagulation indices, and multi-omics signatures that may improve disease stratification and therapeutic monitoring. Integrating validated biomarkers with precision immunotherapeutic approaches may improve diagnosis, risk stratification, and individualized management.

US Public School Operations During the COVID-19 Pandemic: A Scoping Review.

US Public School Operations During the COVID-19 Pandemic: A Scoping Review.
作者： Roberto Henry, Megan E. Collins, Katherine A. Connor, Gronvall GK, Tara Kirk Sell, Gigi Kwik Grönvall
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This scoping review addresses SARS-CoV-2/COVID-19 impacts on US K-12 public school operations during the first two years of the pandemic, focusing on the implementation and effects of interventions and policies used to operate schools while balancing health protection with continuity of education and nonacademic services. [A1][A2][A3][A4][A5].
证据基础与方法
The authors conducted a scoping review of the literature on interventions and policies for operating US K-12 public schools during the first two years of the COVID-19 pandemic, using thematic analysis to characterize the included evidence.
共识与结论
文献支持佩戴口罩、通风、保持身体距离、检测、接触者追踪、隔离与检疫以及疫苗接种干预措施使学校能够安全地开展线下教学,表明适当的减缓措施使公立学校在疫情期间能够安全运行。
争议与缺口
所提供的证据未报告具体的矛盾发现、异质性、方法薄弱、缺失人群、未解决的机制或其他具体证据缺口。
科研与实践启示
The supplied evidence indicates that effective implementation of school mitigation measures during the COVID-19 pandemic required adequate resources, leadership commitment, and community buy-in, and that reopening or continuing remote learning during a public health emergency demands careful planning to safeguard health while ensuring equitable continuation of education and access to nonacademic services.

US Public School Operations During the COVID-19 Pandemic: A Scoping Review.

Authors: Roberto Henry, Megan E. Collins, Katherine A. Connor, Gronvall GK, Tara Kirk Sell, Gigi Kwik Grönvall
Original abstract (English)
The COVID-19 pandemic forced the sudden closure of schools and a transition to remote learning across the United States. The suspension of in-person education not only altered the delivery of education for millions of students, but it effectively separated many students from vital services and functions that supported their ability to learn and develop. Given the unique role that schools play in educating children and young adults, as well as providing important nonacademic services, their closures led to disruptions in learning, added familial responsibilities, and reduced access to critical social services for students. The reopening of schools for in-person learning or the continuation of remote learning during a public health emergency requires careful planning and consideration to not only ensure the health and safety of students and staff, but also ensure equitable continuation of education and access to nonacademic services. We conducted a scoping review to identify and characterize the literature related to the implementation and impact of interventions and policies used to operate K-12 public schools in the United States during the first 2 years of the COVID-19 pandemic. Through thematic analysis, we found that masking, ventilation, physical distancing, testing, contact tracing, quarantine and isolation, and vaccination interventions allowed schools to safely conduct in-person learning during the pandemic. Findings from the literature suggest that there are suitable mitigation measures that allowed for the safe operation of public schools during the COVID-19 pandemic. The effective implementation of these measures required adequate resources, leadership commitment, and community buy-in.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This scoping review addresses SARS-CoV-2/COVID-19 impacts on US K-12 public school operations during the first two years of the pandemic, focusing on the implementation and effects of interventions and policies used to operate schools while balancing health protection with continuity of education and nonacademic services. [A1][A2][A3][A4][A5].
Evidence base and method
The authors conducted a scoping review of the literature on interventions and policies for operating US K-12 public schools during the first two years of the COVID-19 pandemic, using thematic analysis to characterize the included evidence.
Consensus and conclusions
The literature supports that masking, ventilation, physical distancing, testing, contact tracing, quarantine and isolation, and vaccination interventions enabled schools to safely conduct in-person learning, indicating that suitable mitigation measures allowed safe public school operation during the pandemic.
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not report specific conflicting findings, heterogeneity, weak methods, missing populations, unresolved mechanisms or other concrete evidence gaps.
Research and practice implications
The supplied evidence indicates that effective implementation of school mitigation measures during the COVID-19 pandemic required adequate resources, leadership commitment, and community buy-in, and that reopening or continuing remote learning during a public health emergency demands careful planning to safeguard health while ensuring equitable continuation of education and access to nonacademic services.

Extracellular vesicles in COVID-19 and long COVID: Structural mediators of viral persistence and immune dysfunction.

Extracellular vesicles in COVID-19 and long COVID: Structural mediators of viral persistence and immune dysfunction.
作者： Marialaura Fanelli, Vita Petrone, Rossella Chirico, Claudia Matteucci, Antonella Minutolo
摘要中文翻译细胞外囊泡(EVs)已成为长新冠中免疫失调与病毒抗原持续存在的关键结构性介质。在严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染过程中释放的EVs并非被动的生物载体,而是作为高度有序的脂质纳米结构,保持病毒蛋白(包括刺突糖蛋白)的天然构象完整性,从而促进慢性信号传导与全身性炎症。近期在单颗粒分析技术(如冷冻电子断层成像与原子力显微镜)方面的进展,正在克服批量分析的局限性,使EV异质性的可视化与其纳机械特性的定量分析成为可能。本综述整合了当前关于EV相关病毒残余物及宿主来源的炎症性内容物如何传播神经-免疫对话与血管损伤的见解。我们通过评估EVs作为精准生物标志物与生物工程治疗平台的潜力,强调了标准化结构表征的必要性,以将这些纳米囊泡从生理介质转化为临床诊断与再生医学工具。

证据基础与方法
The review synthesizes current insights into EV-associated viral remnants and host-derived inflammatory cargo, emphasizing their structural and functional contributions to neuro-immune crosstalk and systemic inflammation in COVID-19 and long COVID.

Extracellular vesicles in COVID-19 and long COVID: Structural mediators of viral persistence and immune dysfunction.

Authors: Marialaura Fanelli, Vita Petrone, Rossella Chirico, Claudia Matteucci, Antonella Minutolo
Original abstract (English)
Extracellular vesicles (EVs) have emerged as pivotal structural mediators of immune dysregulation and viral antigen persistence in long COVID. Rather than passive biological carriers, EVs released during SARS-CoV-2 infection function as highly organized lipid nanostructures that preserve the native conformational integrity of viral proteins, including the spike glycoprotein, thereby facilitating chronic signaling and systemic inflammation. Recent advancements in single-particle analytical techniques, such as cryo-electron tomography and atomic force microscopy, are now overcoming the limitations of bulk analysis, enabling the visualization of EV heterogeneity and the quantitative profiling of their nanomechanical properties. This review synthesizes current insights into how EV-associated viral remnants and host-derived inflammatory cargo propagate neuro-immune crosstalk and vascular injury. We conclude by evaluating the potential of EVs as precision biomarkers and bioengineered therapeutic platforms, emphasizing the need for standardized structural characterization to transition these nanovesicles from physiological mediators to clinical diagnostic and regenerative tools.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the role of extracellular vesicles (EVs) as structural mediators in SARS-CoV-2 infection and long COVID, focusing on their capacity to preserve viral protein integrity, propagate immune dysregulation, and contribute to chronic inflammation and vascular injury.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes current insights into EV-associated viral remnants and host-derived inflammatory cargo, emphasizing their structural and functional contributions to neuro-immune crosstalk and systemic inflammation in COVID-19 and long COVID.
Consensus and conclusions
Extracellular vesicles released during SARS-CoV-2 infection function as organized lipid nanostructures that preserve the native conformational integrity of viral proteins, including spike glycoprotein, thereby facilitating chronic signaling and systemic inflammation.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights that recent advancements in single-particle analytical techniques, such as cryo-electron tomography and atomic force microscopy, are overcoming prior limitations of bulk analysis, enabling visualization of EV heterogeneity and quantitative profiling of nanomechanical properties.
Research and practice implications
The review evaluates the potential of EVs as precision biomarkers and bioengineered therapeutic platforms, stressing the need for standardized structural characterization to transition these nanovesicles from physiological mediators to clinical diagnostic and regenerative tools.

Refining the role of behavioural and social sciences in pandemic response post-COVID-19.

Refining the role of behavioural and social sciences in pandemic response post-COVID-19.
作者： Maja Grašo, Karl Aquino, Stefan Baral, Nathan Berg, Connie J. Clark, Conway LG 3rd, Paul Dolan, Steven Grover, Kyriaki Fousiani, Claudia Christina Kitz
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review focuses on the role of behavioural and social sciences in pandemic response, specifically examining how these disciplines have studied human behaviour during the COVID-19 pandemic. The central question is about the breadth and depth of this role, particularly in understanding the variability of human responses to public health interventions. The topic boundary includes SARS-CoV-2, its variants, and related terms such as 'post-COVID condition' and 'long COVID'.
证据基础与方法
本综述为现有文献的叙事性综合,未明确提及系统性综述方法或荟萃分析。其依据为疫情期间开展的研究的一般性观察与解读,侧重于理解人类行为的概念框架。综述未报告具体数据库、检索日期或纳入标准。
共识与结论
综述指出,疫情期间将人类行为与公共卫生建议对齐的关注范围相对狭窄。认为该方法可能忽略了人类决策的复杂性,特别是在不确定性与风险认知差异背景下。作者认为,考虑个体间的异质性理性可增进对疫情相关行为的理解。
争议与缺口
综述指出,过度聚焦公共卫生建议的遵从性可能导致对人类行为的过度简化。同时指出在理解人们如何感知与校准疫情风险方面存在研究缺口,尤其是在不确定性与风险分布不均的背景下。这些局限表明未来研究需采用更细致的方法。
科研与实践启示
综述建议扩展行为与社会科学研究范围,纳入更广泛的人类反应与决策过程。强调检视疫情显著性与风险校准的重要性,以提升未来疫情准备、决策及对公共卫生措施信任的能力。

Refining the role of behavioural and social sciences in pandemic response post-COVID-19.

Authors: Maja Grašo, Karl Aquino, Stefan Baral, Nathan Berg, Connie J. Clark, Conway LG 3rd, Paul Dolan, Steven Grover, Kyriaki Fousiani, Claudia Christina Kitz
Original abstract (English)
With the acute stage of COVID-19 behind us, reflection can inform future preparedness. During the pandemic, behavioural and social sciences often studied human behaviour through the lens of alignment with public health recommendations, implicitly treating concern and compliance as desirable and rational. While understandable, this emphasis was relatively narrow. Uncertainty, uneven risk distribution and competing values could have led to heterogeneous appraisals of risk and intervention benefit, even with the evidence available at the time. We show that engaging with the possibility that people could have been heterogeneously rational can broaden understanding of behaviour during COVID-19. Our aim is not to revisit the legitimacy of the response but to encourage reflection on the role of behavioural and social sciences in knowledge generation and highlight opportunities to examine pandemic salience and risk calibration. We outline research directions to guide the field and strengthen decision-making, trust and adaptability in future crises, promoting short-term compliance and long-term trust.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the role of behavioural and social sciences in pandemic response, specifically examining how these disciplines have studied human behaviour during the COVID-19 pandemic. The central question is about the breadth and depth of this role, particularly in understanding the variability of human responses to public health interventions. The topic boundary includes SARS-CoV-2, its variants, and related terms such as 'post-COVID condition' and 'long COVID'.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of existing literature, with no explicit mention of systematic review methods or meta-analysis. It draws on general observations and interpretations of studies conducted during the pandemic, focusing on the conceptual frameworks used to understand human behaviour. The review does not report specific databases, search dates, or eligibility criteria.
Consensus and conclusions
The review highlights that the focus on aligning human behaviour with public health recommendations during the pandemic was relatively narrow. It suggests that this approach may have overlooked the complexity of human decision-making, particularly in the context of uncertainty and varying risk perceptions. The authors argue that considering heterogeneous rationality among individuals can enhance understanding of pandemic-related behaviours.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that the narrow focus on compliance with public health recommendations may have led to an oversimplified view of human behaviour. It also identifies a gap in understanding how people perceive and calibrate risks during pandemics, especially in the context of uncertainty and uneven risk distribution. These limitations suggest a need for more nuanced approaches in future research.
Research and practice implications
The review recommends expanding the scope of behavioural and social science research to include a broader range of human responses and decision-making processes. It emphasizes the importance of examining pandemic salience and risk calibration to improve future preparedness, decision-making, and trust in public health measures.

2023-2025年英国FluSurvey参与者中与疫苗接种季节性流感及严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2相关的因素

Factors associated with vaccination against Seasonal Influenza and SARS-CoV-2 in the UK among FluSurvey participants, 2023-2025.
作者： Lauren Remi Adams, Conall Watson, Rebecca E Green, Gavin Dabrera, Adams, L. R., Green, R. E.
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review aimed to profile vaccination attitudes and examine predictors of COVID-19 and influenza vaccination uptake among a UK participatory surveillance system - FluSurvey. The central question is about factors associated with vaccination against Seasonal Influenza and SARS-CoV-2 in the UK among FluSurvey participants, 2023-2025.
证据基础与方法
The review analyzed FluSurvey data from participants aged [≥]65 years who were eligible for both vaccines in the 2023-2024 and 2024-2025 Autumn - Winter seasonal campaigns. Logistic regression was used to examine factors (age, sex, socioeconomic status, education, employment, transport, smoking and chronic conditions) associated with influenza and COVID-19 vaccination uptake in each season, adjusting for confounders. Descriptive analyses examined self-reported attitudes to influenza vaccination.
共识与结论
Belonging to a risk group and reducing risk of influenza were frequently reported motivations for influenza vaccination, while building natural immunity and concerns around safety and adverse effects were frequently reported barriers. Individuals vaccinated against COVID-19 were more likely to receive an influenza vaccination (aOR2023-2024=13.90 [9.28-21.17]; aOR2024-2025=8.54 [5.82-12.60]), and vice versa (aOR2023-2024=13.91 [9.30-21.19]; aOR2024-2025=8.52 [5.81-12.58]).
争议与缺口
Other results were weaker or demonstrated variation by season. The supplied evidence does not report specific findings on the effectiveness of vaccination programs or the impact of socioeconomic factors on vaccine uptake beyond lower educational attainment being associated with lower odds of COVID-19 vaccination (aOR2023-2024=0.59 [0.45-0.78], aOR2024-2025: 0.56 [0.39-0.79]).
科研与实践启示
Our findings highlight recent attitudes and barriers to influenza and COVID-19 vaccination among the FluSurvey cohort, which may inform approaches to improve vaccination coverage in the population.

Factors associated with vaccination against Seasonal Influenza and SARS-CoV-2 in the UK among FluSurvey participants, 2023-2025.

Authors: Lauren Remi Adams, Conall Watson, Rebecca E Green, Gavin Dabrera, Adams, L. R., Green, R. E.
Original abstract (English)
Seasonal Influenza and COVID-19 vaccination programmes are critical for reducing morbidity and mortality in older adults, yet uptake remains uneven across populations. We aimed to profile vaccination attitudes and examine predictors of COVID-19/influenza vaccination uptake among a UK participatory surveillance system - FluSurvey. We analysed FluSurvey data from participants aged [≥]65 years who were eligible for both vaccines in the 2023-2024 and 2024-2025 Autumn - Winter seasonal campaigns. Descriptive analyses examined self-reported attitudes to influenza vaccination. Logistic regression examined factors (age, sex, socioeconomic status, education, employment, transport, smoking and chronic conditions) associated with influenza and COVID-19 vaccination uptake in each season, adjusting for confounders. Belonging to a risk group and reducing risk of influenza were frequently reported motivations for influenza vaccination, while building natural immunity and concerns around safety and adverse effects were frequently reported barriers. Individuals vaccinated against COVID-19 were more likely to receive an influenza vaccination (aOR2023-2024=13.90 [9.28-21.17]; aOR2024-2025=8.54 [5.82-12.60]), and vice-versa (aOR2023-2024=13.91 [9.30-21.19]; aOR2024-2025=8.52 [5.81-12.58]). Lower educational attainment was associated with lower odds of COVID-19 vaccination (aOR2023-2024=0.59 [0.45-0.78], aOR2024-2025: 0.56 [0.39-0.79]). Other results were weaker or demonstrated variation by season. Our findings highlight recent attitudes and barriers to influenza and COVID-19 vaccination among the FluSurvey cohort, which may inform approaches to improve vaccination coverage in the population.

African Americans' perspective on COVID-19 and influenza vaccines: A qualitative study.

African Americans' perspective on COVID-19 and influenza vaccines: A qualitative study.
作者： Marin Schmitt, Kelly W. Gagnon, Jennifer L. Walsh, Yuri Amirkhanian, Jeffrey A. Kelly, Katherine G. Quinn
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This qualitative study aimed to understand perceptions and factors influencing COVID-19 and influenza vaccination among Black and African American adults to inform interventions and outreach to prevent increased acuity of vaccination disparities.
证据基础与方法
研究在威斯康星州密尔沃基市有意识地招募了86名黑人和非洲裔美国人参与深度访谈。该研究采用定性方法探索疫苗的认知、影响因素及决策过程。
共识与结论
本研究表明,疫苗的认知受个人信念、经历、文化认同及信任等复杂因素交织影响。主要发现:我们识别出两个主题和五个子主题,反映疫苗的认知、影响因素及决策过程。

科研与实践启示

African Americans' perspective on COVID-19 and influenza vaccines: A qualitative study.

Authors: Marin Schmitt, Kelly W. Gagnon, Jennifer L. Walsh, Yuri Amirkhanian, Jeffrey A. Kelly, Katherine G. Quinn
Original abstract (English)
Purpose Annual vaccinations, such as influenza and COVID-19, are a safe and effective way of preventing disease and promoting healthier communities, yet many factors impact uptake. Black and African Americans face numerous barriers to vaccination, reflected in consistently lower vaccination rates and higher hospitalization and mortality rates. This qualitative study aimed to understand perceptions and factors influencing COVID-19 and influenza vaccination among Black and African American adults to inform interventions and outreach to prevent increased acuity of vaccination disparities. Eighty-six Black and African Americans in Milwaukee, WI were purposively recruited to participate in in-depth interviews. Major Findings We identified two themes and five subthemes reflecting vaccine perspectives, influences, and decision-making. In the first theme, similarities and differences in attitudes and willingness to get vaccinated against influenza and COVID-19, we identified three subthemes: (1) prevention was a motivating factor for getting both COVID-19 and influenza vaccines; (2) mistrust surrounding COVID-19 and the COVID-19 vaccine fueled hesitancy; and (3) some participants held discordant beliefs surrounding vaccines and healthcare. In the second theme, sources of vaccine information influencing vaccine perspectives , we identified two subthemes: (1) main sources of vaccine information were television news and the internet, not trusted healthcare professionals, and (2) life experiences affected perspectives. Conclusions Our findings show that vaccine perspectives are shaped by a complex interplay of personal beliefs, experiences, cultural identity, and trust. Public health strategies to improve vaccination rates should acknowledge mistrust, dispel myths and misinformation, and engage trusted communities and messengers across both healthcare and community settings.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This qualitative study aimed to understand perceptions and factors influencing COVID-19 and influenza vaccination among Black and African American adults to inform interventions and outreach to prevent increased acuity of vaccination disparities.
Evidence base and method
Eighty-six Black and African Americans in Milwaukee, WI were purposively recruited to participate in in-depth interviews. The study used qualitative methods to explore vaccine perspectives, influences, and decision-making.
Consensus and conclusions
Our findings show that vaccine perspectives are shaped by a complex interplay of personal beliefs, experiences, cultural identity, and trust. Major Findings We identified two themes and five subthemes reflecting vaccine perspectives, influences, and decision-making.
Controversies and gaps
The study highlights that mistrust surrounding the COVID-19 vaccine fueled hesitancy, and some participants held discordant beliefs surrounding vaccines and healthcare. The study does not report on the effectiveness of interventions to address these issues.
Research and practice implications
Public health strategies to improve vaccination rates should acknowledge mistrust, dispel myths and misinformation, and engage trusted communities and messengers across both healthcare and community settings.

COVID-19 Living Systematic Review Trials

COVID-19 Living Systematic Review Trials
作者： Infectious Diseases Data Observatory
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This review focuses on SARS-CoV-2, COVID-19, and its variants, aiming to explore global registered trials in a frequently updated, open-access, visual database. The central question is to identify ongoing research, spot knowledge gaps, and avoid duplication of efforts in assessing therapeutics, diagnostic tools, vaccines, and other areas related to SARS-CoV-2.
证据基础与方法
The review utilizes a living systematic review approach, developed in collaboration with the COVID-19 Clinical Research Coalition, to track ongoing studies. It is updated quarterly via the WHO International Clinical Trials Registry Platform (ICTRP) and includes metadata such as study design, sample size, trial phase, and active pharmaceutical ingredients (APIs).
共识与结论
本综述强调实时系统性综述在捕捉严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2研究动态变化中的重要性。该综述提供可视化工具,使研究人员能够获取全球试验数据、比较不同研究,并导出数据用于进一步分析,从而确保对治疗干预措施的追踪始终保持最新与全面。
争议与缺口
本综述承认持续开展的研究数量庞大,难以有效整合与评估。指出传统系统性综述为静态快照,可能迅速过时,这凸显了实时综述方法的必要性。然而,本综述未涉及特定干预措施的有效性或安全性争议或证据空白。
科研与实践启示
该综述强调需要创新工具以避免研究工作的不必要重复。同时,它还强调了协作解决方案在加速新型冠状病毒感染预防与诊断研究中的重要性,尤其是在资源受限环境下的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2研究。

COVID-19 Living Systematic Review Trials

Authors: Infectious Diseases Data Observatory
Original abstract (English)
Explore COVID-19 global registered trials in a frequently updated, open-access, visual database based on IDDO�s living systematic review and developed in collaboration with the COVID-19 Clinical Research Coalition.�This tool aims to help researchers identify current ongoing trials, spot knowledge gaps to explore in future studies, and avoid duplication.Since the COVID-19 outbreak was identified in December 2019, a strong response from the research community has resulted in many independent trials assessing therapeutics, diagnostic tools, vaccines and many other areas. The sheer quantity of ongoing research in this field can make it difficult to assimilate and evaluate. Traditional systematic reviews tend to be a snapshot of a situation and can date quite quickly. Uniquely, here IDDO focuses more widely on a whole research landscape using a living systematic review approach to identify knowledge gaps and inform future analyses. The visualisation tool is frequently updated to ensure the constant tracking of studies with a focus on therapeutic interventions. It: Enables users to pull together and access information on global COVID-19 trials;� Details the study design, sample size, trial phase, and the active pharmaceutical ingredients (API) administered in the studies, where the study was conducted, and whether individual patient data (IPD) will be shared; and Is updated via a quarterly search and standardisation of the WHO International Clinical Trials Registry Platform (ICTRP). The tool showcases the metadata available using three different types of visualisations: geography, lists and analytics incorporating descriptive statistical information. Filter and search options pinpoint research according to selected variables and studies of interest. Researchers have the option to compare studies within the tool and data can also be exported for subsequent analysis. As highlighted in our earlier analysis and its�protocol, innovative tools like these are key to preventing unnecessary duplication of independent research efforts. This work is supported by the�COVID-19 Clinical Research Coalition. IDDO is a�founding member of the Coalition which was�formed to build collaborative solutions and accelerate urgent research on prevention and diagnosis�of COVID-19 in resource-limited settings. It provides�free access to COVID-19 clinical trial protocols. A number of Coalition�working groups�are identifying and addressing the most pressing scientific and operational research questions for resource-limited settings.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on SARS-CoV-2, COVID-19, and its variants, aiming to explore global registered trials in a frequently updated, open-access, visual database. The central question is to identify ongoing research, spot knowledge gaps, and avoid duplication of efforts in assessing therapeutics, diagnostic tools, vaccines, and other areas related to SARS-CoV-2.
Evidence base and method
The review utilizes a living systematic review approach, developed in collaboration with the COVID-19 Clinical Research Coalition, to track ongoing studies. It is updated quarterly via the WHO International Clinical Trials Registry Platform (ICTRP) and includes metadata such as study design, sample size, trial phase, and active pharmaceutical ingredients (APIs).
Consensus and conclusions
The review highlights the importance of a living systematic review in capturing the dynamic nature of SARS-CoV-2 research. It provides a visualisation tool that enables researchers to access global trial data, compare studies, and export data for further analysis, ensuring up-to-date and comprehensive tracking of therapeutic interventions.
Controversies and gaps
The review acknowledges the difficulty in assimilating and evaluating the sheer quantity of ongoing research. It notes that traditional systematic reviews are static and can quickly become outdated, which underscores the need for a living review approach. However, it does not address specific controversies or evidence gaps in the efficacy or safety of interventions.
Research and practice implications
The review emphasizes the need for innovative tools to prevent unnecessary duplication of research efforts. It also highlights the importance of collaborative solutions to accelerate urgent research on prevention and diagnosis of SARS-CoV-2 in resource-limited settings.

A Comprehensive Review on Cardioprotective Biological Properties of Crataegus orientalis in Post-COVID-19 Pandemic Cardiovascular Disorders.

A Comprehensive Review on Cardioprotective Biological Properties of Crataegus orientalis in Post-COVID-19 Pandemic Cardiovascular Disorders.
作者： Ayman M Mousa, Hamdoon A Mohammed
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This review focuses on the cardioprotective biological properties of Crataegus orientalis in the context of post-COVID-19 pandemic cardiovascular disorders. The central question is to evaluate the potential of C. orientalis as a therapeutic agent for managing cardiovascular complications following SARS-CoV-2 infection.
证据基础与方法
The review is a comprehensive analysis of the phytochemical constituents and therapeutic properties of Crataegus orientalis. It includes scientific studies from Web of Science, Google Scholar, and PubMed, and the methods are described as systematic, with an emphasis on exploring the cardioprotective mechanisms of C. orientalis in cardiac muscle cell biology post-COVID-19.
共识与结论
综述得出结论:欧李(Crataegus orientalis)展现出多种心脏保护特性,包括抗氧化、抗炎、降血脂、抗高血糖、降血压、抗凝血、抗缺血、利尿和强心作用,这些特性均由大量科学研究所支持。这些特性被视为新型冠状病毒感染疫情后心血管健康的潜在益处。
争议与缺口
该综述指出,对C. orientalis的活性成分仍需进一步研究,以充分了解其机制及在预防和治疗大流行后心血管疾病中的疗效。目前缺乏关于其特定分子组分及其与严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2相关心血管并发症相互作用的详细信息。
科研与实践启示
The review suggests that future research should prioritize the identification and characterization of the bioactive molecular components of C. orientalis to develop new approaches for preventing and treating post-COVID-19 cardiovascular diseases. It also recommends exploring its potential as a functional food dietary supplement to enhance plant-derived cardioprotective formulations.

A Comprehensive Review on Cardioprotective Biological Properties of Crataegus orientalis in Post-COVID-19 Pandemic Cardiovascular Disorders.

Authors: Ayman M Mousa, Hamdoon A Mohammed
Original abstract (English)
The growing use of herbal medicinal plants like hawthorn is recognized globally as a way to meet the demand for new natural pharmaceuticals with health benefits. The current review provides a comprehensive overview of the phytochemical constituents and heart-protective therapeutic properties of Crataegus orientalis. Despite numerous reviews on the biological properties of the Crataegus genus. orientalis has emerged as a promising therapeutic agent in managing the increasing cardiovascular complications that have followed the COVID-19 pandemic. This review highlights the primary cardioprotective biological effects of C. orientalis, including antioxidant, anti-inflammatory, hypolipidemic, antihyperglycemic, antihypertensive, anticoagulant, anti-ischemic, diuretic, and cardiotonic properties, supported by numerous scientific studies in Web of Science, Google Scholar, and PubMed. This updated review provides an overview of the bioactive molecular components of C. orientalis and their cardioprotective mechanisms in cardiac muscle cell biology following the post-COVID-19 Pandemic, exploring its potential as an innovative functional food dietary supplement to enhance plant-derived cardioprotective formulations and its possible health benefits. This study presents a significant challenge for analyzing the active constituents of C. orientalis, which require further research, as they are expected to be promising candidates for developing new approaches to prevent and treat various post-pandemic cardiovascular diseases (CVD).

Scope and question
This review focuses on the cardioprotective biological properties of Crataegus orientalis in the context of post-COVID-19 pandemic cardiovascular disorders. The central question is to evaluate the potential of C. orientalis as a therapeutic agent for managing cardiovascular complications following SARS-CoV-2 infection.
Evidence base and method
The review is a comprehensive analysis of the phytochemical constituents and therapeutic properties of Crataegus orientalis. It includes scientific studies from Web of Science, Google Scholar, and PubMed, and the methods are described as systematic, with an emphasis on exploring the cardioprotective mechanisms of C. orientalis in cardiac muscle cell biology post-COVID-19.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that Crataegus orientalis exhibits multiple cardioprotective properties, including antioxidant, anti-inflammatory, hypolipidemic, antihyperglycemic, antihypertensive, anticoagulant, anti-ischemic, diuretic, and cardiotonic effects, supported by numerous scientific studies. These properties are highlighted as potential benefits for post-pandemic cardiovascular health.
Controversies and gaps
The review acknowledges that the active constituents of C. orientalis require further research to fully understand their mechanisms and efficacy in preventing and treating post-pandemic cardiovascular diseases. There is a lack of detailed information on the specific molecular components and their interactions with SARS-CoV-2-related cardiovascular complications.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that future research should prioritize the identification and characterization of the bioactive molecular components of C. orientalis to develop new approaches for preventing and treating post-COVID-19 cardiovascular diseases. It also recommends exploring its potential as a functional food dietary supplement to enhance plant-derived cardioprotective formulations.

猫感染严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2后的高血糖:胰腺改变及潜在抗病毒药物治疗策略

Hyperglycemia in Cats Infected by SARS-CoV-2: Pancreatic Alterations and Potential Antiviral Therapeutics
作者： Takashi Onodera, Sungwook Seo, Akikazu Sakudo, Antonio Toniolo
摘要中文翻译猫是一种易感宿主,并可能作为研究冠状病毒发病机制及其治疗策略的转化模型。近期在人类和猫组织中的免疫组织化学(IHC)及组织病理学研究显示,在经典时间感染进程后,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2核衣壳蛋白(NP)及刺突蛋白在胰腺岛细胞中表达。值得注意的是,IHC分析还在外分泌导管上皮细胞中检测到NP表达。由于导管上皮在组织损伤或代谢应激期间可作为胰岛祖细胞库,嗜胰腺冠状病毒可能通过利用这种内在细胞分化途径进入内分泌区室。尽管尚未阐明调控胰岛内病毒进入的精确机制,但本综述强调了严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2损害胰腺外分泌(消化)和内分泌功能的能力。最后,我们评估了直接作用型抗病毒药物——特别是RNA依赖性RNA聚合酶(RdRp)抑制剂和蛋白酶抑制剂——作为单药治疗及协同联合疗法,以限制胰腺损伤并减轻冠状病毒感染急性期与后急性期并发症(如人类长新冠综合征)的糖尿病发生风险。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述聚焦于严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2对猫胰腺的影响,探讨其发病机制及与胰腺改变和糖尿病发生风险相关的潜在抗病毒药物治疗策略。
证据基础与方法
本综述综合了近期人类和猫组织中的免疫组织化学(IHC)及组织病理学研究,强调了严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2核衣壳蛋白(NP)及刺突蛋白在胰腺岛细胞和外分泌导管上皮细胞中的表达。这些发现基于在两种物种中观察到的经典时间感染进程。
共识与结论
综述得出结论:严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2可损害胰腺的外分泌和内分泌功能,并有证据表明病毒蛋白在岛细胞和导管上皮细胞中表达。同时强调了直接作用型抗病毒药物(如RNA依赖性RNA聚合酶抑制剂和蛋白酶抑制剂)在减轻胰腺损伤和糖尿病发生风险方面的潜力。
争议与缺口
胰岛内病毒进入的精确机制仍不清楚,表明在理解上存在缺口。此外,综述指出,尽管已评估抗病毒药物治疗策略,但其在缓解长期胰腺影响方面的有效性尚未完全确立。
科研与实践启示
综述建议优先研究严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2诱导胰腺损伤的机制,并开发针对急性期与后急性期并发症(如长新冠综合征)的抗病毒药物治疗策略,在猫模型中进行验证。

Hyperglycemia in Cats Infected by SARS-CoV-2: Pancreatic Alterations and Potential Antiviral Therapeutics

Authors: Takashi Onodera, Sungwook Seo, Akikazu Sakudo, Antonio Toniolo
Original abstract (English)
Cats represent a susceptible host and a possible translational model for investigating coronavirus pathogenesis and therapeutics. Recent immunohistochemical (IHC) and histopathological studies in both human and feline tissues have demonstrated SARS-CoV-2 nucleocapsid protein (NP) and spike protein expression within pancreatic islet cells, following a classic temporal infection course. Notably, IHC analysis also reveals NP expression within exocrine ductal epithelial cells. Given that ductal epithelium functions as an islet progenitor pool during tissue injury or metabolic stress, pancreotropic coronaviruses may gain access to the endocrine compartment by exploiting this intrinsic cellular differentiation pathway. Although the precise mechanisms governing intra-islet viral entry remain to be elucidated, this review highlights the capacity of SARS-CoV-2 to compromise both the exocrine (digestive) and endocrine functions of the pancreas. Finally, we evaluate the therapeutic potential of direct-acting antivirals—specifically RNA-dependent RNA polymerase (RdRp) and protease inhibitors—as monotherapies and in synergistic combination to limit pancreatic injury and mitigate the diabetogenic effects of coronaviruses across both acute infection and post-acute sequelae, such as human long-COVID syndrome.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on SARS-CoV-2 and its impact on the pancreas in cats, exploring the pathogenesis and potential antiviral therapeutics related to pancreatic alterations and diabetogenic effects.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes recent immunohistochemical (IHC) and histopathological studies in human and feline tissues, highlighting SARS-CoV-2 nucleocapsid protein (NP) and spike protein expression within pancreatic islet cells and exocrine ductal epithelial cells. These findings are based on a classic temporal infection course observed in both species.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that SARS-CoV-2 can compromise both the exocrine and endocrine functions of the pancreas, with evidence of viral protein expression in islet cells and ductal epithelial cells. It also emphasizes the potential of direct-acting antivirals, such as RNA-dependent RNA polymerase (RdRp) and protease inhibitors, to mitigate pancreatic injury and diabetogenic effects.
Controversies and gaps
The precise mechanisms of intra-islet viral entry remain unclear, indicating a gap in understanding. Additionally, the review notes that while antiviral therapeutics are evaluated, their efficacy in mitigating long-term pancreatic effects is not fully established.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests prioritizing research into the mechanisms of SARS-CoV-2-induced pancreatic injury and the development of antiviral therapies targeting both acute and post-acute sequelae, such as long-COVID syndrome, in feline models.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 ORF8:一个位于免疫逃逸与炎症之间的辅助蛋白

SARS-CoV-2 ORF8: an accessory protein at the interface of immune evasion and inflammation
作者： Carolina Schäfer, Cristopher Blamey, Rocío Balbiano, Javier Mena, Claudio Acuña-Castillo, Ana María Sandino
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 ORF8是一种快速进化的辅助蛋白,通过细胞内和细胞外机制调节宿主免疫。
证据基础与方法
在细胞内,ORF8通过降低细胞表面MHC-I分子的表达来破坏抗原呈递,并与内质网重塑及应激反应改变相关。
共识与结论
在细胞外,分泌型ORF8作为一种病毒因子,可在髓样细胞和树突状细胞中放大炎症程序,可能对急性期严重程度及组织特异性病理产生影响。ORF8还被报道通过影响IRF3依赖性通路来拮抗I型干扰素的诱导。临床上,循环ORF8与疾病严重程度相关,且在某些长新冠患者中,病毒RNA检测转阴后仍可持续检测到ORF8。
争议与缺口
然而,这种持续检测是否反映了ORF8的直接致病作用,还是标志着持续的病毒负荷与免疫激活,目前尚无定论。
科研与实践启示
In this review, we summarize recent findings on the pleiotropic effects of ORF8 and outline key priorities to clarify when ORF8 acts primarily as an immune-evasion factor, an inflammatory amplifier, or both. Collectively, the evidence reviewed here identifies ORF8 as a promising candidate for further mechanistic studies, whose biomarker potential and therapeutic relevance warrant rigorous evaluation in acute and post-acute COVID-19.

SARS-CoV-2 ORF8: an accessory protein at the interface of immune evasion and inflammation

Authors: Carolina Schäfer, Cristopher Blamey, Rocío Balbiano, Javier Mena, Claudio Acuña-Castillo, Ana María Sandino
Original abstract (English)
SARS-CoV-2 ORF8 is a rapidly evolving accessory protein that modulates host immunity through both intracellular and extracellular mechanisms. Intracellularly, ORF8 disrupts antigen presentation by reducing cell-surface MHC-I and is linked to endoplasmic reticulum remodeling and altered stress responses. Extracellularly, secreted ORF8 behaves as a virokine that can amplify inflammatory programs in myeloid and dendritic cells, with potential implications for acute severity and tissue-specific pathology. ORF8 is also reported to antagonize type I interferon induction through effects on IRF3-dependent pathways. Clinically, circulating ORF8 has been associated with disease severity, and persistent detection of ORF8 after viral RNA becomes undetectable has been reported in some individuals with post-acute symptoms. However, whether this persistence reflects a direct pathogenic role or instead marks ongoing viral burden and immune activation remains unresolved. In this review, we summarize recent findings on the pleiotropic effects of ORF8 and outline key priorities to clarify when ORF8 acts primarily as an immune-evasion factor, an inflammatory amplifier, or both. Collectively, the evidence reviewed here identifies ORF8 as a promising candidate for further mechanistic studies, whose biomarker potential and therapeutic relevance warrant rigorous evaluation in acute and post-acute COVID-19.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
SARS-CoV-2 ORF8 is a rapidly evolving accessory protein that modulates host immunity through both intracellular and extracellular mechanisms.
Evidence base and method
Intracellularly, ORF8 disrupts antigen presentation by reducing cell-surface MHC-I and is linked to endoplasmic reticulum remodeling and altered stress responses.
Consensus and conclusions
Extracellularly, secreted ORF8 behaves as a virokine that can amplify inflammatory programs in myeloid and dendritic cells, with potential implications for acute severity and tissue-specific pathology. ORF8 is also reported to antagonize type I interferon induction through effects on IRF3-dependent pathways. Clinically, circulating ORF8 has been associated with disease severity, and persistent detection of ORF8 after viral RNA becomes undetectable has been reported in some individuals with post-acute symptoms.
Controversies and gaps
However, whether this persistence reflects a direct pathogenic role or instead marks ongoing viral burden and immune activation remains unresolved.
Research and practice implications
In this review, we summarize recent findings on the pleiotropic effects of ORF8 and outline key priorities to clarify when ORF8 acts primarily as an immune-evasion factor, an inflammatory amplifier, or both. Collectively, the evidence reviewed here identifies ORF8 as a promising candidate for further mechanistic studies, whose biomarker potential and therapeutic relevance warrant rigorous evaluation in acute and post-acute COVID-19.

Impact of COVID-19 on neglected tropical diseases in Zambia: a qualitative study of stakeholder perspectives.

Impact of COVID-19 on neglected tropical diseases in Zambia: a qualitative study of stakeholder perspectives.
作者： Caitlin Butala, Jenna Fyfe, Noreen Machila, Susan Christina Welburn
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The study aimed to explore the impact of COVID-19 on neglected tropical diseases (NTDs) in Zambia, focusing on how the pandemic affected NTD programmes, funding, and partnerships from the perspective of national-level stakeholders.
证据基础与方法
本研究采用定性案例研究设计,对参与赞比亚NTD政策、项目或监测的知情人士进行了半结构化访谈。受访者来自政府部委、赞比亚国家公共卫生研究所、国际组织和学术界,共14人。访谈为面对面进行,录音后转录为逐字稿,并进行匿名处理。数据通过归纳主题分析进行分析,代码从转录稿中开发,并通过持续比较方法逐步归类为主题。
共识与结论
受访者表示,疫情暴露并加剧了公共卫生系统能力、常规NTD 监测和资金方面的既有薄弱环节,导致大规模药物治疗(MDA)和其他干预措施的延迟和覆盖率下降。疫情也与赞比亚国家公共卫生研究所的强化以及更新的NTD和One Health战略计划的制定相吻合,这些变化被利益相关者视为构建更加强韧的NTD应对措施的机会。
争议与缺口
本研究发现疫情对NTD项目的影响存在异质性,一些利益相关者指出疫情并未导致项目完全崩溃,而是放大了长期存在的结构性脆弱性。目前缺乏关于疫情对NTD控制工作具体影响的定量数据,研究强调需要更可预测的国内资金以及战略计划的实施。
科研与实践启示
利益相关者建议,加强常规NTD数据系统、确保更可预测的国内资金、实施One Health和NTD主计划,以及建立更加坚韧、本地主导的合作伙伴关系,将是恢复损失进展并保护NTD控制工作免受未来冲击的关键。

Impact of COVID-19 on neglected tropical diseases in Zambia: a qualitative study of stakeholder perspectives.

Authors: Caitlin Butala, Jenna Fyfe, Noreen Machila, Susan Christina Welburn
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Neglected tropical diseases (NTDs) remain a major public health challenge in Zambia and other low‐ and middle‐income countries, and there is concern that the COVID‐19 pandemic has further disrupted progress towards control and elimination targets. This study explored how COVID‐19 affected NTD programmes, funding, and partnerships in Zambia from the perspective of national‐level stakeholders. METHODS: We conducted a qualitative case study using semi‐structured interviews with key informants involved in NTD policy, programming, or surveillance in Zambia. Fourteen participants were purposively sampled from government ministries, the Zambia National Public Health Institute, international organisations, and academia. Interviews were conducted in person, audio‐recorded, transcribed verbatim, and anonymised. Data were analysed using inductive thematic analysis, with codes developed from the transcripts and iteratively grouped into themes through constant comparison. RESULTS: Five overarching themes were identified: (1) public health infrastructure; (2) disease prioritisation and surveillance; (3) resource allocation and funding challenges; (4) collaboration and partnerships; and (5) direct impacts of COVID‐19 on NTD activities. Participants reported that COVID‐19 exposed and intensified pre‐existing weaknesses in health system capacity, routine NTD surveillance, and financing, contributing to delays and reduced coverage of mass drug administration (MDA) and other interventions. Donor funding reallocation and the withdrawal or scaling back of some international partners created further uncertainty for NTD programmes, although various national institutions and the World Health Organization (WHO) helped sustain essential activities. At the same time, the pandemic period coincided with the strengthening of the Zambia National Public Health Institute and the development of updated NTD and One Health strategic plans. Stakeholders viewed these institutional changes as opportunities to build a more resilient NTD response. CONCLUSIONS: COVID‐19 disrupted NTD programmes in Zambia primarily by amplifying long‐standing structural vulnerabilities in funding, surveillance, and service delivery, rather than through complete programme collapse. Strengthening routine NTD data systems, securing more predictable domestic financing, operationalising One Health and NTD master plans, and building more resilient, locally owned partnerships will be essential to recover lost ground and protect NTD control efforts from future shocks.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study aimed to explore the impact of COVID-19 on neglected tropical diseases (NTDs) in Zambia, focusing on how the pandemic affected NTD programmes, funding, and partnerships from the perspective of national-level stakeholders.
Evidence base and method
The study used a qualitative case study design, conducting semi-structured interviews with key informants involved in NTD policy, programming, or surveillance in Zambia. Fourteen participants were purposively sampled from government ministries, the Zambia National Public Health Institute, international organisations, and academia. Data were analysed using inductive thematic analysis, with codes developed from the transcripts and iteratively grouped into themes through constant comparison.
Consensus and conclusions
Participants reported that the pandemic exposed and intensified pre-existing weaknesses in health system capacity, routine NTD surveillance, and financing, leading to delays and reduced coverage of mass drug administration (MDA) and other interventions. The pandemic also coincided with the strengthening of the Zambia National Public Health Institute and the development of updated NTD and One Health strategic plans, which stakeholders viewed as opportunities to build a more resilient NTD response.
Controversies and gaps
The study identified heterogeneity in the impact of the pandemic on NTD programmes, with some stakeholders noting that the pandemic did not cause complete programme collapse but rather amplified long-standing structural vulnerabilities. There is a lack of quantitative data on the specific effects of the pandemic on NTD control efforts, and the study highlights the need for more predictable domestic financing and operationalising of strategic plans.
Research and practice implications
Stakeholders suggested that strengthening routine NTD data systems, securing more predictable domestic financing, operationalising One Health and NTD master plans, and building more resilient, locally owned partnerships will be essential to recover lost ground and protect NTD control efforts from future shocks.

Rising food prices and widening nutrition gaps in Mexico: The cost of nutrient-dense and ultra-processed foods before, during, and after COVID-19.

Rising food prices and widening nutrition gaps in Mexico: The cost of nutrient-dense and ultra-processed foods before, during, and after COVID-19.
作者： Alfonso Mendoza-Velázquez, Lara-Arevalo J, Leonardo Mendoza-Martínez, Adam Drewnowski, J. Lara-Arévalo
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本研究旨在考察墨西哥营养密集型食品、食品价格与通胀之间的关系,重点关注这些因素如何在疫情前、中、后期影响健康饮食的可负担性。
证据基础与方法
研究采用面板数据模型(含食品层面固定效应)分析价格与通胀趋势。食品按NOVA分类系统归类,并与营养成分数据及2012—2025年全国食品价格序列进行关联。
共识与结论
最小加工食品的营养密集度(NRF9.3:202.2)远高于加工食品(15.7)和超加工食品(UPFs;5.4),但每100千卡价格也显著更高。在整个研究期间,营养密集度更高的食品每卡路里价格也持续更高。
争议与缺口
研究观察到,疫情期间最小加工食品通胀率相较疫情前上升1.64个百分点(p=0.064),疫情后期上升6.04个百分点(p<0.001)。加工食品与烹饪原料在疫情期间分别额外通胀1.99个百分点和1.21个百分点,而疫情后期各加工类别间无显著差异。
科研与实践启示
为在经济波动条件下支持健康饮食,制定提升营养密集型食品可负担性与价格稳定性的政策可能至关重要。

Rising food prices and widening nutrition gaps in Mexico: The cost of nutrient-dense and ultra-processed foods before, during, and after COVID-19.

Authors: Alfonso Mendoza-Velázquez, Lara-Arevalo J, Leonardo Mendoza-Martínez, Adam Drewnowski, J. Lara-Arévalo
Original abstract (English)
The COVID-19 pandemic disrupted global food systems, contributing to rising food prices and altering the affordability of healthy diets. This study examined the relationship between nutrient density of foods, food prices and inflation in Mexico before, during, and after the pandemic. Foods from the ENSANUT dietary survey were assigned to 4 categories using the NOVA classification system and were linked to nutrient composition data and national food price series from 2012 to 2025. The Nutrient Rich Food Index (NRF9.3) was a measure of nutrient density. Affordability was defined as nutrient density relative to price per 100 kcal. Trends in prices and inflation were analyzed using a panel data model with food-level fixed effects. Minimally processed foods had substantially higher nutrient density (NRF9.3: 202.2) than processed (15.7) and ultra-processed foods (UPFs; 5.4), but were also markedly more expensive per 100 kcal. Across the study period, higher nutrient density was consistently associated with higher prices per calorie. During the pandemic, inflation for minimally processed foods increased by 1.64 percentage points (p.p.; p = 0.064) relative to pre-pandemic levels, and by 6.04 p.p. in the post-pandemic period (p < 0.001). Processed foods and culinary ingredients experienced additional inflation of 1.99 p.p. and 1.21 p.p., respectively, during the pandemic, while no significant differences across processing categories were observed post-pandemic. Following the onset of the pandemic, food prices increased across all processing categories. Because minimally processed foods had higher baseline prices, similar inflation rates translated into a widening affordability gap between more and less nutritious foods. These findings suggest that foods aligned with dietary recommendations became relatively less affordable during a period of economic disruption, potentially incentivizing substitution toward lower-cost, energy-dense UPFs. Policies that improve the affordability and price stability of nutrient-dense foods may be critical to support healthy diets under conditions of economic volatility.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study aimed to examine the relationship between nutrient density of foods, food prices and inflation in Mexico before, during, and after the pandemic, focusing on how these factors influenced the affordability of healthy diets.
Evidence base and method
The study used a panel data model with food-level fixed effects to analyze trends in prices and inflation. Foods were categorized using the NOVA classification system and linked to nutrient composition data and national food price series from 2012 to 2025.
Consensus and conclusions
Minimally processed foods had substantially higher nutrient density (NRF9.3: 202.2) than processed (15.7) and ultra-processed foods (UPFs; 5.4), but were also more expensive per 100 kcal. Higher nutrient density was consistently associated with higher prices per calorie across the study period.
Controversies and gaps
The study observed that during the pandemic, inflation for minimally processed foods increased by 1.64 percentage points (p = 0.064) relative to pre-pandemic levels, and by 6.04 p.p. in the post-pandemic period (p < 0.001). Processed foods and culinary ingredients experienced additional inflation of 1.99 p.p. and 1.21 p.p., respectively, during the pandemic, while no significant differences across processing categories were observed post-pandemic.
Research and practice implications
Policies that improve the affordability and price stability of nutrient-dense foods may be critical to support healthy diets under conditions of economic volatility.

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

COVID-19 dashboard

COVID-19 dashboard
新闻简报The WHO COVID-19 dashboard provides a global summary of confirmed SARS-CoV-2 cases and deaths, with data collected from countries, territories, and areas. It includes information on case definitions, testing strategies, and reporting practices, which vary by region. The dashboard has been updated since March 2020, with weekly data from July 2023 onwards. Data is subject to continuous verification and may change due to retrospective updates or shifts in reporting standards. WHO acknowledges that case detection, testing, and reporting practices differ globally, which can lead to under or overestimation of true case and death counts. Additionally, some countries have stopped reporting or changed their reporting frequency, which may affect the completeness of the data. The dashboard serves as a tool for monitoring the pandemic's spread and impact, but it is important to note that the data reflects official reports and may not capture all cases or deaths accurately.
查看新闻五要素
时间
从2019年12月31日至2020年3月21日,以及自2020年3月22日起,从2023年7月起每周更新(N14、N15、N21)。
地点与对象
全球,数据收集自国家、地区和地区,包括高收入、低收入、中低收入和中上收入群体(N1、N16)。
事件
WHO monitors and reports on the global epidemiological situation of SARS-CoV-2 through the WHO COVID-19 dashboard, including confirmed cases and deaths (N1, N4, N10).
规模、影响与风险
该仪表板包括向世卫组织报告的确诊病例和死亡病例,数据需要经过验证,并且由于回顾性更新或报告做法的变化而可能发生变化(N1、N8、N18)。
应对、状态与不确定性
世卫组织收集和传播其会员国报告的数据,可能存在重大数据错误和更正,并承认各地区在病例发现、检测策略和报告做法方面存在差异(N13、N9、N18)。
A high priorityPublic-health news

COVID-19 dashboard

News Brief
The WHO COVID-19 dashboard provides a global summary of confirmed SARS-CoV-2 cases and deaths, with data collected from countries, territories, and areas. It includes information on case definitions, testing strategies, and reporting practices, which vary by region. The dashboard has been updated since March 2020, with weekly data from July 2023 onwards. Data is subject to continuous verification and may change due to retrospective updates or shifts in reporting standards. WHO acknowledges that case detection, testing, and reporting practices differ globally, which can lead to under or overestimation of true case and death counts. Additionally, some countries have stopped reporting or changed their reporting frequency, which may affect the completeness of the data. The dashboard serves as a tool for monitoring the pandemic's spread and impact, but it is important to note that the data reflects official reports and may not capture all cases or deaths accurately.

epidemiological situation via the WHO COVID-19 dashboard. If data for certain countries is unavailable in this section, it may indicate that they have either ceased reporting COVID-19 surveillance data to WHO or have integrated the COVID-19 surveillance into existing respiratory disease surveillance. In the latter case, SARS-CoV-2 detections from sentinel and systematic virological surveillance sites in those countries may be found in the Circulation section which also includes information on SARS-CoV-2 variant circulation. This global summary of COVID-19 cases includes data on confirmed cases reported to WHO from the comprehensive COVID-19 case monitoring. Coronavirus (COVID-19) cases, %%COUNTRY%% data Source: World Health Organization Loading... Loading... Loading... What is the definition of 'confirmed' and 'reported' in COVID-19 data? All data represents the date of reporting as opposed to date of symptom onset. All data is subject to continuous verification and may change based on retrospective updates to accurately reflect trends, changes in country case definitions and/or reporting practices. Significant data errors detected or reported to WHO may be corrected at more frequent intervals with some countries performing retrospective bulk corrections may lead to the appearance of significant spikes or negative values which are verified and validated by WHO. In the context of WHO's statistical reporting of COVID-19 data, it is important to note that only confirmed cases are included in case and death counts. In guidance updated on 22 July 2023; there are two alternative definitions for 'Confirmed case of SARS-CoV-2 infection' in international surveillance reporting, although some departures may exist due to local adaptations: a) A person with a positive Nucleic Acid Amplification Test (NAAT), regardless of clinical criteria OR epidemiological criteria. b) A person meeting clinical criteria AND/OR epidemiological criteria (suspect case A) with a positive professional-use or self-test SARS-CoV-2 Antigen-RDT. Additionally, WHO only disseminates data as reported by its Member States. From the 31 December 2019 to the 21 March 2020, WHO collected the numbers of confirmed COVID-19 cases and deaths through official communications under the International Health Regulations (IHR, 2005), complemented by monitoring the official ministries of health websites and social media accounts. Since 22 March 2020, global data has been compiled through WHO region-specific dashboards, and/or aggregate count data reported directly to WHO headquarters by Member States. Statistical counts include both domestic and repatriated cases. Case detection, definitions, testing strategies, reporting practice, and lag times (e.g. time to case notification, and time to reporting of deaths) differ between countries, territories and areas. These factors, amongst others, influence the counts presented with variable under or overestimation of true case and death counts, and variable delays to reflecting these statistics at a global level. Please note that statistics do not necessarily reflect the actual number of cases and deaths or the actual number of countries where cases and deaths are occurring, as a number of countries have stopped reporting or changed their frequency of reporting. Trends in COVID-19 cases, %%COUNTRY%% Recent COVID-19 cases reported to WHO (weekly) %%COUNTRY%%, July 2023 - present Source: World Health Organization Data may be incomplete for the latest week. Total COVID-19 cases reported to WHO (weekly) %%COUNTRY%%, January 2020 - present Source: World Health Organization Why is COVID-19 data being presented as weekly statistics? A number of countries have stopped reporting or changed their frequency of reporting COVID-19 case and death counts to WHO. An outcome of these differences in reporting is that WHO may receive daily data from some countries, while other countries may only report data to WHO once every 14 days. In addition, countries differ in how they choose to report statistics; some countries provide their data attributed to specific dates while others who report less frequently may group data from 7 days into a single statistic attributed to a week in their reporting. As of 25 August 2023, WHO declared that it is no longer necessary for Member States to report daily counts of cases and deaths to WHO and requested strengthening of weekly reporting. Reported data is still available attributed to specific dates (daily data) here as a download. This dashboard presents the same statistics as weekly figures in charts in order to mitigate against the visual misinterpretation of data. Should daily data have been presented here, many countries would show zero counts for multiple consecutive days due in part to the differences in how they choose to report. While weekly intervals do not completely mitigate against this, the approach reduces the risk that some dashboard users might infer zero cases or deaths when lack of data is often due to reporting differences. What do negative counts of cases or deaths mean? All data is subject to continuous verification and may change based on retrospective updates to accurately reflect trends, changes in country case definitions and/or reporting practices. Significant data errors detected or reported to WHO may be corrected by Member States at more frequent intervals with some countries performing retrospective bulk corrections in their reporting. Bulk corrections may lead to the appearance of significant spikes or negative values which are verified and validated by WHO. Updated 22 July 2023 Last 7 days ‘Last 7 days’ refers to the most recent complete epidemiological week (commonly referred to as an 'epi week'). Last 28 days ‘Last 28 days’ refers to the four preceding most recently complete epidemiological weeks. WHO Regions The World Health Organization (WHO) is divided into six regions with regional offices, each responsible for addressing health challenges and promoting public health within its designated geographical area. These regions serve as administrative units that allow the WHO to tailor its efforts to the specific needs, priorities, and health conditions of member countries within each region. World Bank Income Groups The World Bank classifies economies into income groups based on their Gross National Income (GNI) per capita. These annually updated classifications help the World Bank and other organizations analyze and understand global economic trends, allocate resources, and design development policies. Data sources COVID-19 case data From the 31 December 2019 to the 21 March 2020, WHO collected the numbers of confirmed COVID-19 cases and deaths through official communications under the International Health Regulations (IHR, 2005), complemented by monitoring the official ministries of health websites and social media accounts. Since 22 March 2020, global data is compiled through WHO region-specific dashboards, and/or aggregate count data reported to WHO headquarters. WHO COVID-19 Dashboard is updated every Tuesday for the period of two full weeks prior. Counts primarily reflect laboratory-confirmed cases and deaths, based upon WHO case definitions; although some departures may exist due to local adaptations. Counts include both domestic and repatriated cases. Case detection, definitions, testing strategies, reporting practice, and lag times (e.g. time to case notification, and time to reporting of deaths) differ between countries, territories and areas. These factors, amongst others, influence the counts presented with variable under or overestimation of true case and death counts, and variable delays to reflecting these data at a global level. All data represent date of reporting as opposed to date of symptom onset. All data are subject to continuous verification and may change based on retrospective updates to accurately reflect trends, changes in country case definitions and/or reporting practices. Significant data errors detected or reported to WHO may be corrected at more frequent intervals. Data have been included retrospectively since 31 July 2023. Rates <0.001 per 100,000 population may be rounded to 0. Global, with data collected from countries, territories, and areas, including high, low, lower middle, and upper middle income groups (N1, N16).

WHO monitors and reports on the global epidemiological situation of SARS-CoV-2 through the WHO COVID-19 dashboard, including confirmed cases and deaths (N1, N4, N10).

The dashboard includes confirmed cases and deaths reported to WHO, with data subject to verification and potential changes due to retrospective updates or changes in reporting practices (N1, N8, N18).

WHO collects and disseminates data as reported by its Member States, with potential for significant data errors and corrections, and acknowledges differences in case detection, testing strategies, and reporting practices across regions (N13, N9, N18).

呼吸道病毒活动水平 - 美国疾病控制与预防中心 | CDC (.gov)

Respiratory Virus Activity Levels - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)
新闻简报美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)于2026年8月28日发布了一份报告,详细介绍了呼吸道病毒活动水平及相关公共卫生更新。该报告包含与大肠杆菌和沙门氏菌相关的暴发的信息,以及埃博拉和环孢子虫病的最新情况。然而,该报告并未重点关注严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2,也未提供任何特定暴发的具体病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。相反,它作为公共卫生主题的总体概述,强调了CDC在监测和应对各类健康威胁方面的作用。该报告未详细说明任何提及的暴发的具体应对措施或控制措施,表明其目的在于提供更广泛的信息概述,而非针对特定严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2活动的专项分析。

时间
跳转至网站内容 跳转至搜索 西班牙语 官方网站使用 .gov .gov网站属于美国官方政府组织
地点与对象
以下是您了解的方式 西班牙语 官方网站使用 .gov .gov网站属于美国官方政府组织
事件
CDC发布了一份关于呼吸道病毒活动水平的报告,其中包含与大肠杆菌、沙门氏菌等多种病原体相关的暴发的信息,以及埃博拉和环孢子虫病的最新情况。该报告是一份公共卫生主题的总体概述,并未重点关注某一特定暴发或病毒。
规模、影响与风险
该报告未提供任何特定暴发的具体病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。报告提及了公共卫生风险的总体情况及更新,但未对其进行量化。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC提供了多种暴发的总体信息和更新,但该报告未详细说明任何特定事件的具体应对措施、调查或控制措施。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Respiratory Virus Activity Levels - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)

News Brief
The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) published a report on August 28, 2026, detailing respiratory virus activity levels and related public health updates. The report includes information on outbreaks linked to E. coli and Salmonella, as well as updates on Ebola and Cyclosporiasis. However, it does not focus on SARS-CoV-2 or provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers for any particular outbreak. Instead, it serves as a general overview of public health topics, highlighting the CDC's role in monitoring and responding to various health threats. The report does not detail specific responses or containment measures for any of the mentioned outbreaks, indicating a broader informational purpose rather than a targeted analysis of SARS-CoV-2 activity.

IVAC Researchers Inform Global Guidance on COVID-19 Vaccines - Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health

IVAC Researchers Inform Global Guidance on COVID-19 Vaccines - Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health
新闻简报Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health's International Vaccine Access Center (IVAC) researchers contributed to the World Health Organization's (WHO) first-ever position paper on COVID-19 vaccines, released in 2026. The paper summarizes essential background information on the disease and vaccine performance, offering data-backed recommendations for national immunization programs. The research, funded by the Coalition for Epidemic Preparedness Innovations (CEPI), evaluated over 5,000 studies assessing vaccine effectiveness across diverse populations, including older adults, immunocompromised individuals, children, and pregnant individuals. The findings indicate that the vaccine remains highly effective at preventing hospitalizations and deaths, with no identified group where it does not work. The paper emphasizes routine vaccination for high-risk populations and suggests that countries may consider it for others based on context-specific factors such as cost-effectiveness. The recommendations are supported by systematic reviews of global evidence and are available in an interactive database for public health decision-makers.
